  $\ker(T^{\dagger}T)$ and lies in the closure of its range; each element of that range is the limit of images of compactly
  supported smooth functions.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Letwin: ordinary diffusion coercivity and inverse Young inequality (Lemma~A.6 supports)]
  \label{lem:letwin-finite-energy-supports}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.compact_ordinary_diffusion_coercivity,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.inverse_quadratic_young}
  \leanok
  Letwin \cite{Letwin2026QuadraticKLS}, Lemma~A.6 (Brascamp--Lieb for $\nu$), two supporting steps of the finite-energy
  argument.  (i) For $\mu=m.\mathrm{tilted}(-U)$ on a finite-dimensional real inner product space, with $U\in C^2$, $m$ an
  additive Haar measure and $u$ smooth with compact support, $\nabla^2U(\nabla u,\nabla u)$ and $(\mathcal Lu)^2$ are
  $\mu$-integrable and $\int\nabla^2U(\nabla u,\nabla u)\,d\mu\le\int(\mathcal Lu)^2\,d\mu$, for the ordinary weighted
  Laplacian $\mathcal Lu=\Delta u-\langle\nabla U,\nabla u\rangle$ (not the inverse-Hessian operator; no lower Hessian
  bound).  (ii) For a positive definite real matrix $H$ over any finite index type, the empty one included,
  $2\,v\cdot w\le v\cdot H^{-1}v+w\cdot Hw$.  Not provided: Brascamp--Lieb itself, Lemma~A.6, the moment map and
  Theorem~2.5.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  (i) Pointwise $\Gamma_2(u)=\|\nabla\nabla u\|^2+\nabla^2U(\nabla u,\nabla u)\ge\nabla^2U(\nabla u,\nabla u)$, the Hessian
  square is integrable by compact support, and $\int\Gamma_2(u)\,d\mu=\int(\mathcal Lu)^2\,d\mu$.  (ii) Expand the
  nonnegative quadratic form $(w-H^{-1}v)\cdot H(w-H^{-1}v)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Letwin: inverse-Hessian energy of a gradient]
  \label{lem:letwin-inverse-hessian-energy}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.hessian_inverse_young,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.inverse_hessian_energy_eq_coordinate_sum,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.continuous_inverse_hessian_gradient_energy,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.inverse_hessian_gradient_energy_nonneg}
  % Gradient pairing uses Mathlib's inner_gradient_left.
  \leanok
  Letwin \cite{Letwin2026QuadraticKLS}, Lemma~A.6, the energy $\varphi^{ab}\partial_af\,\partial_bf$.  For a $C^2$ potential
  $U$ whose Hessian operator $H(x)$ is positive definite at $x$: $2\langle v,w\rangle\le\langle v,H(x)^{-1}v\rangle+D^2U(x)(w,w)$
  (no uniform curvature constant), and $\langle\nabla f(x),H(x)^{-1}\nabla f(x)\rangle\ge0$.  For a smooth $U$ with
  everywhere positive definite Hessian: in any orthonormal basis the energy equals
  $\sum_{i,j}(H^{-1})_{ij}\,\partial_if\,\partial_jf$, and for $f\in C^1$ it is continuous.  Every dimension, zero included.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Positivity of the Hessian form at $w-H^{-1}v$ and self-adjointness; coordinates of the gradient in the basis; continuity
  of the smooth inverse entries.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Letwin: scalar Brascamp--Lieb for a given potential (Lemma~A.6, given-potential form)]
  \label{thm:letwin-scalar-brascamp-lieb}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.scalar_brascamp_lieb_finite_energy,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.scalar_brascamp_lieb_extended_energy,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.scalar_brascamp_lieb_extended_energy_of_bounded,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.compact_variational_estimate}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:letwin-centered-range, lem:letwin-finite-energy-supports, lem:letwin-inverse-hessian-energy}
  Letwin \cite{Letwin2026QuadraticKLS}, Lemma~A.6, for a given potential.  Let $\nu=m.\mathrm{tilted}(-U)$ be a log-concave
  probability law on a finite-dimensional real inner product space (any dimension, zero included), $m$ an additive Haar
  measure, with the Hessian of $U$ positive definite at every point (no uniform lower bound), and let $f\in C^1\cap L^2(\nu)$,
  not necessarily compactly supported.  (Finite-$C^2$ scope) If $U\in C^2$ and the energy
  $\langle\nabla f,H^{-1}\nabla f\rangle$ is $\nu$-integrable, then $\mathrm{Var}_\nu f\le\int\langle\nabla f,H^{-1}\nabla
  f\rangle\,d\nu$.  (Extended-smooth scope) If $U\in C^\infty$, then with no energy premise
  $\mathrm{Var}_\nu f\le\int\langle\nabla f,H^{-1}\nabla f\rangle\,d\nu$ in $[0,\infty]$; for bounded $f\in C^1$ the $L^2$
  premise is derived.  Equality holds for the standard Gaussian law on $\mathbb R$ and $f=\mathrm{id}$.  Not
  provided: the source moment map, its Euler--Lagrange identification, push-forward and regularity, hence Lemma~A.6 for the
  source law, Theorem~2.5, Theorem~1.2, BKL and KLS.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  On the compact smooth diffusion images $w=-\mathcal Lu$, Young, compact integration by parts and ordinary-diffusion
  coercivity give $2\langle\bar f,w\rangle-\|w\|^2\le\int\langle\nabla f,H^{-1}\nabla f\rangle$ for the centered class
  $\bar f$; these images are dense in the centered classes, so the bound holds at $w=\bar f$, i.e. $\mathrm{Var}f\le$ energy.
  The extended form splits on finiteness of the lower integral; finiteness gives integrability before any real conversion.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Letwin: trace evolution of the Hessian (identity (2.8), Theorem~2.5 support)]
  \label{lem:letwin-hessian-trace}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.stdHessianMatrix,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.stdHessianMatrixDeriv,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.stdHessianMatrixGenerator,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.hessianInverseOperator_trace_stdHessianMatrix_sq}
  \leanok
  Letwin \cite{Letwin2026QuadraticKLS}, proof of Theorem~2.5, the pointwise identity (2.8).  For a smooth $\varphi$ on a
  finite-dimensional real inner product space, any $V$, the inverse-Hessian operator
  $L=\varphi^{ij}\partial_{ij}-(\partial_iV\circ\nabla\varphi)\partial_i$, the Hessian matrix $H$ of $\varphi$ in the standard
  orthonormal basis and every constant real matrix $B$, at every point
  $L\,\mathrm{tr}(BHBH)=2\,\mathrm{tr}(BHB\,LH)+2\sum_{a,b}\varphi^{ab}\,\mathrm{tr}(B\,\partial_aH\,B\,\partial_bH)$, with
  $LH$ taken entrywise.  No positive semidefiniteness, commutation or Monge--Amp\`ere premise.  Not provided: the
  substitution of Lemma~2.4, (2.10), the tensor comparison and the trace bound of Theorem~2.5.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The product rule $L(fg)=fLg+gLf+2\varphi^{ij}\partial_if\,\partial_jg$, additivity and homogeneity of $L$, and
  linearity of the trace in the entries of $BH$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Trace pairings of positive semidefinite matrices]
  \label{lem:letwin-trace-gram-positivity}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.sum_entry_mul_nonneg,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.trace_mul_nonneg,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.traceGram_posSemidef}
  \leanok
  Matrix support for Letwin \cite{Letwin2026QuadraticKLS}, (2.10).  For real positive semidefinite $A,C$ over any finite
  index type (the empty one included), $\sum_{ij}A_{ij}C_{ij}\ge0$ and $\mathrm{tr}(AC)\ge0$ (the product itself is not claimed
  positive semidefinite).  For positive semidefinite $B$ and symmetric $T_a$, the matrix
  $(\mathrm{tr}(B\,T_a\,B\,T_b))_{a,b}$ is positive semidefinite.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The Frobenius pairing is the quadratic form of $A\otimes C$ at the diagonal vector; the trace-weighted Gram matrix is a
  weighted Gram matrix after reindexing.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Letwin: (2.9), (2.10) and the pointwise generator condition for a given potential]
  \label{lem:letwin-ma-trace-210}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.stdHessianConjugate,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.stdThirdGram,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.stdHessianMatrixGenerator_eq_on_range,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.traceGenerator_210_on_range,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.weightedDerivativeTrace_nonneg,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.hessianConjugateTrace_nonneg_on_range,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.thirdGramTrace_nonneg,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.traceGeneratorCondition_on_range}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:letwin-hessian-trace, lem:letwin-trace-gram-positivity}
  Letwin \cite{Letwin2026QuadraticKLS}, proof of Theorem~2.5, (2.9)--(2.10), for a given potential.  Let $\varphi$ be smooth
  with positive definite Hessian at every point, $V$ smooth only on an open $U\supseteq\overline K$, $\mathrm{range}\,
  \nabla\varphi\subseteq K$, and $\log\det\nabla^2\varphi=-\varphi+V(\nabla\varphi)$ everywhere.  Then (2.9)
  $LH=-H+H\,\nabla^2V(\nabla\varphi)\,H+G$ with the inverse-Hessian third-derivative Gram matrix $G$, and (2.10) holds for every
  constant real $B$ (every finite dimension, zero included).  If moreover $K$ is open, $V$ convex on $K$ and $B$ positive
  semidefinite, the three remainders $\sum\varphi^{ab}\mathrm{tr}(B\partial_aHB\partial_bH)$,
  $\mathrm{tr}(BHB\cdot H\nabla^2V(\nabla\varphi)H)$ and $\mathrm{tr}(BHB\cdot G)$ are nonnegative, so
  $L\,\mathrm{tr}(BHBH)+2\,\mathrm{tr}(BHBH)\ge0$ pointwise.  An explicit nonconstant interval potential is an
  example.  Not provided: the integration of this condition (Lemmas~A.4--A.5 for this trace function), the moment map of an
  arbitrary source law, the source trace bound and tensor comparison, Theorem~2.5.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  (2.9) is the second derivative of the Monge--Amp\`ere relation (Lemma~2.4 on the range); substituting it into the trace
  identity of \texttt{lem:letwin-hessian-trace} gives (2.10).  The remainders are traces of products of positive semidefinite
  matrices and a pairing of the inverse Hessian with a positive semidefinite trace-weighted Gram matrix.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Letwin: scalar cutoff profile (Lemma~A.4, real-variable part)]
  \label{lem:letwin-cutoff-profile}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.smoothTransition_two_sub_cutoff,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_majorant_abs_deriv_deriv_smoothTransition_two_sub,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.monotone_and_tendsto_smoothTransition_two_sub_div_succ,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.inv_mul_intervalIntegral_div_three_cutoff,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.deriv_comp_sub_div_and_deriv_deriv,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.monotoneOn_and_tendsto_smoothTransition_two_sub_div}
  \leanok
  Letwin \cite{Letwin2026QuadraticKLS}, Lemma~A.4 (p.~17), the real-variable part of the cutoff construction.  The
  profile $\eta(t)=\mathrm{smoothTransition}(2-t)$ is $C^\infty$, antitone, with values in $[0,1]$, equal to $1$ for
  $t\le1$ and to $0$ for $t\ge2$; $\eta'$ and $\eta''$ vanish off $[1,2]$, are compactly supported and share a finite
  bound $B$.  There is a $C^\infty$, compactly supported, nonnegative $w$, vanishing for $t\le0$ and for $t\ge3$, with
  $|\eta''|\le w$.  For every real $a$, $n\mapsto\eta(a/(n+1))$ is monotone and tends to $1$, and so is the real-parameter
  family $R\mapsto\eta(a/R)$: monotone on $(0,\infty)$ with limit $1$ as $R\to\infty$.  Tail primitive: for every
  $C^\infty$ nonnegative $w$ vanishing for $t\le0$ and $t\ge3$ and every $R>0$, $q_R(t)=R^{-1}\int_{t/R}^{3}w$ is
  $C^\infty$ with $q_R'(t)=-R^{-2}w(t/R)$, $0\le q_R\le R^{-1}\int_0^3w$ and $q_R(t)=0$ for $t\ge3R$.  Affine rescaling:
  for arbitrary $f$, $c$, $R$, $t$, $\big(f((\cdot-c)/R)\big)'(t)=R^{-1}f'((t-c)/R)$ and
  $\big(f((\cdot-c)/R)\big)''(t)=R^{-2}f''((t-c)/R)$ (total-derivative conventions, $R=0$ included).  These facts are used for the
  cutoffs $\chi_R=\eta((\varphi-\min\varphi+1)/R)$ of Lemma~A.4 with $\|L\chi_R\|_{L^1(\nu)}=O(1/R)$, then Lemma~A.5,
  Theorem~2.5 and Theorem~1.2, the input of Lemma~4.2 of \cite{BizeulKlartagLehec2026}.  Lemma~A.4 itself (the minimum of
  $\varphi$, the cutoffs $\chi_R$, their compact support and generator-defect bound) is Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-a4-cutoffs}.
  Not provided: the moment map, Theorems~2.5 and~1.2.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The smooth transition is locally constant off $[1,2]$, so $\eta'$ and $\eta''$ vanish there and, being continuous with
  compact support, are bounded; $w$ is $B$ times a product of two smooth transitions, equal to $B$ on $[1,2]$.
  Exhaustion: for $a<0$ every argument $a/(n+1)$ is negative, so every term $\eta(a/(n+1))$ already equals $1$;
  for $a\ge0$ the arguments $a/(n+1)$ decrease in $n$ and $\eta$ is antitone, which gives monotonicity; and
  $a/(n+1)\to0$ with $\eta=1$ on $t\le1$ gives the limit $1$; the real parameter $R>0$ is handled by the same argument
  with $a/R$.  Primitive: the fundamental theorem of calculus for the
  continuous $w$ composed with $t\mapsto t/R$, smoothness from the smooth derivative, nonnegativity of $w$ on $[t/R,3]$, and
  $w=0$ off $(0,3)$.  Rescaling: the chain rule for the affine map $s\mapsto(s-c)/R$, applied twice.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Letwin: nonnegative inverse-Hessian quadratic form (Lemma~A.4 support)]
  \label{lem:letwin-inverse-hessian-quadratic}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.sum_inverseHessianEntry_mul_mul_nonneg}
  \leanok
  Letwin \cite{Letwin2026QuadraticKLS}, Lemma~A.4 (p.~17), the sign of $\varphi^{ij}\partial_iW\partial_jW$ in
  $L\chi_R=R^{-1}\eta'(W/R)\,LW+R^{-2}\eta''(W/R)\,\varphi^{ij}\partial_iW\partial_jW$: for a $C^2$ potential $\varphi$ whose
  Hessian at $x$ is positive definite, every orthonormal basis (of any rank $m$) and all coefficients $a$,
  $\sum_{i,j}\big((\nabla^2\varphi(x))^{-1}\big)_{ij}a_ia_j\ge0$.  It is used for the pointwise generator-defect
  domination of the Lemma~A.4 cutoffs.  Lemma~A.4 and Lemma~A.5 for given cutoffs are Lemmas~\ref{lem:letwin-a4-cutoffs}
  and~\ref{lem:letwin-a5-cutoffs}.  Not provided: the moment map, Theorems~2.5 and~1.2.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The inverse of the Hessian matrix in the basis is the matrix of inverse-Hessian entries and is positive semidefinite;
  the quadratic form is the dot product $a\cdot(A^{-1}a)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Letwin: supports of the cutoff construction (Lemma~A.4)]
  \label{lem:letwin-a4-supports}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_forall_le_and_isCompact_sublevel_of_convexOn_integrable_exp_neg,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.hessianInverseOperator_self,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.bounded_hessianInverseOperator_self,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.hessianInverseOperator_comp,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.sum_inverseHessianEntry_mul_fderiv_comp,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.abs_hessianInverseOperator_comp_sub_div_le,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.continuous_hessianInverseOperator,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.integral_hessianInverseOperator_self_mul_comp_eq_neg,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.integrable_and_neg_integral_deriv_comp_mul_le}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:letwin-inverse-hessian-ibp, lem:letwin-inverse-hessian-quadratic}
  Letwin \cite{Letwin2026QuadraticKLS}, Lemma~A.4 (p.~17), the steps of the proof for a given potential.  Coercivity: a
  convex $\varphi$ with $e^{-\varphi}$ integrable for an additive Haar measure attains its minimum and all its sublevel sets
  are compact (no coercivity premise; dimension zero included).  Self-generator: where the Hessian of a $C^2$ potential is
  positive definite, $L\varphi=\dim E-\langle\nabla V(\nabla\varphi),\nabla\varphi\rangle$, and $L\varphi$ is uniformly
  bounded when $\nabla\varphi$ takes values in a bounded $K$ and $V$ is $C^1$ on an open set containing $\overline K$.
  Composition: $L(h\circ W)=h'(W)\,LW+h''(W)\sum_{ij}A_{ij}\partial_iW\,\partial_jW$ for $C^2$ $W$ and $h$; the coefficient
  extraction $\sum_{ij}A_{ij}\partial_i\varphi\,\partial_j(h\circ\varphi)=h'(\varphi)\sum_{ij}A_{ij}\partial_i\varphi\,
  \partial_j\varphi$; the pointwise domination $|L(e((\varphi-c)/R))|\le|R^{-1}e'((\varphi-c)/R)|\,|L\varphi|
  +R^{-2}|e''((\varphi-c)/R)|\,|\sum_{ij}A_{ij}\partial_i\varphi\,\partial_j\varphi|$; continuity of $Lf$ for $C^2$ $f$.
  Energy: for smooth $h$ with $h\circ\varphi$ compactly supported, $\int(L\varphi)\,h(\varphi)\,d\nu=-\int h'(\varphi)
  \sum_{ij}A_{ij}\partial_i\varphi\,\partial_j\varphi\,d\nu$, and with $|L\varphi|\le D$, $|h|\le M$ the energy integrand
  is integrable with $-\int h'(\varphi)\sum_{ij}A_{ij}\partial_i\varphi\,\partial_j\varphi\,d\nu\le DM$.  No minimum,
  cutoff, energy inequality or global integrability is a premise.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  A convex set of finite Haar volume containing a ball is bounded, so the sublevels are compact and the minimum over
  $\{\varphi\le\varphi(0)\}$ is global.  The self-generator identity is the trace of $A\nabla^2\varphi$; its bound uses the
  compact images of $DV$ and $\nabla\varphi$.  Composition and domination are the chain rule with the affine rescaling;
  the energy identity is (2.2) with $f=\varphi$, $g=h\circ\varphi$, and the bound integrates it.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Letwin: cutoffs with small generator (Lemma~A.4)]
  \label{lem:letwin-a4-cutoffs}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.exists_scaled_cutoffs_hessianInverseOperator,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.exists_cutoffs_hessianInverseOperator}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:letwin-cutoff-profile, lem:letwin-a4-supports}
  Letwin \cite{Letwin2026QuadraticKLS}, Lemma~A.4 (p.~17), for every potential in the source class: $\varphi$ smooth and
  convex on a finite-dimensional real inner product space $E$ with $\nabla\varphi(E)\subseteq K$, $K$ bounded, $V$ smooth
  on an open $U\supseteq\overline K$, $\log\det\nabla^2\varphi=-\varphi+V\circ\nabla\varphi$, positive definite Hessian
  and $\int e^{-\varphi}\,dm=1$ for an additive Haar measure $m$; $\nu=e^{-\varphi}m$.  There are $\chi_R$ for real
  $R\ge1$ and one $C\ge0$ such that each $\chi_R$ is $C^\infty$, compactly supported and $[0,1]$-valued, $R\mapsto
  \chi_R(x)$ is monotone on $[1,\infty)$ with limit $1$, $L\chi_R\in L^1(\nu)$ and $\int|L\chi_R|\,d\nu\le C/R$; explicitly
  $\chi_R=\eta((\varphi-\min\varphi+1)/R)$.  The natural index $\chi_n=\chi_{n+1}$ gives cutoffs monotone in $n$ with
  $\int|L\chi_n|\,d\nu\le C/(n+1)\to0$.  The source-class properties are hypotheses: they are the properties the paper
  uses for a smooth moment map of a regular law. That full smoothness conclusion is not proved; the weak $C^{1,1}$ construction for the source law and its $C^1$ cutoffs are developed separately below.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The minimum and compact sublevels come from Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-a4-supports}, so $\chi_R$ is compactly supported;
  the composition rule, the bounded self-generator and the profile bounds dominate $|L\chi_R|$ by
  $R^{-1}B\,D+R^{-2}w(W/R)\,\Gamma(W)$ with $W=\varphi-\min\varphi+1$; the energy estimate with the tail primitive $q_R$
  bounds the second term by $R^{-1}D\int_0^3w$; monotonicity and the limit come from the real-parameter exhaustion.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Letwin: mean zero of $Lf$ for given cutoffs (Lemma~A.5)]
  \label{lem:letwin-a5-cutoffs}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.integrable_and_integral_eq_zero_of_cutoff_defect,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.integrable_and_integral_hessianInverseOperator_eq_zero_of_cutoffs}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:letwin-inverse-hessian-ibp}
  Letwin \cite{Letwin2026QuadraticKLS}, Lemma~A.5 (p.~18), with the cutoffs as a premise: for a given potential as in
  (2.2), smooth $f$ with $|f|\le C$ and $Lf+cf\ge0$ for some $c>0$, and smooth compactly supported $\chi_n$ with
  $0\le\chi_n\le1$, $\chi_n\to1$, $L\chi_n\in L^1(\nu)$ and $\|L\chi_n\|_{L^1(\nu)}\to0$: $Lf\in L^1(\nu)$ and
  $\int Lf\,d\nu=0$.  Generic core: for a probability law, bounded $f$, $g+cf\ge0$ with $c>0$, cutoffs $0\le\chi_n\le1$,
  $\chi_n\to1$ and defects $d_n$ with $\int|d_n|\to0$ and $\int\chi_ng=\int f\,d_n$, the function $g$ is integrable with
  $\int g=0$.  For a potential in the source class of Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-a4-cutoffs} its natural-index cutoffs satisfy
  the premises; that composition is carried out for the uniform-interval law
  (Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-uniform-interval-client}), not as a separate general statement.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The integrated symmetry (2.2) against $\chi_n$ gives $\int\chi_n\,Lf\,d\nu=\int f\,L\chi_n\,d\nu$; Fatou's lemma gives
  integrability of $Lf+cf$, and dominated convergence gives $\int Lf\,d\nu=0$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Letwin: the uniform-interval moment potential]
  \label{lem:letwin-uniform-interval-source}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.uniformInterval_momentPotential_gradient,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.uniformInterval_momentPotential_contDiff_convexOn,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.uniformInterval_momentPotential_bilinearIteratedFDerivTwo,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.uniformInterval_momentPotential_logDetHessian,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.uniformInterval_momentPotential_integral_exp_neg,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.uniformInterval_momentPotential_source_hypotheses,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.uniformInterval_tanhSq_nonconstant_facts}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:letwin-inverse-hessian-derivative}
  The uniform law on $K=(-\sqrt3,\sqrt3)$ (mean $0$, variance $1$) with $V\equiv\log(2\sqrt3)$: the explicit potential
  $\varphi(y)=2\log\cosh(\sqrt3y/2)+\log(4/\sqrt3)$ on $\mathbb R$ is smooth and convex, with
  $\varphi'(y)=\sqrt3\tanh(\sqrt3y/2)\in K$, $\varphi''(y)=\tfrac32\operatorname{sech}^2(\sqrt3y/2)>0$,
  $\log\varphi''=-\varphi+\log(2\sqrt3)$ and $\int e^{-\varphi}=1$: the source class of Lemmas~A.4 and~A.5.  For the
  nonconstant $f(y)=\tanh^2(\sqrt3y/2)$: $f$ is smooth, $|f|\le1$, $f(0)\ne f(1)$, $Lf=f''/\varphi''=1-3\tanh^2(\sqrt3y/2)$
  and $Lf+2f\ge0$.  Only the range inclusion $\varphi'(\mathbb R)\subseteq K$ is proved, not that $\varphi'$ pushes
  $e^{-\varphi}dy$ forward to the uniform law.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Direct calculus with $\cosh$ and $\tanh$; the normalization integrates $\tfrac{\sqrt3}{4}\operatorname{sech}^2$ with
  the antiderivative $\tfrac12\tanh(\sqrt3y/2)$ on both half-lines; with constant $V$ the drift vanishes and the rank-one
  Hessian gives $Lg=g''/\varphi''$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Letwin: Lemma~A.5 for the uniform-interval potential]
  \label{lem:letwin-uniform-interval-client}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.uniformInterval_tanhSq_integrable_and_integral_hessianInverseOperator_eq_zero}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:letwin-uniform-interval-source, lem:letwin-a4-cutoffs, lem:letwin-a5-cutoffs}
  For $\varphi$, $V$ and $f$ as in Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-uniform-interval-source} and $\nu=e^{-\varphi}dy$:
  $Lf\in L^1(\nu)$ and $\int Lf\,d\nu=0$.  The proof applies the source package, the natural-index cutoffs of
  Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-a4-cutoffs} with $K=(-\sqrt3,\sqrt3)$, $U=\mathbb R$, and Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-a5-cutoffs} with
  $C=1$, $c=2$.  No integral-zero statement for $f$ is assumed or proved separately.  This is a concrete source-law
  example, not Letwin's Theorem~2.5 or~1.2.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-a4-cutoffs} applied to the source package supplies the cutoffs, and
  Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-a5-cutoffs} with $|f|\le1$ and $Lf+2f\ge0$ gives the conclusion.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Third cumulant under the Letwin bound]
  \label{lem:bkl-third-cumulant-conditional}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.LetwinQuadraticBound,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.fderiv_logLaplace_zero_apply,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.taylorTensor_two_inner_eq_of_centered,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.cumulant_three_eq_integral,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.sum_sq_cumulant_three_le_of_letwin}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:bkl-linear-cumulant}
  Lemma~4.2 of \cite{BizeulKlartagLehec2026}, conditionally on the Letwin quadratic-form bound, which
  stays an explicit hypothesis.  For a centered isotropic probability measure with all exponential
  moments, $\kappa_3(u,v,w)=\int\langle u,x\rangle\langle v,x\rangle\langle w,x\rangle\,d\mu$, and if
  $\mathrm{Var}_\mu\langle Mx,x\rangle\le8\norm M_{HS}^2$ for every self-adjoint $M$ (the Letwin bound, the
form taken by the variance estimate for isotropic $\mu$), then
  $\sum_{j,k}\kappa_3(u,e_j,e_k)^2\le 8|u|^2$, i.e.\ $R_3\le8\,\mathrm{Id}$.  The unconditional Lemma~4.2 is
  not proved.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The cumulant formula for linear functions and centering, then Cauchy--Schwarz between
  $\langle u,x\rangle$ and the centered quadratic form of $M=\sum_{j,k}\kappa_3(u,e_j,e_k)\,e_j\otimes e_k$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Third cumulant from one exponential radius]
  \label{lem:bkl-local-third-cumulant}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.cumulant_three_eq_integral_of_integrable_exp,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.memLp_of_polynomial_bound_of_integrable_exp,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.sum_sq_cumulant_three_le_of_letwin_of_integrable_exp,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.sum_sq_cumulant_three_le_of_letwin_of_integrable_exp_basis,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.compact_support_integrable_exp,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.sum_sq_cumulant_three_le_of_letwin,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.Suspension.cumulantBound_two_of_letwin}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:bkl-third-cumulant-conditional, lem:bkl-borell-uniform-moments}
  Lemma~4.2 of \cite{BizeulKlartagLehec2026} in its source scope, conditionally on the Letwin
  quadratic-form bound.  Log-concavity guarantees some positive exponential radius (Borell) but not, in general,
  every exponential moment; the Laplace law is isotropic and log-concave but has no exponential moment at
  its own rate.  For a centered probability law with $\int e^{r|x|}d\mu<\infty$ for one $r>0$,
  $\kappa_3(u,v,w)=\int\langle u,x\rangle\langle v,x\rangle\langle w,x\rangle\,d\mu$ and continuous
  functions with polynomial growth lie in $L^2(\mu)$; if $\mu$ is isotropic and satisfies the Letwin
  bound, $\sum_{j,k}\kappa_3(u,e_j,e_k)^2\le 8|u|^2$ in every orthonormal basis.  Hence every isotropic
  log-concave law satisfying the Letwin bound has $R_3\le 8\,\mathrm{Id}$, and if all isotropic
  log-concave laws in positive dimension satisfy the Letwin bound then the universal cumulant bound of
  order $3$ with constant $8$ holds.  Letwin Theorem~1.2 and the unconditional Lemma~4.2 are not proved.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Differentiation of the Laplace transform inside the exponential radius for the cumulant identity;
  the contraction argument of Lemma~\ref{lem:bkl-third-cumulant-conditional} with every $L^2$ fact
  taken from the single radius; Borell's exponential moment for the log-concave adapter.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Second cumulant and the Theorem 4.1 base case]
  \label{lem:bkl-second-cumulant-base}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.cumulant_two_eq_centered_moment_of_integrable_exp,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.cumulant_two_eq_covariance_of_integrable_exp,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.cumulant_two_eq_inner_of_isotropic,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.sum_sq_cumulant_two_eq_norm_sq_of_isotropic,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.Suspension.cumulantBound_one_one}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:bkl-local-third-cumulant}
  \cite{BizeulKlartagLehec2026}, p.~18, the base $m=2$ of Theorem~4.1.  For a probability law with
  one exponential moment $\int e^{r|x|}d\mu<\infty$, $r>0$ (no centering), the second cumulant is the
  centered second moment $\kappa_2(u,v)=\int\langle u,x\rangle\langle v,x\rangle\,d\mu-\int\langle u,x\rangle d\mu\int\langle v,x\rangle d\mu
  =\langle\mathrm{Cov}(\mu)u,v\rangle$.  For an isotropic log-concave law $\kappa_2(u,v)=\langle u,v\rangle$,
  so $\sum_j\kappa_2(u,e_j)^2=|u|^2$ in every orthonormal basis and the universal cumulant bound
  $\mathrm{CumulantBound}\,1\,1$ ($R_2=\mathrm{Id}$) holds unconditionally in every dimension, including
  dimension zero. This lemma supplies the base order only; Theorem~\ref{thm:bkl-cumulant-bound} supplies and certifies the all-order result.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Differentiate $\log\int e^{\langle z,x\rangle}d\mu$ twice inside the exponential radius with the
  product rule for $z\mapsto\int e^{\langle z,x\rangle}\langle v,x\rangle d\mu=F(z)Z(z)$; isotropy and Parseval.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Gradient jets of the cumulant-generating function at zero]
  \label{lem:bkl-cgf-gradient-jets}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.cumulantVector,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.cumulantVector_inner,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.gradient_logLaplace_zero_of_compact,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.fderiv_centeredGradient_logLaplace_zero_of_compact,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.iteratedFDeriv_sub_const_of_pos,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.inner_iteratedFDeriv_gradient_apply,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.iteratedFDeriv_centeredGradient_logLaplace_zero_of_compact,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.cumulant_comp_perm_of_compact,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.inverseSqrt_mul_mul_inverseSqrt}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:bkl-second-cumulant-base}
  \cite{BizeulKlartagLehec2026}, p.~20, the sentence before~(74): for the cumulant-generating function
  $\Lambda=\log\int e^{\langle z,x\rangle}d\mu$ of a compactly supported probability law and
  $v(z)=\nabla\Lambda(z)-\nabla\Lambda(0)$, one has $\nabla\Lambda(0)=\int x\,d\mu$ (so $v(0)=0$) and
  $Dv(0)=A$, the covariance operator.  The Riesz vector $\kappa_{|hs|+1}(hs,\cdot)$ of the last slot of a
  scalar cumulant is recorded as \texttt{cumulantVector}.  The cumulant tensors of a compactly supported law are
  symmetric: $\kappa_m(h\circ\sigma)=\kappa_m(h)$ for every permutation $\sigma$ and every $m$.  Also: the principal inverse square root of a positive
  unit satisfies $\sigma A\sigma=\mathrm{Id}$ (it whitens the covariance; no statement about the transported law is
  made here).  For every $r\ge1$ the higher jet is the cumulant Riesz vector,
  $D^rv(0)[h]=\kappa_{r+1}(h,\cdot)$, i.e. $\langle D^rv(0)[h],w\rangle=\kappa_{r+1}(h,w)$ with $w$ in the last slot,
  from two generic supports: $D^r(f-c)=D^rf$ for $r\ge1$ on real normed spaces and
  $\langle D^r(\nabla f)(x)[h],w\rangle=D^{r+1}f(x)[h,w]$ on complete real inner product spaces.  Not provided: the
  drift expansion~(74) and any stochastic statement.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Riesz representation of the first derivative of $\Lambda$ at $0$ (the mean pairing); the derivative of
  $\nabla\Lambda$ at $0$ is the second derivative, which is the covariance by the second-cumulant identity;
  $\sigma A\sigma\,\sigma=\sigma$ and right cancellation by the unit $\sigma$.  Higher jets: shift invariance,
  the Riesz chart of the gradient commuted with the iterated derivative, and the successor formula with the new
  direction in the last slot, identified with the scalar cumulant's last slot.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Deterministic supports of the localization noise and non-explosion estimates]
  \label{lem:bkl-localization-supports}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Probability.covariance_sq_le_variance_mul_variance,
        FunctionalInequalities.Covariance.variance_inner_eq_of_sandwich,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.sum_trace_sq_le_card_mul_sum_sq,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.sum_sq_integral_mul_mul_le_integral_norm_pow_six,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.sum_sq_integral_comp_mul_mul_le_integral_norm_pow_six,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.opNorm_covariance_compactPosterior_le_sq_radius}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:bkl-posterior-covariance-domain}
  \cite{BizeulKlartagLehec2026}, (67)--(68): three deterministic supports of the proposed noise estimate for
  the expectation equation~(68) and of the proposed log-determinant non-explosion route for~(67).
  (i)~Covariance Cauchy--Schwarz on an arbitrary probability space: $\mathrm{cov}(X,Y)^2\le\mathrm{Var}X\,\mathrm{Var}Y$
  for square-integrable real statistics.  (ii)~Whitened linear statistics: for a law with finite second moment
  on a finite-dimensional inner product space and a self-adjoint $S$ with the actual sandwich
  $S\,\mathrm{Cov}(\mu)\,S=\mathrm{Id}$, $\mathrm{Var}\langle Sh,\cdot\rangle=|h|^2$; the sandwich is a hypothesis,
  so this is conditional on a root and produces none.  (iii)~Trace energy for real $n\times n$ matrices
  $B_1,\dots,B_n$ without symmetry or positivity: $\sum_i(\operatorname{tr}B_i)^2\le n\sum_{i,j,k}(B_i)_{jk}^2$.
  (iv)~Third-moment contraction for a probability law on $\mathbb R^n$ with integrable sixth moment (no centering
  or log-concavity): $\sum_{i,j,k}\big(\int x_ix_jx_k\,d\nu\big)^2\le\int|x|^6\,d\nu$, and the same for an
  a.e.-measurable random vector $Y$ on any probability space: $\sum_{i,j,k}\big(\int Y_iY_jY_k\big)^2\le\int|Y|^6$.  (v)~Covariance radius: for a
  compactly supported prior with support in the closed ball of radius $r$, every posterior $\mu_{c,Q}$ (all finite
  pairs, no isotropy) has $\|\mathrm{Cov}(\mu_{c,Q})\|\le r^2$.
  The noise bound itself is \cref{lem:bkl-noise-bound}.  Not provided: the covariance dynamics, the It\^o formula, a strong solution or any non-explosion theorem.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  (i)~The variance of $Y-tX$ is a nonnegative quadratic polynomial in $t$, expanded by bilinearity of the
  covariance; both the zero- and positive-variance branches in $X$ are treated.  (ii)~The variance of a linear
  statistic is the covariance bilinear form, which is the Riesz pairing with the covariance operator; move $S$
  across the pairing by self-adjointness and cancel with the sandwich.  (iii)~Cauchy--Schwarz on the diagonal
  and the nonnegative off-diagonal squares.  (iv)~$(\int g)^2\le\int g^2$ termwise and
  $\sum_{ijk}(x_ix_jx_k)^2=|x|^6$.  (v)~$\langle\mathrm{Cov}\,v,w\rangle=\mathrm{cov}(\langle v,\cdot\rangle,
  \langle w,\cdot\rangle)$, (i), $\mathrm{Var}\langle v,\cdot\rangle\le r^2|v|^2$ on the support (the posterior
  keeps the prior's support), then $|Av|^2\le r^2|v||Av|$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Canonical standardisation of the localization posterior]
  \label{lem:bkl-canonical-standardisation}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.KLS.Isotropic.exists_inverseSqrt_equiv_isotropic_standardized}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:bkl-posterior-covariance-domain,lem:bkl-cgf-gradient-jets}
  \cite{BizeulKlartagLehec2026}, (67)--(68) and the transported law of (75)--(77): for a compactly supported
  isotropic prior $\mu$ and every finite parameter pair $(c,Q)$ (including $c=Q=0$, non-symmetric and negative
  $Q$, and rank zero), the principal inverse square root $\sigma=\mathrm{Cov}(\mu_{c,Q})^{-1/2}$ of~(67) is a
  continuous linear equivalence $W$ with $W=\sigma$ as a map, and the image of the centred posterior
  $\mu_{c,Q}(\cdot+a)$, $a$ the posterior mean, under $W$ is isotropic (finite second moment, mean zero,
  covariance $\mathrm{Id}$).  The same principal root is used, not an arbitrary whitening.  Not provided: any
  rotation of the Brownian driver, stochastic equation, It\^o identity, martingale property, non-explosion or
  Theorem~4.1 claim.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  $\sigma$ is a unit (positive root of a positive unit); transport the second moment, the mean (translation by
  the mean centres the law) and the covariance along the translation and the equivalence,
  $\mathrm{Cov}(T_\#\nu)=T\,\mathrm{Cov}(\nu)\,T^*$; self-adjointness of $\sigma$ and the sandwich
  $\sigma A\sigma=\mathrm{Id}$ give the identity covariance.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Noise coefficient of the expectation equation]
  \label{lem:bkl-noise-bound}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.KLS.Isotropic.opNorm_sq_fderiv_posteriorExpectation_comp_inverseSqrt_le_variance,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.Isotropic.opNorm_sq_fderiv_posteriorExpectation_comp_inverseSqrt_le_sq}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:bkl-localization-supports,lem:bkl-cgf-gradient-jets,lem:bkl-posterior-parameter-response}
  \cite{BizeulKlartagLehec2026}, (68): for a compactly supported isotropic prior, a measurable statistic $f$ with
  $|f|\le C$ on the prior support and every finite pair $(c,Q)$ (signed, non-symmetric $Q$ and rank zero included),
  the noise coefficient of the equation of $\int f\,d\mu_{c,Q}$, namely $D_c\big(\int f\,d\mu_{\cdot,Q}\big)(c)\circ
  \mathrm{Cov}(\mu_{c,Q})^{-1/2}$ with the same principal root as~(67), has squared operator norm at most
  $\mathrm{Var}_{\mu_{c,Q}}f\le C^2$.  Deterministic coefficient bound only: no stochastic integral, It\^o identity,
  martingale property or quadratic-variation statement.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  $D_c(\int f)(c)[\sigma h]=\mathrm{cov}_{\mu_{c,Q}}(f,\langle\sigma h,\cdot\rangle)$ (parameter response),
  covariance Cauchy--Schwarz, $\mathrm{Var}\langle\sigma h,\cdot\rangle=|h|^2$ from the sandwich
  $\sigma A\sigma=\mathrm{Id}$, then $\mathrm{Var}f\le\int f^2\le C^2$ on the posterior, which keeps the prior's support.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Principal-root noise entries]
  \label{lem:bkl-noise-entries}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.KLS.Isotropic.operatorMatrix_inverseSqrt_mul_thirdMomentMatrix_mul_apply,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.operatorMatrix_mul_coordinateThirdMoment_mul_apply_of_ae_norm_sub_le,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.integrable_coord_mul_coord_mul_clm_coord_of_ae_norm_sub_le,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.integrable_coord_mul_coord_mul_coord_of_ae_norm_sub_le,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.integral_sum_sum_const_mul,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.Posterior.compactPosterior_exists_ae_norm_sub_le,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.euclidean_clm_apply_eq_sum_operatorMatrix,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.euclidean_inner_selfAdjoint_basisFun,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.sum_sum_mul_mul_mul_mul_eq_of_symm}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:bkl-canonical-standardisation}
  \cite{BizeulKlartagLehec2026}, p.~19, (69), the deterministic entry identity: for a compactly supported isotropic prior
  and every finite $(c,Q)$ (signed, non-symmetric $Q$ included), with $V=x-a$, the same principal root
  $\sigma=\mathrm{Cov}(\mu_{c,Q})^{-1/2}$ as in~(67), its matrix $P$ in the fixed original basis $e$ and the computed
  slices $H_i(j,k)=\int V_jV_k\langle\sigma e_i,V\rangle\,d\mu_{c,Q}$, one has
  $(PH_iP)_{jk}=\int(\sigma V)_j(\sigma V)_k(\sigma V)_i\,d\mu_{c,Q}$.  Generic form: any finite measure a.e.\ supported
  in a ball and any self-adjoint $T$.  Not provided: the covariance equation~(69) as a stochastic equality, the energy
  bounds built on these slices, or any moment bound.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Coordinates of $\sigma V$ through $P$, self-adjointness of $\sigma$, integrability of cubic coordinate monomials on the
  bounded support, exchange of finite double sums with the integral, and the symmetric-sandwich entry algebra.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Canonical sixth moment of the posterior]
  \label{lem:bkl-posterior-sixth-moment}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.KLS.Isotropic.integral_norm_pow_six_inverseSqrt_sub_posteriorMean_le}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:bkl-canonical-standardisation}
  \cite{BizeulKlartagLehec2026}, the sixth-moment input of the energy bounds: for a compactly supported isotropic
  log-concave prior on $\mathbb R^n$, every $c$ and every self-adjoint positive semidefinite $Q$, the canonically
  standardised posterior variable $Y=\mathrm{Cov}(\mu_{c,Q})^{-1/2}(x-a)$ ($a$ the posterior mean, principal root as
  in~(67)) has integrable $|Y|^6$ and
  $\int|Y|^6\,d\mu_{c,Q}\le 6!\,\big(\log 3/(8\sqrt{n+1})\big)^{-6}\cdot 3^{3/4}/(1-3^{-1/4})$.  The constant is the
  explicit one of the formal statement, dimension-dependent; signed or non-self-adjoint $Q$ is outside this statement.
  Not provided: the third-moment matrix identity~(69), the energy bounds that use this moment, or any stochastic statement.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The standardised posterior is isotropic log-concave; an exponential moment of $|Y|$ at an explicit rate from the
  log-concave tail bound, and $t^6\le 6!\,e^{t}$ after rescaling.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Permutation symmetry and raw Leibniz expansion of iterated derivatives]
  \label{lem:iterated-derivative-symmetry-leibniz}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.directional,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.directional_perm,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.directional_ofFn,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.iteratedFDeriv_perm,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.selected,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.selected_mask_partition,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.directional_bilinear,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.iteratedFDeriv_bilinear,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.sum_filter_eq_two_mul_sum_filter_of_compl_invariant}
  \leanok
  Generic calculus supports for the cumulant algebra of \cite{BizeulKlartagLehec2026}, pp.~20--21,
  (71)--(74).  For a globally $C^\infty$ map $f$ between real normed spaces, successive directional
  derivatives along a list commute under every permutation (genuine real $C^2$ symmetry, no analytic or
  tensor-symmetry premise), the list convention equals the raw iterated Fr\'echet derivative, so
  $D^mf(x)[h\circ\sigma]=D^mf(x)[h]$; and for a continuous bilinear $B$ and $C^\infty$ maps $f,g$,
  $D^m\big(B(f,g)\big)(x)[h]=\sum_{q\in\{0,1\}^m}B\big(D^{|q|}f(x)[h_q],D^{m-|q|}g(x)[h_{\neg q}]\big)$,
  every subset allocation with coefficient~$1$ in inherited slot order (no factorial).  Complement pairing: a
  complement-invariant real summand over a complement-invariant set of masks sums to twice its sum over the masks
  selecting a fixed slot.  Ordered-mask partition: for every $h:[m]\to E$ and mask $q$ the two selections have
  lengths adding to $m$ and together form a permutation of $(h_1,\dots,h_m)$, the indicator of one slot $i$ selects
  exactly $[h_i]$, and a selection of length~$1$ comes from such an indicator (repeated values and $m=0$ included).
  The spatial expansion~(74) of $\langle v,Mv\rangle$ and its grouping are
  Lemmas~\ref{lem:bkl-raw-drift-expansion} and~\ref{lem:bkl-grouped-drift-expansion}.  Not provided: parameter jets
  or any stochastic statement.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Adjacent swaps by the symmetric second derivative of the $C^\infty$ composite; induction on the list and on
  the permutation; the Leibniz rule for a bilinear image at each successive derivative, splitting the Boolean
  indicator at the new slot.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Raw expansion of the drift quadratic form]
  \label{lem:bkl-raw-drift-expansion}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.iteratedFDeriv_inner_centeredGradient_zero_of_compact,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.directional_centeredGradient_logLaplace_zero_of_compact,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.iteratedFDeriv_inner_clm,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.selected_eq_nil_iff}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:bkl-cgf-gradient-jets,lem:iterated-derivative-symmetry-leibniz}
  \cite{BizeulKlartagLehec2026}, p.~21, (74), at a fixed law: for a compactly supported probability law with
  $\Lambda=\log\int e^{\langle z,x\rangle}d\mu$, $v(z)=\nabla\Lambda(z)-\nabla\Lambda(0)$ and any continuous linear $M$,
  $D^m\langle v,Mv\rangle(0)[h]=\sum_{\emptyset\ne J\subsetneq[m]}\langle\kappa_{|J|+1}(h_J,\cdot),
  M\kappa_{m-|J|+1}(h_{J^c},\cdot)\rangle$, every subset with coefficient~$1$ in inherited slot order (no factorial);
  the empty and full subsets vanish because $v(0)=0$.  Supports: the directional derivatives of $v$ at $0$ are $0$ for
  the empty list and the cumulant Riesz vectors otherwise; the Leibniz rule for $\langle f,Mg\rangle$; a Boolean mask
  selects nothing exactly when it is all false.  The grouping for $M=A^{-1}$ is
  Lemma~\ref{lem:bkl-grouped-drift-expansion}.  Not provided: parameter jets or any stochastic statement (the
  source's (71)/(73) are stochastic).
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Raw bilinear Leibniz rule for $(x,y)\mapsto\langle x,My\rangle$, the jets of $v$ at $0$, and the vanishing of the
  empty/full allocations.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Singleton allocation of the drift expansion]
  \label{lem:bkl-singleton-allocation}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.inner_cumulantVector_single_ringInverse_cumulantVector}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:bkl-cgf-gradient-jets}
  \cite{BizeulKlartagLehec2026}, p.~21, the singleton terms of~(74): for a compactly supported probability law whose
  covariance $A$ is invertible, every list $h_1,\dots,h_k$ and every $w$,
  $\langle\kappa_2(w,\cdot),A^{-1}\kappa_{k+1}(h_1,\dots,h_k,\cdot)\rangle=\kappa_{k+1}(h_1,\dots,h_k,w)$, the cumulant of
  order $k+1$ with $w$ in the last slot.  The full expansion~(74) and its grouping are
  Lemmas~\ref{lem:bkl-raw-drift-expansion} and~\ref{lem:bkl-grouped-drift-expansion}.  Not provided: parameter jets
  or any stochastic statement.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  $\kappa_2(w,\cdot)=Aw$ (second cumulant equals covariance), self-adjointness of $A$ and $AA^{-1}=\mathrm{Id}$, then the
  Riesz representation of the cumulant vector.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Grouped drift expansion]
  \label{lem:bkl-grouped-drift-expansion}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.iteratedFDeriv_inner_centeredGradient_zero_grouped_of_compact}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:bkl-raw-drift-expansion,lem:bkl-singleton-allocation,lem:iterated-derivative-symmetry-leibniz}
  \cite{BizeulKlartagLehec2026}, pp.~20--21, the deterministic spatial step of the proof of Lemma~4.3: the expansion~(74)
  grouped with the form $L_m$ of~(72), at a fixed law.  For a compactly supported probability law with invertible
  covariance $A$, $\Lambda=\log\int e^{\langle z,x\rangle}d\mu$, $v(z)=\nabla\Lambda(z)-\nabla\Lambda(0)$, every $m\ge3$,
  all $h_1,\dots,h_m$ (repeated directions allowed) and every slot $i_0$,
  $D^m\langle v,A^{-1}v\rangle(0)[h]=2m\,\kappa_m(h)+2\sum_{J\ni i_0,\ |J|\ge2,\ |J^c|\ge2}
  \langle\kappa_{|J|+1}(h_J,\cdot),A^{-1}\kappa_{m-|J|+1}(h_{J^c},\cdot)\rangle$; for $i_0=1$ the sum is $L_m(h)$
  of~(72) (reviewed stronger: any representative slot).  Not provided: the stochastic differential~(73), Eldan's process
  and It\^o calculus, hence Lemma~4.3 and Theorem~4.1.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The raw expansion~(74) for $M=A^{-1}$; the $2m$ allocations with $|J|\in\{1,m-1\}$ equal $\kappa_m(h)$ by the singleton
  allocation, cumulant symmetry and the ordered-mask partition; the other allocations pair with their complements by
  self-adjointness of $A^{-1}$, keeping the representative containing $i_0$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Uniform mass of open halfspaces]
  \label{lem:uniform-halfspace-mass}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Probability.exists_pos_le_measure_halfspace_of_mem_interior_convexHull_support,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.exists_mem_support_inner_sub_pos_of_mem_interior_convexHull,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.exists_pos_ball_inner_sub_pos_of_mem_support}
  \leanok
  Cordero-Erausquin--Klartag \cite{CorderoKlartag2015}, proof of Lemma~16, bound~(54), probability specialization: for a
  Borel probability measure $\mu$ on a finite-dimensional real inner product space and $x_0$ in the interior of the convex
  hull of its support there is $m\in(0,1]$ with $\mu\{x:\langle u,x-x_0\rangle>0\}\ge m$ for every unit vector $u$ (strict
  open halfspaces; in dimension~$0$ the unit sphere is empty).  Lemma~16 itself is
  Lemma~\ref{lem:cek-point-control}.  Not provided: the existence of a moment potential, or Letwin's quadratic-form
  bound with constant~$8$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  A support point strictly on the positive side of each direction, a ball of positive mass inside every nearby open
  halfspace, a finite cover of the compact unit sphere, and the finite product of the ball masses (each in $(0,1]$).
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Pointwise bound by the integral]
  \label{lem:cek-point-control}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Probability.exists_pos_le_mul_lintegral_toReal_of_convex_epigraph,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.mem_interior_setOf_ne_top_of_lintegral_ne_top,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.exists_pos_le_measure_halfspace_of_mem_interior_convexHull_support_of_isFiniteMeasure,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.real_convexOn_effective_domain}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:uniform-halfspace-mass}
  Cordero-Erausquin--Klartag \cite{CorderoKlartag2015}, Lemma~16: let $\mu$ be a finite Borel measure on a
  finite-dimensional real inner product space and $x_0$ a point of the interior of the convex hull of its support.  There
  is $C>0$, chosen before $\varphi$, such that $\varphi(x_0)\le C\int\varphi\,d\mu$ for every $\varphi$ with values in
  $[0,+\infty]$, convex real epigraph, a.e.-measurable values and finite integral.  Dimension~$0$ is included; $\mu$ is not
  normalized.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  $\varphi$ is finite near $x_0$; the halfspace bound transfers to finite measures by normalization ($m\le\mu(E)$); either
  $x_0$ minimizes $\varphi$ or a separating unit vector gives an open halfspace on which $\varphi\ge\varphi(x_0)$.  The
  halfspace bound replaces the source's Fatou argument.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Extended-real Legendre transform and convexification]
  \label{lem:legendre-convexification}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.legendre_antitone, FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.fenchel_le,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.biconjugate_le, FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.triconjugate_eq,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.lowerSemicontinuous_legendre,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.convexPotential_legendre,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.lowerTruncate_convexification,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.MomentMeasure.mass_legendre_mono}
  \leanok
  For $\varphi\colon E\to[-\infty,+\infty]$ on a real inner product space, $\varphi^*(y)=\sup_x(\langle x,y\rangle-\varphi(x))$.
  The transform reverses order, is lower semicontinuous with convex epigraph for every input, has no value $-\infty$ exactly
  when $\varphi$ has a finite value, and satisfies $\langle x,y\rangle\le\varphi(x)+\varphi^*(y)$, $\varphi^{**}\le\varphi$
  and $\varphi^{***}=\varphi^*$ for proper $\varphi$.  For the lower truncation $g=\max(\varphi,-M)$ of
  \cite{CorderoKlartag2015}, (61), with one finite value of $\varphi$, $g^{**}$ is convex, lower semicontinuous and proper,
  lies between $-M$ and $g$, and has the same transform as $g$; $\int e^{-\varphi^*}$ is monotone in $\varphi$.  These are the
  truncation steps of the proof of Proposition~12; the maximiser is Theorem~\ref{thm:cek-variational-maximum}.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Suprema of continuous affine functions, with the infinite intercepts handled separately; the triconjugate identity from
  order reversal and $\varphi^{**}\le\varphi$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Compactness of normalised potentials, geometric form]
  \label{lem:cek-compactness}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Probability.MomentMeasure.exists_subseq_integral_bounds_of_convex_epigraph,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.exists_subseq_lintegral_le_liminf_of_convex_epigraph,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.MomentMeasure.limsup_mass_legendre_le,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_subseq_tendsto_of_convexOn_of_bddAbove,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_radial_extension_of_convexOn}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:cek-point-control, lem:legendre-convexification}
  \cite{CorderoKlartag2015}, Lemma~17, with $0\in\operatorname{int}\operatorname{conv}\operatorname{Supp}\mu$ as an explicit
  hypothesis: let $\mu$ be a finite Borel measure on a finite-dimensional real inner product space and $\varphi_\ell$ functions
  with values in $[0,+\infty]$, convex real epigraphs, $\varphi_\ell(0)=0$, a.e.-measurable values and
  $\sup_\ell\int\varphi_\ell\,d\mu<\infty$.  One subsequence and one nonnegative convex $\mu$-integrable $\psi$ satisfy
  $\int\psi\,d\mu\le\liminf_j\int\varphi_{\ell_j}\,d\mu$ and, for every additive Haar measure,
  $\limsup_j\int e^{-\varphi_{\ell_j}^*}\le\int e^{-\psi^*}<\infty$.  The finiteness is derived; zero mass and dimension~$0$
  are included.  The conditions of Theorem~2 give the interior-hull hypothesis by Lemma~\ref{lem:cek-zero-interior}; no
  statement of the composition in the source form is made.  Not provided: Theorem~2.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Lemma~\ref{lem:cek-point-control} bounds $\varphi_\ell$ on the interior $K$ of the support hull; pointwise compactness of
  bounded convex functions gives a subsequence converging on $K$, and the radial extension gives $\psi$.  Fatou along the
  scaled potentials and then along the radial limit gives the first bound.  A finite set whose hull contains a neighbourhood
  of $0$ gives a common integrable exponential dominator, and reverse Fatou with the interior formula for $\psi^*$ gives the
  second.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Interior of the support hull of a centered measure]
  \label{lem:cek-zero-interior}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Probability.zero_mem_interior_convexHull_support_of_integral_eq_zero}
  \leanok
  \cite{CorderoKlartag2015}, the step implicit in the proof of Lemma~17 under the conditions of Theorem~2: for a nonzero
  finite Borel measure $\mu$ on a finite-dimensional real inner product space with finite first moment and barycenter $0$,
  such that no proper linear subspace has full measure, $0$ lies in the interior of the convex hull of $\operatorname{Supp}\mu$.
  The total mass is not normalized; in dimension~$0$ the zero measure is excluded by $\mu\ne0$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  A nonzero continuous functional vanishing on the direction of the affine span of the support would make $\mu$ concentrated
  on its kernel, so the hull has nonempty interior; a functional separating $0$ from the interior is $\le0$ on the support,
  has integral $0$, and again forces $\mu$ onto its kernel.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Coercivity, truncation and normalization for Proposition~12]
  \label{lem:cek-coercivity}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Probability.MomentMeasure.exists_coercivity_of_centered_probability,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.MomentMeasure.exists_convexified_lowerTruncate_variational_le_add,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.MomentMeasure.variationalFunctional_affineSubtract,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_supporting_affine_at_zero,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.MomentMeasure.norm_baseline_finite,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.CEK.exists_normalized_competitor}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:cek-zero-interior, lem:legendre-convexification, lem:cek-point-control}
  Support for \cite{CorderoKlartag2015}, Proposition~12, for a probability measure with finite first moment, barycenter $0$
  and no proper linear subspace of full measure: a conjugate-mass bound polynomial in the integral, with its constant chosen
  before the potential; every admissible competitor is within $\varepsilon$ of the biconjugate of a lower truncation; the
  functional is invariant under subtracting an affine function (for finite measures the integral changes by the mass times
  the constant); a supporting affine function at an interior point of the effective domain normalizes a convex potential to
  be nonnegative and zero at the origin; the norm potential has finite positive mass and finite value.  The bound is not the
  constant of Lemma~15.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Lemma~16 at the vertices of a fixed simplex and the affine-maximum lower bound of the transform give linear growth, then
  Haar scaling; dominated convergence for the truncations; Hahn--Banach separation for the supporting plane.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Existence of a variational maximiser]
  \label{thm:cek-variational-maximum}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.KLS.CEK.exists_variational_maximum,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.CEK.exists_limit_ge_normalized_supremum,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.CEK.exists_normalized_maximizing_sequence,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.MomentMeasure.log_sub_ge_of_integral_limits}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:cek-coercivity, lem:cek-compactness}
  \cite{CorderoKlartag2015}, Proposition~12~(38): let $\mu$ be a probability measure on a finite-dimensional real inner product
  space with finite first moment and barycenter $0$ such that no proper linear subspace has full measure, and $dx$ an additive
  Haar measure.  There is $\varphi$ with values in $(-\infty,+\infty]$, convex real epigraph, lower semicontinuous and
  $\mu$-integrable, with $\int e^{-\varphi^*}\,dx=1$ and $I_\mu(f)\le I_\mu(\varphi)$ for every $\mu$-integrable $f$, where
  $I_\mu(f)=\log\int e^{-f^*}\,dx-\int f\,d\mu$ with the extended logarithm.  Not provided: essential continuity, the
  identification of $\mu$ as the moment measure of the maximiser, or Theorem~2.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  A normalized maximizing sequence approaching the finite supremum; coercivity bounds its integrals; an integral-convergent
  subsequence is chosen before Lemma~17's subsequence, so both limit inequalities hold along one composed subsequence; the
  biconjugate preserves the dual mass, and affine invariance with injectivity of the logarithm normalizes the mass to $1$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[CEK maximiser for a bounded source: normalized Lipschitz primal]
  \label{lem:cek-bounded-primal}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.KLS.CEK.bounded_source_cap_lsc,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.CEK.bounded_source_finite_conjugate,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.CEK.bounded_source_lipschitz_primal,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.CEK.bounded_source_real_density_client,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.closedBallCap,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.legendre_finite_of_bounded_domain,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.lipschitzWith_real_legendre,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.MomentMeasure.real_legendre_normalization}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:cek-variational-maximum}
  \cite{CorderoKlartag2015}, Proposition~12 for a bounded source, a support toward Theorem~2 and Letwin
  \cite{Letwin2026QuadraticKLS}, Lemmas~2.1--2.2.  Let $\mu$ be an isotropic probability law on a finite-dimensional real
  inner product space (dimension zero included), $L\ge0$ and $\|y\|\le L$ for $\mu$-almost every $y$.  The Proposition~12
  maximiser capped to $\bar B(0,L)$ (value $+\infty$ outside) is again a maximiser: convex, lower semicontinuous,
  $\mu$-integrable, with effective domain in the ball and $\int e^{-q^*}=1$.  It attains a real minimum $m$ at some
  $a\in\bar B(0,L)$, its conjugate is finite everywhere with $q^*(x)\le L\|x\|-m$, and the real representative $\psi=q^*$ is
  convex, $L$-Lipschitz and continuous, with $e^{-\psi}$ Bochner integrable, $\int e^{-\psi}=1$ and $e^{-\psi}dx$ a
  probability measure; moreover $\psi(0)=-m$, $\langle a,x\rangle-m\le\psi(x)$, $|\psi(x)+m|\le L\|x\|$, and $e^{-\psi}$ is
  positive and midpoint log-concave.  The uniform law on $[-\sqrt3,\sqrt3]$ is an example.  A weak normalized
  primal: not provided are the Euler--Lagrange identification, the gradient push-forward $(\nabla\psi)_\#e^{-\psi}=\mu$,
  smoothness, a positive definite Hessian, the gradient diffeomorphism, the Monge--Amp\`ere equation and Theorem~2.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The cap agrees with the maximiser $\mu$-almost everywhere and only raises the potential, so its dual mass is at least $1$
  while optimality bounds it by $1$.  Lower semicontinuity and compactness of the ball give the minimum; bounded slopes bound
  the supremum defining $q^*$; convexity and the Lipschitz bound pass to the finite representative, and mass one gives
  Bochner integrability before any real conversion.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Attainment of the Legendre supremum and the dual gradient]
  \label{lem:legendre-attainment}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_finite_maximum_of_upperSemicontinuous_linear_decay,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_coercive_tilt_of_mem_interior_legendre_domain,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.tiltedObjective_data,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.conjugate_maximizer_eq_gradient,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_legendre_maximizer_with_gradient_identity}
  \leanok
  Pointwise prerequisite of \cite{CorderoKlartag2015}, (42) (Berman--Berndtsson, Lemma~2.7 / Appendix~6).  On a
  finite-dimensional real inner product space (dimension zero included): an upper semicontinuous $G\colon E\to
  [-\infty,+\infty]$ that never equals $+\infty$, is finite somewhere and satisfies $G(x)\le b-a\|x\|$ with $a>0$ attains a finite
  maximum.  For a proper $\varphi\colon E\to(-\infty,+\infty]$ and $y$ interior to $\{\varphi^*<\infty\}$: the tilted primal grows
  linearly, $a\|z\|-b\le\varphi(z)-\langle z,y\rangle$; every $z$ with $\langle z,y\rangle-\varphi(z)=\varphi^*(y)$ equals
  $\nabla\varphi^*(y)$ when $\varphi^*$ is differentiable at $y$; and, for lower semicontinuous $\varphi$, such a maximizer
  exists.  No convexity, differentiability, nonnegativity or global finiteness of $\varphi$ is assumed.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Compactness of a large ball and the decay; finitely many finite dual values around $y$ give an affine maximum below the
  tilted primal; a maximizer is a local maximum of $q\mapsto\langle z,q\rangle-\varphi^*(q)$ by the Fenchel inequality.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[First variation of the Legendre transform]
  \label{lem:legendre-first-variation}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.legendre_bounded_perturbation,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.legendre_bounded_perturbation_finite_curve,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_delta_nearMaximizer_norm_sub_gradient_lt,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.hasDerivAt_legendre_add_smul_of_dualDifferentiable}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:legendre-attainment}
  \cite{CorderoKlartag2015}, (42), pointwise.  For proper $\varphi$ and $|b|\le M$: $\varphi^*-|t|M\le(\varphi+tb)^*\le
  \varphi^*+|t|M$ for the whole extended-real supremum, so at a point where $\varphi^*$ is finite the perturbed conjugates form a
  finite real curve.  If moreover $\varphi$ is lower semicontinuous, $y$ is interior to $\{\varphi^*<\infty\}$ and $\varphi^*$ is
  differentiable at $y$, then near-maximizers concentrate at $\nabla\varphi^*(y)$, and for every bounded continuous real $b$,
  $\frac{d}{dt}\big|_{t=0}(\varphi+tb)^*(y)=-b(\nabla\varphi^*(y))$.  $\varphi$ may take $+\infty$; every dimension, zero
  included.  Not provided: the integral first variation (Euler--Lagrange) for a source law, the gradient push-forward, source
  regularity, Theorem~2, and the Letwin and BKL theorems that use them.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The perturbation bound is termwise; approximate maximizers of the perturbed problem are near-maximizers of the unperturbed
  one, hence converge to $\nabla\varphi^*(y)$, which squeezes the difference quotient.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Eighth-moment tail bounds for ball conditioning]
  \label{lem:moment-tails-eighth}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Probability.measure_ball_compl_toReal_le_integral_norm_pow_eight_div,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.integrable_norm_pow_four_and_tail_le_of_integrable_pow_eight,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.one_half_le_measure_ball_toReal_of_integral_norm_pow_eight}
  \leanok
  First supporting estimates for the ball-conditioning stage of the moment-potential construction (Letwin \cite{Letwin2026QuadraticKLS},
  Appendix~A.1: conditioning a smoothed law on a ball of radius $\varepsilon^{-1/2}$ before centering and
  whitening).  For a probability law $\nu$ on a normed group with Borel structure, integrable $|x|^8$ and $R>0$,
  with $M_8=\int|x|^8\,d\nu$: $\nu(B(0,R)^c)\le M_8/R^8$; $|x|^4$ and its restriction to $B(0,R)^c$ are integrable and
  $\int_{B(0,R)^c}|x|^4\,d\nu\le M_8/R^4$; and if $2M_8\le R^8$ then $\nu(B(0,R))\ge 1/2>0$ (half mass).  The ball is
  open and $R>0$ is indispensable (a Dirac at $0$ with $R=0$).  Not provided: covariance convergence or the canonical
  whitening of the conditioned sequence.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Markov's inequality for $|x|^8$ at level $R^8$ and monotonicity from the open-ball complement; $|x|^4\le1+|x|^8$ and
  $|x|^4\le|x|^8/R^4$ off the ball, then monotonicity of the integral; the half mass from the tail bound and
  $\nu(B)+\nu(B^c)=1$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Conditioning on a ball]
  \label{lem:ball-conditioning}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Probability.conditionOnBall,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.coe_conditionOnBall,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.abs_integral_conditionOnBall_sub_le}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:moment-tails-eighth}
  Letwin \cite{Letwin2026QuadraticKLS}, Appendix~A.1, the conditioning step.  For a probability law $\nu$ on a normed group with
  Borel structure and an open ball $B=B(0,R)$ of positive mass $p$, $\nu_B=\nu(\cdot\mid B)$.  If $|x|^8$ is integrable,
  $R>0$, $C\ge0$ and $f$ is measurable with $|f(x)|\le C(1+|x|^4)$, then $f$ is integrable under $\nu$ and $\nu_B$ and
  $\big|\int f\,d\nu_B-\int f\,d\nu\big|\le\big((1-p)\,|\int f\,d\nu|+\int_{B^c}|f|\,d\nu\big)/p
  \le (C/p)\big((1-p)(2+\int|x|^4\,d\nu)+M_8/R^4\big)$.  Positive ball mass is a definition domain of $\nu_B$, not a
  premise on the conclusion.  Not provided: smoothing, covariance convergence or whitening of the conditioned sequence.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  $\int f\,d\nu_B=(\int_B f\,d\nu)/p$ and $\int f=\int_B f+\int_{B^c}f$; the exact error identity, then the tail bounds
  and the degree-four growth.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Near-symmetric gradient]
  \label{lem:bkl-near-symmetric-gradient}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.gradCoord_gradCoord_symm,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.exists_symm_integral_norm_sq_sub_le,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.norm_sub_symmetrizeFirstTwo_le,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.integral_norm_sq_sub_symmetrizeFirstTwo_le}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:bkl-ibp-temperate,lem:bkl-energy-recursion}
  Partial counterpart of Lemma~3.2 of \cite{BizeulKlartagLehec2026}: the generalized implication for a
  supplied pair.  Let $\mu=m.\mathrm{tilted}(-V)$ be a probability measure with $V$ of temperate growth
  and all exponential moments, and $g,h$ tensor fields of temperate growth with
  $-\mathcal Lh=\alpha\nabla_0g$ (relation~(30)).  If $\lambda>0$ and $\lambda\le\alpha^2$ whenever
  $\alpha\ne0$ (the source's~(32) with $\lambda=\lambda_1$), there is a tensor field $\tilde T$ symmetric in
  its first two indices with $\norm{\nabla_0h-\tilde T}_2^2\le\chi/\lambda$, where
  $\chi=\norm{\nabla^2g-\alpha\nabla h}_2^2$; this is the square of~(38).  The normalization~(33) is not
  needed.  Since $S_2$ (the average over the transposition of the first two indices) is the nearest
  tensor symmetric in those indices, also $\norm{\nabla_0h-S_2\nabla_0h}_2^2\le\chi/\lambda$: Lemma~3.3 in the
  case $q=2$ (with constant $1$).  The source's iterates and the bound~(32) are not constructed here.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  For $\alpha\ne0$ take $\tilde T=\alpha^{-1}\nabla_0(\nabla g)$, symmetric since $\nabla^2g$ is a Hessian;
  then $\nabla_0h-\tilde T=-\alpha^{-1}(F-\int F\,d\mu)$ with $F=\nabla^2g-\alpha\nabla h$, and centering
  decreases the $L^2(\mu)$ norm.  For $\alpha=0$, $\mathcal Lh=0$ and~(15) give $\nabla h=0$ in $L^2(\mu)$, and
  $\tilde T=0$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The generator on the class $\mathcal A$]
  \label{lem:bkl-generator-temperate}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.exists_toLp_mem_closedWeightedOperator_domain_of_hasTemperateGrowth,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.exists_toLp_mem_adjointComp_domain_of_hasTemperateGrowth,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.centered_inverse_eq_toLp_of_hasTemperateGrowth}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:bkl-centered-inverse,lem:bkl-ibp-temperate}
  A step of Lemma~2.1 of \cite{BizeulKlartagLehec2026} relating its class $\mathcal A$ to the operator
  domain.  In the heat model $\nu=m.\mathrm{tilted}(-U)$ ($\nu$ log-concave with full-dimensional
  support) with $U$ of temperate growth and all exponential moments, the $L^2(\nu)$ class of every $f$
  of temperate growth lies in the domain of the closed unit gradient with $\bar T[f]=[\nabla f]$, and in
  the domain of $A=\bar T^\dagger\bar T$ with $A[f]=[-\mathcal Lf]$.  Consequently, if $\CPLit(\nu)\le C$
  and a centered $u$ of temperate growth solves $-\mathcal Lu=g$ pointwise, the centered operator-domain
  inverse of $[g]$ is $[u]$.  The existence of such a $u$ for $g\in\mathcal A_0$ (smoothness and growth of
  the inverse) is not proved.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Cutoffs $\chi_R f\in C_c^\infty$ converge to $f$ in the graph norm of $\bar T$ by dominated convergence;
  closedness gives the first statement.  Integration by parts~(15) against compactly supported smooth
  tests and the core property of $C_c^\infty$ give $\langle[-\mathcal Lf],x\rangle=\langle[\nabla f],\bar Tx\rangle$
  for $x\in D(\bar T)$, hence the second; uniqueness of the centered inverse gives the third.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Poincaré on $\mathcal A$ and the spectral bound of the iterates]
  \label{lem:bkl-poincare-temperate}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.integral_sq_sub_integral_le_of_hasTemperateGrowth,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.integral_norm_sq_sub_integral_le_of_hasTemperateGrowth,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.integral_norm_sq_gradCoord_le_of_hasTemperateGrowth,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.one_le_mul_sq_of_poisson,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.exists_symm_integral_norm_sq_sub_le_mul,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.integral_norm_sq_sub_symmetrizeFirstTwo_le_mul}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:bkl-generator-temperate,lem:bkl-near-symmetric-gradient}
  In the heat model $\nu=m.\mathrm{tilted}(-U)$ ($\nu$ log-concave with full-dimensional support, $U$ of
  temperate growth, all exponential moments) with $\CPLit(\nu)\le C$ (so $\lambda_1\ge1/C$ in the notation
  of \cite{BizeulKlartagLehec2026}): $\mathrm{Var}_\nu f\le C\int\abs{\nabla f}^2d\nu$ for $f$ of temperate
  growth, $\int\norm{h-\int h}^2\le C\int\norm{\nabla h}^2$ for tensor fields of temperate growth, and
  $\int\norm{\nabla h}^2\le C\int\norm{\mathcal Lh}^2$ (the energy bound corresponding to~(20)).  For a supplied pair with
  $-\mathcal Lh=\alpha\nabla_0g$~(30), $\norm{\nabla h}_2=\norm{\nabla_0g}_2$~(33) and $\nabla h\ne0$ in
  $L^2$, $1\le C\alpha^2$ (the source's~(32) when $C=\CPLit(\nu)$); and Lemma~3.2 holds with the bound
  $C\chi$ ($\lambda=1/C\le\lambda_1$ for $C>0$, equality for $C=\CPLit(\nu)$) and no further premise: $\nabla_0h$ lies within squared $L^2$ distance $C\chi$ of a field symmetric in its first two
  indices.  The source's iterates are not constructed.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The gap inequality of the closed gradient applied to $[f]$ (in its domain by
  \cref{lem:bkl-generator-temperate}); components in an orthonormal basis for tensors; integration by parts
  against $h-\int h$ and $ab\le\frac t2a^2+\frac1{2t}b^2$; then~(33).
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Approximate first eigenfunctions]
  \label{lem:bkl-approximate-eigenfunction}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.Semigroup.Generated.exists_approx_eigenvector_of_gap,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.exists_centered_approx_eigenfunction}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:bkl-centered-inverse}
  Let $T$ be closed and densely defined between real Hilbert spaces, $A=T^*T$, $\lambda>0$ with
  $\lambda\norm y^2\le\norm{Ty}^2$ on $D(T)\cap(\ker T)^\perp$.  If a unit $y$ there has
  $\norm{Ty}^2\le\lambda(1+\eta)$, the solution $z\in D(A)$ of $(A+\lambda)z=2\lambda y$ is orthogonal to
  $\ker T$, $\norm{y-z}\le\sqrt{\eta/8}$ and $\norm{Az-\lambda z}\le\lambda\sqrt{\eta/2}$.  In the heat
  model with $0<\CPLit(\nu)=C<\infty$, every $\eta>0$ admits a centered $f\in D(A)$ with $\norm f_2=1$ and
  $\norm{Af-C^{-1}f}_2\le C^{-1}\eta$.  This replaces the first eigenfunction~(19) of
  \cite{BizeulKlartagLehec2026} in the operator route to its Theorem~3.1; no compactness is used and no
  eigenfunction is asserted to exist.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The weak resolvent equation gives $Az=\lambda(2y-z)$; pairing $Az-\lambda z$ with $y-z$ and the gap at
  $y/2-z$ give $8\lambda\norm{y-z}^2\le\norm{Ty}^2-\lambda$.  In the heat model, optimality of $C$
  (\cref{lem:bkl-centered-inverse} and the converse gap comparison) yields a centered near-optimal $y$;
  normalize $z$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Weak Hessian, Bochner, (48) and Lemma~3.6 on the generator domain]
  \label{lem:bkl-generator-domain}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.compLp_innerSL_mem_closedWeightedOperator_domain,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.compLp_innerSL_closedWeightedOperator_comm,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.sum_norm_sq_closedWeightedOperator_add_le,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.tiltAverage_adjointComp_eq,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.taylorTensor_adjointComp_eq}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:bkl-bochner-temperate,lem:bkl-tilt-generator,lem:bkl-taylor-l2}
  In the heat model $\nu=m.\mathrm{tilted}(-U)$ with all exponential moments and $F\in D(A)$,
  $A=\bar T^*\bar T=-\mathcal L$: for $U$ of temperate growth, each component
  $\partial_iF=\langle e_i,\bar TF\rangle$ lies in $D(\bar T)$, the weak Hessian
  $\langle e_j,\bar T\partial_iF\rangle$ is symmetric, and under $\nabla^2U\ge\rho$,
  $\sum_i\norm{\bar T\partial_iF}^2+\rho\norm{\bar TF}^2\le\norm{AF}^2$ (the curvature form of~(16) used
  in Lemma~3.4 of \cite{BizeulKlartagLehec2026}); for $U\in C^1$, $F_{AF}(z)=\langle z,F_{\bar TF}(z)\rangle$
  (48) and $T_{d+1}(AF)=S_{d+1}T_d(\bar TF)$ (Lemma~3.6).  The iterates are not constructed here.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  $C_c^\infty$ is an operator core of $A$; the smooth identities pass to graph-norm limits, using the
  closedness of $\bar T$, $\norm{\bar Tu}^2=\langle Au,u\rangle$, the continuity of tilt averages and
  Taylor tensors on $L^2$, and convexity of $U$ from log-concavity for the Cauchy step.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The Poisson iterates in the generator domain]
  \label{lem:bkl-poisson-iterates}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.PoissonIterate.iter_mem_domain_and_mean_eq_zero,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.PoissonIterate.genField_iter_succ,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.PoissonIterate.energy_iter_succ,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.PoissonIterate.inv_le_scale_sq,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.PoissonIterate.energy_iter_zero_eq,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.PoissonIterate.normSq_gradField_gradField_iter,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.PoissonIterate.normSq_genField_iter_succ_add_defect_le,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.PoissonIterate.tendsto_energy_iter_zero,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.PoissonIterate.exists_symm_normSq_grad0_iter_succ_sub_le,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.PoissonIterate.normSq_grad0_iter_sub_symField_le,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.PoissonIterate.symmetrize_taylorField_grad0_iter_succ,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.PoissonIterate.norm_taylorField_grad0_iter_sq_le,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.PoissonIterate.sum_meanGradSq_iter_le}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:bkl-generator-domain,lem:bkl-centered-inverse,lem:bkl-partial-symmetrization}
  Let $\nu=m.\mathrm{tilted}(-U)$ be the heat model ($\nu$ log-concave with full-dimensional support,
  $m$ a Haar measure) with $\CPLit(\nu)\le C$, $C>0$, and let $h_1\in D(A)$ be centered.  The iterates
  $h_{i+1}=\alpha_{i+1}(-\mathcal L)^{-1}\nabla_0h_i$ of~(30)--(31) of \cite{BizeulKlartagLehec2026},
  built componentwise in $D(A)$, satisfy, for $U\in C^2$: (30), (33), (46), (35)--(36) and (32) with
  $\lambda=1/C$.  If moreover $U$ has temperate growth and $\nu$ has all exponential moments: (47);
  Lemma~3.4 under $\nabla^2U\ge a$ (any real $a$); Corollary~3.5 under $\nabla^2U\ge a$ with $a>0$;
  Lemma~3.2 with bound $C\chi_i$; Lemma~3.3 for every $2\le q\le i$ under $0<\alpha_j^2\le\beta/C$ on the
  relevant steps, with the constant $4q^4\max(1,\beta)^qC$; Corollary~3.7 for $d\ge1$; Lemma~3.8 under
  the Taylor bound~(29) (componentwise, Hilbert--Schmidt norm, $R\ge1$) and $0<\alpha_j^2\le\beta/C$ for
  all earlier steps, with the explicit constant $2^{20}\max(1,\beta)^2$; and Lemma~3.9 in the form
  $\sum_{k<N}p_k\le\norm{\mathcal Lh_1}^2+(\beta/C)\sum_{k<N-1}\abs{T_1(\nabla_0h_k)}^2$ under
  $\int\langle v,x\rangle^2d\nu\le\norm v^2$ and $\alpha_j^2\le\beta/C$ for $j+1<N$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  As in Section~3 of the source, with the identities of \cref{lem:bkl-generator-domain} in place of
  their versions on $\mathcal A$, the centered inverse of \cref{lem:bkl-centered-inverse}, and
  Lemma~2.2 (\cref{lem:bkl-partial-symmetrization}) for Lemma~3.8.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Taylor-coefficient criterion]
  \label{thm:bkl-taylor-criterion}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.KLS.RegularPotential.poincare_le_of_taylor,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.PoissonIterate.taylorCriterion,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.PoissonIterate.le_of_iterate_bounds,
        Solutions.ThmBklTaylorCriterion.certificate}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:bkl-poisson-iterates,lem:bkl-approximate-eigenfunction,lem:bkl-regular-law}
  This is Theorem~3.1 of \cite{BizeulKlartagLehec2026}.  There is a universal constant $K>0$ such that
  for every regular log-concave probability measure $\mu=e^{-V}dx/\int e^{-V}$ on $\R^n$ (BKL~(12)),
  centered with $\mathrm{Cov}\,\mu\le\mathrm{Id}$, and every $R\ge1$ with
  $\abs{T_df}\le R^d\norm f_{L^2(\mu)}$ for all $f\in L^2(\mu)$ and $d\ge1$ (29) (Hilbert--Schmidt
  norm),
  \[
    \CPLit(\mu)\le KR^2.
  \]
  The certificate states it in this source form and discharges every hypothesis in dimension zero
  and, for the standard Gaussian on $\R$ with $R=8$, in dimension one.
  The proof follows Section~3 of the source through the operator domain of the generator and an
  approximate first eigenfunction; only the value of $K$ differs.  Theorem~1.1 of the paper is not
  claimed.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  With $\lambda=1/\CPLit(\mu)$, an approximate eigenfunction $f$ with $\norm{Af-\lambda f}\le\lambda/10$
  (\cref{lem:bkl-approximate-eigenfunction}) starts the iterates of \cref{lem:bkl-poisson-iterates};
  then $v_1\in[\lambda,1.1\lambda]$, $\sum\chi_i\le1.21\lambda^2$, $\alpha_i^2\le3\lambda$ before the
  first index with $v_{N+1}<\lambda/2$, and the recursion~(63) with the dyadic summation gives a
  contradiction unless $\lambda R^2$ is bounded below by a universal constant.
\end{proof}


\begin{lemma}[Weak Hessian identification at gradient differentiability points]
  \label{lem:letwin-weak-hessian-identification}
