  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Probability.Stein.hessianMatrix_toEuclideanLin_of_differentiableAt_gradient,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.Stein.hessianMatrix_isHermitian_of_differentiableAt_gradient}
  \leanok
  Supporting result for Letwin \cite{Letwin2026QuadraticKLS}, Lemma~2.2, in its weak regular-class form.
  For $u\in C^1(\mathbb R^n)$ and a point $x$ where $\nabla u$ is differentiable, the coordinate Hessian
  represents $D(\nabla u)(x)$ under \texttt{Matrix.toEuclideanLin} and is Hermitian. Dimension zero is included.
  No global $C^2$ premise, moment-map premise or weak third-derivative tensor is required.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Reuse the pointwise coordinate and Schwarz-symmetry Hessian lemmas; identify
  the matrix through the standard orthonormal basis. For a Lipschitz gradient this
  identity gives the upper matrix-order bound at Rademacher points.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Bounded isotropic source: weak moment map]
  \label{lem:cek-bounded-moment-map}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.KLS.CEK.exists_momentMap_of_isotropic_of_ae_norm_le}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:cek-bounded-primal, lem:legendre-first-variation}
  The bounded-source existence case of Cordero--Erausquin--Klartag
  \cite{CorderoKlartag2015}, Theorem~2, used by Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-weak-regular-moment-potential}: for an isotropic probability law
  $\mu$ on a finite-dimensional real inner product space, $L\ge0$ and $\|y\|\le L$ $\mu$-a.e.,
  there is a finite convex $L$-Lipschitz $u$ with $\int e^{-u}=1$ and
  $(\nabla u)_\#(e^{-u}dx)=\mu$. Dimension zero is included. General unbounded/finite-mass existence
  and uniqueness are not claimed here.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The bounded normalized CEK primal and the integral first-variation/Euler--Lagrange
  identification give equality of integrals of bounded continuous tests, hence the pushforward identity.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Moment measure and essential continuity]
  \label{def:cek-moment-measure}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Probability.MomentMeasure.ofPotential,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.EssentiallyContinuous,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.ConvexPotential.essentiallyContinuous_iff,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.ConvexPotential.essentiallyContinuous_of_ne_top}
  \leanok
  \cite{CorderoKlartag2015}, Definitions~1 and~2. On a finite-dimensional real inner product space $E$ of dimension $n$
  with an additive Haar measure $dx$, the moment measure of $\psi\colon E\to(-\infty,+\infty]$ is the push-forward of
  $e^{-\psi}dx$ under $\nabla\psi$, the gradient of the real function $\psi$ (the density vanishes where
  $\psi=+\infty$). Convexity and $0<\int e^{-\psi}<\infty$ are separate hypotheses. The function $\psi$ is essentially
  continuous if it is lower semicontinuous and its set of discontinuity points has zero $\mathcal H^{n-1}$ measure. For
  convex lower semicontinuous $\psi$ the discontinuity set is $\dom\psi\setminus\operatorname{int}\dom\psi$, which is the
  source's equivalent form; finite convex potentials are essentially continuous.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Integrability of moment potentials and Fenchel--Moreau]
  \label{lem:cek-legendre-integrability}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.legendre_legendre_eq,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.MomentMeasure.addHaar_setOf_exists_line_mem_eq_zero,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.MomentMeasure.lintegral_indicator_abs_inner_gradient_lt_top,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.MomentMeasure.integrable_norm_ofPotential,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.MomentMeasure.lintegral_indicator_inner_gradient_sub_le,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.MomentMeasure.potentialIntegrable_legendre}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:cek-moment-measure}
  Supports of \cite{CorderoKlartag2015}, Theorem~2, for a convex lower semicontinuous $\psi\colon E\to(-\infty,+\infty]$
  with $0<\int e^{-\psi}<\infty$. Fenchel--Moreau: $\psi^{**}=\psi$. Lemma~4, first part: $\int|\langle\nabla
  e^{-\psi},\theta\rangle|<\infty$ for every unit $\theta$, and the moment measure has a finite first moment. Lemma~5,
  integrability part: for $\int e^{-\psi}=1$, an interior point $a$ of $\dom\psi$ and $C_0\ge0$ with
  $\psi\ge\psi(a)-C_0$, the integral of $e^{-\psi}(\langle\nabla\psi,x-a\rangle+C_0)$ is at most $n2^n+C_0$, not the
  source's sharp constant. Proposition~7 for $\int e^{-\psi}=1$: $\psi^*$ is integrable with respect to the moment
  measure of $\psi$. Lemma~3, projection step: almost every line parallel to a unit vector misses a given
  $\mathcal H^{n-1}$-null set. Not provided: the ``furthermore'' part of Lemma~4, the sharp inequality of Lemma~5,
  Lemma~3 as a separate statement, Lemma~6, Proposition~1, and Proposition~7 for unnormalized or
  non-lower-semicontinuous $\psi$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Fenchel--Moreau by Hahn--Banach separation of the closed epigraph. On each line the variation of the log-concave
  $e^{-\psi}$ is at most twice its maximum, and these maxima are integrable by linear growth of $\psi$. Homothety of
  $\psi$ about $a$ bounds the Lemma~5 integral, and $\psi^*(\nabla\psi(x))=\langle x,\nabla\psi(x)\rangle-\psi(x)$ at
  interior differentiability points gives Proposition~7.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[CEK Theorem~8: the conjugate maximizes the variational functional]
  \label{thm:cek-prekopa-maximality}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Probability.MomentMeasure.variationalFunctional_le_legendre,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.MomentMeasure.lintegral_le_exp_of_convexOn,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.MomentMeasure.lintegral_le_exp_integral_of_map_gradient_of_integrable,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.integral_abs_rightDeriv_mul_exp_neg_le,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.two_mul_exp_neg_sub_le_integral_abs}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:cek-moment-measure, lem:cek-legendre-integrability}
  \cite{CorderoKlartag2015}, Theorem~8, display~(18), in maximizer form. Let $\psi$ be convex and essentially continuous
  with $\int e^{-\psi}=1$ and moment measure $\mu$. Then $I_\mu(f)\le I_\mu(\psi^*)$ for every $\mu$-integrable
  $f\colon E\to(-\infty,+\infty]$, where $I_\mu(f)=\log\int e^{-f^*}-\int f\dd\mu$. For $f=\psi_1^*$ with $\psi_1$
  convex, lower semicontinuous, finite somewhere and $\psi_1^*$ $\mu$-integrable this is (18) for $\psi_0=\psi$. Display
  (18) is invariant under adding a constant to $\psi_0$; the general-mass form is not stated separately. The proof
  differs from the source: no Brenier map or McCann regularity is used.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Truncate the conjugate: $\psi_k=(\psi^*|_{B_{c+k}})^*$ are finite convex Lipschitz minorants increasing to $\psi$, equal
  to $\psi$ with the same gradient where $\norm{\nabla\psi}\le c+k$. The finite-potential Theorem~8 (Pr\'ekopa--Leindler)
  applies to each $\psi_k$. In the limit the remaining error is controlled by $\int|\langle\nabla\psi_k,\theta\rangle|
  e^{-\psi_k}$ off $\{\norm{\nabla\psi}\le c+k\}$. On almost every line essential continuity makes $\psi$ continuous, so
  the variation of $e^{-\psi}$ there is at least twice its maximum, which bounds the limit of the variations of
  $e^{-\psi_k}$. These line-variation estimates replace the integration by parts of Lemma~9.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[CEK Proposition~10: maximizers are essentially continuous]
  \label{prop:cek-essential-continuity}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.KLS.CEK.essentiallyContinuous_legendre_of_maximizer,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_convex_frontier_chart,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.hausdorffMeasure_mul_le_of_graph}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:cek-moment-measure}
  \cite{CorderoKlartag2015}, Proposition~10. Let $\mu$ be a finite measure with finite first moment, and $\varphi$ convex
  and $\mu$-integrable with $\int e^{-\varphi^*}=1$ and $I_\mu(f)\le I_\mu(\varphi)$ for every convex $\mu$-integrable
  $f$. Then $\varphi^*$ is essentially continuous. The source assumes instead that $\mu$ is the moment measure of the
  lower semicontinuous normalized $\psi=\varphi^*$. That hypothesis enters the proof only through the first moment
  (Lemma~4) and the integrability of $\varphi$ (Proposition~7), which follow from it by
  Lemma~\ref{lem:cek-legendre-integrability}.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Near a point of $\dom\psi\setminus\operatorname{int}\dom\psi$ the boundary is a Lipschitz graph, (23)--(24). The
  competitor $\max\{\varphi,\varphi+\varepsilon\langle\cdot,\theta\rangle-1\}$ of (25) changes $\int\varphi\dd\mu$ by
  $o(\varepsilon)$, while the mass of its conjugate grows by at least $c\,\varepsilon\,\mathcal H^{n-1}(A_k)$ on the
  segments above $A_k=\{\psi\le k\}$, (32)--(34). Maximality forces $\mathcal H^{n-1}(A_k)=0$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Uniqueness of moment potentials: finite and bounded-support forms]
  \label{lem:cek-finite-potential-uniqueness}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Probability.MomentMeasure.map_gradient_eq_iff_exists_eq_translate_unbounded,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.MomentMeasure.map_gradient_eq_iff_exists_eq_translate,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.CEK.exists_eq_translate_of_ofPotential_eq_of_ne_top,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.MomentMeasure.ofPotential_eq_iff_exists_eq_translate,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.MomentMeasure.exists_eq_coe_of_ofPotential_eq,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.MomentMeasure.exists_eq_translate_legendre_of_maximizer,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.MomentMeasure.exists_eq_translate_of_midpoint_le_of_lintegral_le_one,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.Line.eq_of_midpoint_le_of_integral_mul_eq}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:cek-moment-measure}
  Uniqueness half of \cite{CorderoKlartag2015}, Theorem~2, in restricted forms. A finite convex $\psi_1$ with
  $\int e^{-\psi_1}=1$ has the moment measure of a finite convex normalized $\psi_0$ if and only if it is a translate of
  $\psi_0$, for every moment measure. Finite convex potentials with the same finite nonzero moment measure are
  translates. For a probability measure with bounded support, every essentially continuous convex moment potential is
  finite and Lipschitz, and two of them are translates of each other (if and only if). Normalized maximizers of $I_\mu$
  have translated conjugates. The equality case of the midpoint Pr\'ekopa--Leindler inequality (Dubuc), in extended-real
  and one-dimensional forms, underlies these.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The finite-potential Theorem~8 at both potentials and the midpoint competitor force equality in
  Pr\'ekopa--Leindler, and Dubuc's characterization gives a translation. Bounded support makes an essentially continuous
  moment potential finite and Lipschitz, which reduces to the finite case.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[CEK Theorem~2: existence and uniqueness of moment measures]
  \label{thm:cek-moment-measures}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.KLS.CEK.exists_essentiallyContinuous_ofPotential_eq_unique,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.CEK.exists_eq_translate_of_ofPotential_eq_of_essentiallyContinuous,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.CEK.exists_ofPotential_eq_essentiallyContinuous,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.CEK.exists_ofPotential_eq_essentiallyContinuous_of_probability}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:cek-variational-maximum, prop:cek-essential-continuity, thm:cek-prekopa-maximality,
        lem:cek-legendre-integrability, lem:cek-finite-potential-uniqueness, lem:legendre-first-variation}
  \cite{CorderoKlartag2015}, Theorem~2 (= Letwin \cite{Letwin2026QuadraticKLS}, Lemma~2.1). Let $\mu$ be a finite nonzero
  Borel measure on a finite-dimensional real inner product space $E$ with finite first moment and barycenter $0$, not
  supported in a proper linear subspace. Then there is a convex essentially continuous $\psi\colon E\to(-\infty,+\infty]$
  whose moment measure is $\mu$, and every convex essentially continuous potential with moment measure $\mu$ is a
  translate $\psi(\cdot-y_0)$. The existence half also gives $\int e^{-\psi}=\mu(E)$ and a full-dimensional domain, for
  every additive Haar measure; the uniqueness half needs only that $\mu$ is finite and nonzero.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Existence: the conjugate of the Proposition~12 maximizer of Theorem~\ref{thm:cek-variational-maximum} has moment
  measure $\mu$ by the first variation (42)--(44) and is essentially continuous by
  Proposition~\ref{prop:cek-essential-continuity}; a constant fixes the mass. Uniqueness: after normalization each
  $\psi_i^*$ is $\mu$-integrable (Proposition~7) and maximizes $I_\mu$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:cek-prekopa-maximality}); two
  normalized maximizers have translated conjugates (Lemma~\ref{lem:cek-finite-potential-uniqueness}), and
  $\psi_i^{**}=\psi_i$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Weak regular-class moment-potential package]
  \label{lem:letwin-weak-regular-moment-potential}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_regular_moment_potential}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:cek-bounded-moment-map, lem:letwin-weak-hessian-identification}
  Weak supporting counterpart of Letwin \cite{Letwin2026QuadraticKLS}, Lemma~2.2 for the compact
  regular class: $n>0$, $K$ compact convex with nonempty interior, $W$ globally smooth,
  $W-\frac\kappa2\|\cdot\|^2$ convex for $\kappa>0$, and $\mu=1_K e^{-W}dx$ an isotropic
  probability. There is a finite convex Lipschitz moment potential $u$ with $\int e^{-u}=1$,
  $(\nabla u)_\#(e^{-u}dx)=\mu$, $u\in C^1$ and $\nabla u$ $16/\kappa$-Lipschitz. Almost everywhere,
  $H=\nabla^2u$ is positive definite, $\log\det H=-u+W(\nabla u)$ and
  $(16/\kappa)I-H$ is positive semidefinite.
  This does not establish global smoothness, everywhere positive definiteness, a gradient
  diffeomorphism, the integrated trace bound or Theorems~2.5/1.2.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Compactness bounds the source; the bounded CEK moment map, Caffarelli's $C^1$ result and the
  Lipschitz-gradient estimate give $u$. At Rademacher points the weak Hessian identification
  identifies the gradient derivative. The subgradient quadratic expansion and the
  viscosity/Alexandrov result give positive definiteness and the determinant identity. The derivative
  operator-norm bound transfers to the stated matrix-order cap. No $C^1$-to-$C^2$ promotion is used.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Inverse-Hessian integrability against bounded compact multipliers]
  \label{lem:letwin-weak-inverse-hessian-integrability}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.integrable_inv_hessianMatrix_mul_of_bounded_compact}
  \leanok
  Supporting result for the weak counterpart of Letwin \cite{Letwin2026QuadraticKLS}, equation~(2.2).
  Let $\nabla u$ be Lipschitz, $F$ continuous and $\det\nabla^2u=e^{F}$ almost everywhere. For every measurable,
  globally bounded, compactly supported $f$ and all indices $i,j$, the function $(\nabla^2u)^{-1}_{ij}f$ is
  integrable. No $C^1$ or $C^2$ hypothesis on $u$, positive definiteness or bound on $F$ is assumed; all finite
  dimensions are included.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The cofactors of $\nabla^2u$ are measurable and bounded by the Lipschitz constant of $\nabla u$; almost
  everywhere $(\nabla^2u)^{-1}_{ij}=e^{-F}\operatorname{adj}(\nabla^2u)_{ij}$, and $e^{-F}f$ is integrable because
  $e^{-F}$ is continuous and $f$ is bounded with compact support.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Weak inverse-Hessian generator identities]
  \label{lem:letwin-weak-generator-symmetry}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.integral_mul_generator_eq_neg_energy,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.integral_mul_generator_eq_neg_energy'}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:letwin-weak-inverse-hessian-integrability, lem:letwin-weak-hessian-identification}
  Weak $C^{1,1}$ counterpart of Letwin \cite{Letwin2026QuadraticKLS}, equation~(2.2). Let $u\in C^1$ with
  $\nabla u$ Lipschitz, $V\in C^1$, the exponent $F=-u+V(\nabla u)$ Lipschitz and
  $\det\nabla^2u=e^{F}$ almost everywhere, and set $Lf=\operatorname{tr}((\nabla^2u)^{-1}\nabla^2f)-DV(\nabla u)(\nabla f)$.
  For every $w\in C^1$ with Lipschitz gradient and every compactly supported $\eta\in C^2$,
  \[
    \int \eta\,(Lw)\,e^{-u}=-\int\langle(\nabla^2u)^{-1}\nabla\eta,\nabla w\rangle e^{-u}
    =\int w\,(L\eta)\,e^{-u}.
  \]
  Both identities hold in all finite dimensions. They are supports for
  Theorem~2.5; Theorems~2.5 and~1.2 are not proved here.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Apply the weak inverse-Hessian divergence identity to the compactly supported Lipschitz tests
  $\eta e^{-u}\partial_iw$ and $w e^{-u}\partial_i\eta$ (the latter compact through $\partial_i\eta$, without assuming
  $w$ compact). Integrability of every summand comes from
  Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-weak-inverse-hessian-integrability}. At almost every point the derivatives of the tests and
  of the exponent are computed with the pointwise Hessian identification of
  Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-weak-hessian-identification}, without promoting $C^1$ to $C^2$, and a finite inverse-matrix
  cancellation identifies the density with the energy plus $\eta\,(Lw)\,e^{-u}$; symmetry of $(\nabla^2u)^{-1}$ gives
  the second orientation.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Total coordinate Hessian entries: measurability and Lipschitz-gradient bound]
  \label{lem:hessian-entry-measurable-bounded}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Probability.Stein.measurable_hessianMatrix_entry,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.Stein.norm_hessianMatrix_entry_le_of_lipschitz_gradient}
  \leanok
  For every function $u$ on $\mathbb R^n$ and all indices $i,j$, the total coordinate Hessian entry
  $x\mapsto H_u(x)_{ij}$ (defined through the total Fr\'echet derivative, zero at nondifferentiability points) is
  measurable. If $\nabla u$ is $G$-Lipschitz, then $|H_u(x)_{ij}|\le G$ for every $x$. No $C^1$ or $C^2$ hypothesis, no
  positive dimension; this asserts no classical Hessian.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The entry is a continuous function of $D(\nabla u)(x)$ through the dual isometry and two evaluations, composed with the
  measurable total derivative; the bound is the operator-norm bound of $D(\nabla u)(x)$ by the Lipschitz constant.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Centered second difference: regularity and bounds]
  \label{lem:centered-difference-regularity-bounds}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.centeredDifference_contDiff_lipschitz_gradient,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.centeredDifference_nonneg_le}
  \leanok
  Let $w(x)=u(x+h)+u(x-h)-2u(x)$. If $u\in C^1$ with $G$-Lipschitz gradient, then $w\in C^1$ and $\nabla w$ is
  $4G$-Lipschitz. If $u$ is convex and $w\le D\|h\|^2$ everywhere, then $0\le w\le D\|h\|^2$ and $0\le D\|h\|^2$. Every $h$
  and every finite dimension; no sign of $D$ is assumed.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Translations are isometries, which gives $G+G+2G$; the lower bound is midpoint convexity at $x\pm h$, and the scalar
  bound is the two bounds at the origin.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Weak generator integrability]
  \label{lem:letwin-weak-generator-integrability}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.integrable_weak_generator_densities}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:hessian-entry-measurable-bounded, lem:letwin-weak-inverse-hessian-integrability}
  Let $u$ be continuous with Lipschitz gradient, $V\in C^1$, $F=-u+V(\nabla u)$ continuous with $\det\nabla^2u=e^F$ almost
  everywhere, $w$ continuous with Lipschitz gradient and $\eta\in C^2$ compactly supported, and
  $Lf=\operatorname{tr}((\nabla^2u)^{-1}\nabla^2f)-DV(\nabla u)(\nabla f)$. Then $\eta\,(Lw)\,e^{-u}$, $w\,(L\eta)\,e^{-u}$ and
  $|\eta+L\eta|\,e^{-u}$ are integrable. Neither compact support of $w$ nor $C^2$ regularity of $u$ or $w$ is assumed.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The Hessian entries of $w$ are bounded and measurable, those of $\eta$ continuous with compact support; each trace term is
  a bounded compactly supported multiplier against inverse-Hessian entries, and the drift terms are continuous with compact
  support through $\eta$ or $\nabla\eta$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Weighted Hessian-increment integrability]
  \label{lem:letwin-hessian-increment-integrability}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.integrable_hessian_increment_energy}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:hessian-entry-measurable-bounded}
  For $u$ continuous with Lipschitz gradient, $\eta$ continuous with compact support and every shift $h$, the density
  $\eta(x)\bigl(\|H_u(x+h)-H_u(x)\|_F^2+\|H_u(x-h)-H_u(x)\|_F^2\bigr)e^{-u(x)}$ is integrable, in every finite dimension.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The squared entry differences are measurable and bounded by $(2G)^2$; the weight $\eta e^{-u}$ is continuous with compact
  support.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Integrated energy of Hessian increments]
  \label{lem:letwin-hessian-increment-energy}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.integral_mul_hessian_increment_le}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:centered-difference-regularity-bounds, lem:letwin-hessian-increment-integrability,
        lem:letwin-weak-generator-integrability, lem:letwin-weak-generator-symmetry}
  Weak supporting estimate toward Letwin \cite{Letwin2026QuadraticKLS}, Theorem~2.5. Let $u\in C^1$ be convex with
  $G$-Lipschitz gradient, $V\in C^1$ convex, $-u+V(\nabla u)$ Lipschitz, $K>0$, and almost everywhere $\nabla^2u$ positive
  definite with $\log\det\nabla^2u=-u+V(\nabla u)$ and $\nabla^2u\le K$. For every nonnegative compactly supported
  $\eta\in C^2$ and every $h$ with $u(x+h)+u(x-h)-2u(x)\le D\|h\|^2$ for all $x$,
  \[
    \int\eta\bigl(\|H_u(x+h)-H_u(x)\|_F^2+\|H_u(x-h)-H_u(x)\|_F^2\bigr)e^{-u}
    \le 2K^2\,(D\|h\|^2)\int|\eta+L\eta|\,e^{-u},
  \]
  in every finite dimension. It does not produce weak third derivatives or the
  integrated trace bound, and does not prove Theorems~2.5 or~1.2.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Multiply the existing pointwise coercivity of Hessian increments by $\eta e^{-u}\ge0$ and integrate, using only proved
  integrability; the two weak generator identities give equal integrals for $\eta Lw$ and $wL\eta$, and $0\le w\le D\|h\|^2$
  bounds $w(\eta+L\eta)$ by $D\|h\|^2|\eta+L\eta|$. The determinant identity $\det\nabla^2u=e^{-u+V(\nabla u)}$ follows from the
  positive determinant and the log-determinant data. No $C^1$ to $C^2$ promotion is used.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Uniform smooth majorant for translated compact cutoffs]
  \label{lem:uniform-translated-cutoff-majorant}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_smooth_compact_translate_majorant}
  \leanok
  Let $u$ and $\chi$ be continuous on $\mathbb R^n$, $\chi$ compactly supported, and $v\in\mathbb R^n$. There is one
  nonnegative compactly supported $\eta\in C^\infty$ with $\chi(x+tv)^2e^{u(x)}\le\eta(x)$ for every $x$ and every $|t|\le1$.
  Every finite dimension and every direction are included; no energy or envelope premise is assumed.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The support of $\chi$ lies in a ball of radius $r$; on the closed ball of radius $R=|r|+\|v\|+1$ the functions $\chi^2$ and
  $e^u$ are bounded, and a smooth bump equal to $1$ on that ball, multiplied by the product of the two bounds, dominates all
  translates.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Hessian entries: local and cutoff $L^2$]
  \label{lem:hessian-entry-local-l2}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Probability.Stein.memLp_hessianMatrix_entry_of_isCompact,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.Stein.memLp_cutoff_mul_hessianMatrix_entry}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:hessian-entry-measurable-bounded}
  If $\nabla u$ is $G$-Lipschitz, every total coordinate Hessian entry $H_u(\cdot)_{ij}$ is in $L^2$ of Lebesgue measure
  restricted to any compact set, and $\chi\,H_u(\cdot)_{ij}$ is in $L^2(\mathbb R^n)$ for every continuous compactly supported
  $\chi$. No differentiability premise; all finite dimensions; Lebesgue measure on the whole space is not treated as finite.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The entry is measurable and bounded by $G$; a compact set has finite measure. The cutoff product is bounded on the compact
  support of $\chi$ and vanishes outside it, so it is the indicator extension of a function in the restricted $L^2$ space.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Cutoff difference energy of Hessian entries]
  \label{lem:letwin-cutoff-hessian-difference-energy}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_hessian_cutoff_sq_difference_bound}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:uniform-translated-cutoff-majorant, lem:hessian-entry-local-l2, lem:letwin-hessian-increment-energy,
        lem:letwin-hessian-increment-integrability}
  Let $u\in C^1$ be convex with $G$-Lipschitz gradient, $V\in C^1$ convex, $-u+V(\nabla u)$ $L_F$-Lipschitz, $K>0$, and almost
  everywhere $\nabla^2u$ positive definite with $\log\det\nabla^2u=-u+V(\nabla u)$ and $\nabla^2u\le K$; assume the global
  centered-difference bound $u(x+h)+u(x-h)-2u(x)\le D\|h\|^2$ for all $x,h$. Let $\chi\in C^1$ be compactly supported,
  $v\in\mathbb R^n$ and $i,j$ arbitrary indices. There is $C\ge0$ such that for every $t\ne0$ with $|t|\le1$,
  \[
    \int\bigl(\chi(x+tv)H_u(x+tv)_{ij}-\chi(x)H_u(x)_{ij}\bigr)^2\,dx\le C\,t^2 .
  \]
  No test function or single shift is an input: the compactly supported test used in the proof is produced, and the global
  translation bound $C\,t^2$ is a conclusion, while the global centered-difference bound is an original input. Every finite
  dimension is included.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Split the difference as $\chi(x+tv)(H(x+tv)-H(x))+H(x)(\chi(x+tv)-\chi(x))$. The first square is dominated through the uniform
  majorant by the weighted increment density, whose integral is at most $A t^2$ by Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-hessian-increment-energy}
  at $h=tv$. The second is at most $G^2L^2\|v\|^2t^2$ on the compact support of the majorant and zero outside it. Translation
  invariance and the cutoff $L^2$ statement justify integrability.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Weak derivatives of the Hessian entries (third-derivative tensor)]
  \label{lem:letwin-weak-third-derivative}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_weak_third_derivative}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:letwin-cutoff-hessian-difference-energy, lem:hessian-entry-local-l2}
  Weak supporting step toward Letwin \cite{Letwin2026QuadraticKLS}, Theorem~2.5. Let $u\in C^1$ be convex with $G$-Lipschitz
  gradient, $V\in C^1$ convex, $-u+V(\nabla u)$ $L_F$-Lipschitz, $K>0$, and almost everywhere $\nabla^2u$ positive definite with
  $\log\det\nabla^2u=-u+V(\nabla u)$ and $\nabla^2u\le K$, and assume the global centered-difference bound
  $u(x+h)+u(x-h)-2u(x)\le D\|h\|^2$ for all $x,h$. Then there is a tensor $T$ such that for all coordinate indices $k,i,j$ the
  entry $H_u(\cdot)_{ij}$ has the weak derivative $T(\cdot,k,i,j)$ along $e_k$, and $T(\cdot,k,i,j)$ is $L^2$ on every compact
  set. It has no cutoff or test-function input, and every finite dimension is included.
  No given tensor, $H^1$ or cutoff square-difference-energy conclusion is assumed. The result is the original weak, locally
  $L^2$ tensor support; it does not establish global $C^2$ or smooth regularity, the trace bound or Theorems~2.5 and~1.2.
  The tensor $T(\cdot,k,i,j)=\partial_k H_u(\cdot)_{ij}$ consists of weak first derivatives of the Hessian entries, i.e.\ the
  weak third-derivative tensor of $u$. This generic theorem keeps its centered-difference and exponent Lipschitz inputs; for
  the regular source class they are discharged in Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-regular-moment-weak-tensor}.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  For each $k,i,j$ apply the cutoff gluing criterion for weak derivatives with the compact-local $L^2$ statement; for each
  compactly supported $C^1$ cutoff, the difference-quotient criterion applies to the cutoff product with the constant from
  Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-cutoff-hessian-difference-energy}. Choosing the resulting derivatives for all indices gives $T$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Centered second differences of the regular moment potential]
  \label{lem:moment-map-second-difference}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.secondDifference_le_of_map_gradient}
  \leanok
  Let $u$ be convex, Lipschitz and $C^1$ with $\int e^{-u}=1$, let $K$ be compact and convex, $V\in C^1$ with
  $V-\frac\kappa2\|\cdot\|^2$ convex for $\kappa>0$, and suppose $(\nabla u)_\#(e^{-u}dx)=1_Ke^{-V}dx$. Then
  $u(x+h)+u(x-h)-2u(x)\le\frac4\kappa\|h\|^2$ for all $x,h$, in every finite dimension. No difference bound is assumed.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  This is the step of the proof of the $16/\kappa$-Lipschitz gradient theorem preceding co-coercivity: the maximum
  principle for penalized second differences of mollified potentials. That theorem is derived from this
  statement.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Lipschitz exponent from a Lipschitz potential and gradient]
  \label{lem:lipschitz-gradient-exponent}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_lipschitzWith_neg_add_comp_gradient}
  \leanok
  If $u$ is $L$-Lipschitz, its total gradient is $G$-Lipschitz and $V\in C^1$, then $-u+V(\nabla u)$ is globally Lipschitz
  with some constant $L_F$. Every finite dimension; $L$ and $G$ may vanish. No differentiability of $u$, convexity, equation,
  bounded range or derivative bound is assumed.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The gradient takes values in the closed ball of radius $L$, on which the locally Lipschitz $V$ is $C$-Lipschitz; hence
  $V\circ\nabla u$ is $CG$-Lipschitz and $L_F=L+CG$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Regular moment potential: weak third-derivative tensor]
  \label{lem:letwin-regular-moment-weak-tensor}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_regular_moment_weak_tensor}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:letwin-weak-regular-moment-potential, lem:moment-map-second-difference, lem:lipschitz-gradient-exponent,
        lem:letwin-weak-third-derivative}
  Under the hypotheses of Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-weak-regular-moment-potential} ($n>0$, $K$ compact convex with nonempty
  interior, $W$ smooth with $W-\frac\kappa2\|\cdot\|^2$ convex for $\kappa>0$, $\mu=1_Ke^{-W}dx$ an isotropic probability),
  there are $u$, $L$, $L_F$ and a tensor $T$ such that $u$ has every conclusion of that lemma, $-u+W(\nabla u)$ is
  $L_F$-Lipschitz, $u(x+h)+u(x-h)-2u(x)\le\frac4\kappa\|h\|^2$ for all $x,h$, and for all coordinate indices $k,i,j$ the
  Hessian entry $H_u(\cdot)_{ij}$ has the weak derivative $T(\cdot,k,i,j)$ along $e_k$, which is $L^2$ on every compact set.
  No difference, exponent, tensor, $H^1$, trace or main-bound premise is assumed. This is weak, locally $L^2$ Hessian
  regularity; it does not establish global $C^2$ or smooth regularity, a gradient diffeomorphism, the trace bound or
  Theorems~2.5 and~1.2.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Take $u$ from Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-weak-regular-moment-potential}; the pushforward identity gives the centered-difference
  bound by Lemma~\ref{lem:moment-map-second-difference} and the exponent constant by
  Lemma~\ref{lem:lipschitz-gradient-exponent}; $W$ is convex as the sum of $W-\frac\kappa2\|\cdot\|^2$ and a convex quadratic.
  Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-weak-third-derivative} with cap $16/\kappa$ produces $T$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Weak product rule for global $L^2$ factors]
  \label{lem:weak-product-global-l2}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.HasWeakDerivAlong.mul_of_memLp_two}
  \leanok
  If $f$ and $g$ have weak derivatives $df$ and $dg$ along a direction $v$ of $\mathbb R^n$, and $f,df,g,dg\in L^2(\mathbb R^n)$,
  then $fg\in L^1(\mathbb R^n)$ has the weak derivative $df\,g+f\,dg$ along $v$. Every finite dimension and every direction,
  including zero, are included. Only the one direction $v$ is involved: membership of $fg$ in the full-gradient space $W^{1,1}$ is
  not asserted, nor is an $L^2$ bound for the derivative. No boundedness, smoothness or $C^1/C^2$ regularity of the rough factors
  and no finite volume is assumed.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Mollify $g$ by a shrinking normalized bump. The mollifications are smooth, converge to $g$ in $L^2$, and their derivative is the
  mollification of $dg$. Test the weak derivative of $f$ against $\psi g_m$ and pass to the limit in the three $L^2$ pairings.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Weak product rule for bounded factors with locally $L^2$ derivatives]
  \label{lem:weak-product-local-bounded}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.HasWeakDerivAlong.mul_of_locally_memLp_two}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:weak-product-global-l2}
  If $f,g\in L^\infty(\mathbb R^n)$ have weak derivatives $df,dg$ along $v$ that are in $L^2$ on every compact set, then the uncut
  product $fg$ has the weak derivative $df\,g+f\,dg$ along $v$, and this derivative is $L^2$ on every compact set. Every finite
  dimension and every direction are included; $f,df,g,dg$ are not assumed globally $L^2$, and Lebesgue measure on the whole space
  is not treated as finite. Applied to the regular moment potential, this gives the weak derivatives
  $T_{kij}H_{ab}+H_{ij}T_{kab}$ of products of bounded Hessian entries, where $T_{kij}$ is the weak derivative of $H_{ij}$ along
  $e_k$; it is not determinant or inverse calculus, Hessian evolution or the trace bound.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  For a test $\psi$ choose a smooth bump $\chi$ equal to one on a neighbourhood of its support. The cutoff factors $\chi f$ and
  $\chi g$ have weak derivatives with global $L^2$ data by H\"older's inequality, so Lemma~\ref{lem:weak-product-global-l2}
  applies; on the support of $\psi$ the cutoff is one with vanishing derivative, which removes it from the tested identity.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Finite weak linear combinations]
  \label{lem:weak-finite-linear-combination}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.hasWeakDerivAlong_finsetSum_mul}
  \leanok
  Let $s$ be a finite set (possibly empty) and $c_i\in\mathbb R$. If each $f_i$ has the weak derivative $df_i$ along a fixed
  direction $v$ of $\mathbb R^n$ and each $df_i$ is $L^2$ on every compact set, then $\sum_{i\in s}c_if_i$ has the weak derivative
  $\sum_{i\in s}c_i\,df_i$ along $v$, and this derivative is $L^2$ on every compact set. Every finite dimension and direction are
  included; no global $L^2$, boundedness or $C^1/C^2$ regularity of the $f_i$ is assumed.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Local integrability and compact-local $L^2$ are preserved by scalar multiples and finite sums; the tested identity is the finite
  sum of the component identities.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Finite products of bounded weak factors]
  \label{lem:weak-finite-product}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.hasWeakDerivAlong_finsetProd_of_locally_memLp_two}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:weak-product-local-bounded}
  If $f_i\in L^\infty(\mathbb R^n)$, $i\in s$ finite (possibly empty), have weak derivatives $df_i$ along $v$ that are $L^2$ on every
  compact set, then $\prod_{i\in s}f_i$ has the weak derivative $\sum_{i\in s}df_i\prod_{j\in s\setminus\{i\}}f_j$ along $v$, which
  is $L^2$ on every compact set. The empty product $1$ has derivative $0$. Signed factors and every dimension and direction are
  included; no global $L^2$, $C^1/C^2$ regularity of the rough factors or finite volume is assumed.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Induction on $s$ with the bounded binary product rule; a finite product of $L^\infty$ factors is $L^\infty$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Weak derivative of the determinant of a bounded matrix field]
  \label{lem:weak-determinant}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.hasWeakDerivAlong_det_of_locally_memLp_two}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:weak-finite-product, lem:weak-finite-linear-combination}
  Let $\iota$ be a finite index type (possibly empty) and $A:\mathbb R^d\to\mathbb R^{\iota\times\iota}$ have $L^\infty$ entries with
  weak derivatives $DA_{ij}$ along $v$ that are $L^2$ on every compact set. Then $\det A$ has the weak derivative
  \[
    \sum_{\sigma}\operatorname{sign}\sigma\sum_{i}DA_{\sigma(i)i}\prod_{j\ne i}A_{\sigma(j)j}
  \]
  along $v$, which is $L^2$ on every compact set. The ambient dimension $d$ is independent of $\iota$; no symmetry, positivity or
  invertibility of $A$ is assumed. This is not inverse or log-determinant calculus.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Apply Lemma~\ref{lem:weak-finite-product} to each permutation product and Lemma~\ref{lem:weak-finite-linear-combination} to the
  signed sum, then use the Leibniz formula for the determinant.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Weak derivative of the trace quadratic $\operatorname{tr}(BABA)$]
  \label{lem:weak-trace-quadratic}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.hasWeakDerivAlong_trace_quadratic_of_locally_memLp_two}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:weak-product-local-bounded, lem:weak-finite-linear-combination}
  For every constant real $n\times n$ matrix $B$ and every $A:\mathbb R^d\to\mathbb R^{n\times n}$ as in
  Lemma~\ref{lem:weak-determinant}, $\operatorname{tr}(BABA)$ has the weak derivative $2\operatorname{tr}(BAB\,DA)$ along $v$, which
  is $L^2$ on every compact set. The dimensions $d$ and $n$ are independent and may vanish; $B$ need not be symmetric, positive
  semidefinite or invertible. Applied to the regular moment potential, this is the first weak derivative
  $2\operatorname{tr}(BHBT_k)$ of $\operatorname{tr}(BHBH)$; it is not the generator identity (2.8)/(2.10), Hessian evolution or
  the integrated trace estimate of Theorem~2.5.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Expand the trace as a finite sum of products of two entries with coefficients $B_{ab}B_{ce}$, apply the bounded binary product rule
  to each and Lemma~\ref{lem:weak-finite-linear-combination} to the sum, and identify the result with finite matrix identities.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Determinant polynomial density and the outer Jacobi differential]
  \label{lem:determinant-permutation-differential}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.fderiv_det_eq_sum_permutation}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:weak-determinant, lem:letwin-monge-ampere-first}
  For every finite index type $\iota$ (possibly empty) and all real matrices $A,N\in\mathbb R^{\iota\times\iota}$, singular or not,
  \[
    D(\det)(A)[N]=\sum_{\sigma}\operatorname{sign}\sigma\sum_{i}N_{\sigma(i)i}\prod_{j\ne i}A_{\sigma(j)j}.
  \]
  Hence the signed permutation-polynomial density of Lemma~\ref{lem:weak-determinant} is the outer differential of the determinant
  applied to the weak derivative field. For the regular moment potential, where the Hessian is almost
  everywhere positive definite, Jacobi's formula of Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-monge-ampere-first} identifies this density with
  $\det(H)\operatorname{tr}(H^{-1}T_k)$ almost everywhere. By itself this is not weak $\log\det$ or inverse calculus, the exponent chain rule or
  the weak first Monge--Amp\`ere derivative~(2.4).
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The derivative of $t\mapsto\det(A+tN)$ at $0$ is computed entrywise from the Leibniz formula; the existing directional-derivative
  identity for the differentiable map $\det$ equates it with $D(\det)(A)[N]$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The exponential is Lipschitz on a half-line]
  \label{lem:exp-lipschitz-half-line}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.lipschitzOnWith_exp_Iic}
  \leanok
  For every real $C$, negative values included, $\exp$ is $e^{C}$-Lipschitz on $(-\infty,C]$. No lower bound is assumed.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Mean-value inequality on the convex half-line: $|\exp'|=\exp\le e^{C}$ there.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Weak directional derivatives under almost-everywhere changes]
  \label{lem:weak-derivative-ae-congr}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.HasWeakDerivAlong.congr_ae}
  \leanok
  On every finite-dimensional real inner-product space and along every direction $v$: if $f$ has the weak derivative $g$ along $v$,
  $f=f'$ almost everywhere and $g=g'$ almost everywhere, then $f'$ has the weak derivative $g'$ along $v$; the local integrability of
  $f'$ and $g'$ is produced, not assumed.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Local integrability is invariant under a.e.\ equality, and both sides of the testing identity are integrals that change only on null
  sets.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Almost-everywhere derivative of the Monge--Amp\`ere exponent]
  \label{lem:ae-mongeampere-exponent}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.ae_fderiv_mongeAmpere_exponent}
  \leanok
  Let $u\in C^1(\mathbb R^n)$ have a Lipschitz gradient and let $V\in C^1$. For almost every $x$ and every coordinate $j$,
  \[
    \partial_j\bigl(-u+V(\nabla u)\bigr)(x)=-\partial_ju(x)+\sum_k H_u(x)_{jk}\,\partial_kV(\nabla u(x)).
  \]
  No convexity, $C^2$ or smooth-Hessian regularity, or Monge--Amp\`ere equation is assumed.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The Lipschitz gradient is differentiable almost everywhere (Rademacher); at such points the chain rule applies to $V\circ\nabla u$ and
  the Hessian matrix is the derivative of the gradient.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Regular moment potential: first weak Monge--Amp\`ere derivative]
  \label{lem:letwin-regular-moment-weak-first-ma}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_regular_moment_weak_first_derivative}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:letwin-regular-moment-weak-tensor, lem:weak-determinant, lem:determinant-permutation-differential,
        lem:letwin-monge-ampere-first, lem:ae-mongeampere-exponent, lem:exp-lipschitz-half-line,
        lem:weak-derivative-ae-congr}
  Under the hypotheses of Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-regular-moment-weak-tensor} there are $u$, $L$, $L_F$ and $T$ with every conclusion
  of that lemma (the normalization and the gradient pushforward are part of the conclusion, not assumed) and in addition, with
  $H=H_u$ and $T_k=(T(\cdot,k,i,j))_{ij}$:
  \begin{itemize}
    \item $\exp(-u+W(\nabla u))$ is globally Lipschitz;
    \item for every coordinate $k$, $\det H$ has the weak derivative $\det(H)\operatorname{tr}(H^{-1}T_k)$ along $e_k$, which is $L^2$
      on every compact set;
    \item $\log\det H$ has the weak derivative $\operatorname{tr}(H^{-1}T_k)$ along $e_k$;
    \item almost everywhere,
      $\operatorname{tr}(H^{-1}T_k)=\partial_k\bigl(-u+W(\nabla u)\bigr)=-\partial_ku+\sum_aH_{ka}\,\partial_aW(\nabla u)$.
  \end{itemize}
  The last identity is the weak form, for the source law, of Letwin \cite{Letwin2026QuadraticKLS}, display~(2.4). The $\log\det$
  identification holds for this source law, whose Hessian is almost everywhere positive definite with $\log\det H=-u+W(\nabla u)$;
  it is not unrestricted $\log\det$ calculus for rough matrix fields. No $C^1$-to-$C^2$ step, given tensor, given equation or trace
  estimate is used. The twice-differentiated Lemma~2.4 is not obtained: its second evolution identity~(2.6) and the
  positivity/trace integration are not supplied, and neither are Theorems~2.5 and~1.2.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Coercivity of the normalized convex Lipschitz $u$ bounds the exponent $F=-u+W(\nabla u)$ above by some $C$; since $F$ is
  $L_F$-Lipschitz, $e^F$ is Lipschitz by Lemma~\ref{lem:exp-lipschitz-half-line}. The Lipschitz function $e^F$ has the weak
  derivative $e^F\partial_kF$ (its almost-everywhere classical derivative), which Lemma~\ref{lem:weak-derivative-ae-congr} transfers to
  $\det H=e^F$ almost everywhere. Uniqueness of weak derivatives identifies the signed permutation density of
  Lemma~\ref{lem:weak-determinant} with $e^F\partial_kF$; by Lemma~\ref{lem:determinant-permutation-differential} and Jacobi's formula
  (Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-monge-ampere-first}) that density is $\det(H)\operatorname{tr}(H^{-1}T_k)$ almost everywhere, and cancelling
  $\det H>0$ gives $\operatorname{tr}(H^{-1}T_k)=\partial_kF$. Lemma~\ref{lem:ae-mongeampere-exponent} gives the explicit right-hand
  side. Finally $\log\det H=F$ almost everywhere, so the weak derivative of the Lipschitz $F$ transfers to $\log\det H$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Weak negative exponential of a Lipschitz exponent]
  \label{lem:weak-exp-neg-lipschitz}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.hasWeakDerivAlong_exp_neg_of_lipschitz}
  \leanok
  On every finite-dimensional real inner-product space, for every direction $v$ (including $0$) and every globally Lipschitz $F$,
  $e^{-F}$ has the weak derivative $-e^{-F}\,DF[v]$ along $v$, and this density is $L^2$ on every compact set. No lower bound on $F$,
  global bound on $e^{-F}$, $C^1$ regularity of $F$ or finite whole-space measure is assumed.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  By Rademacher's theorem the density is the almost-everywhere chain rule. For a compactly supported $C^1$ test $\psi$, a $C^1$
  compactly supported $\chi$ equal to $1$ near $F(\operatorname{tsupport}\psi)$ makes $q(t)=\chi(t)e^{-t}$ globally Lipschitz; the
  Lipschitz weak rule for $q\circ F$ transfers to $e^{-F}$ near $\operatorname{tsupport}\psi$. On a compact set $e^{-F}$ is bounded
  and $|DF[v]|\le L\|v\|$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Weak product rule for compact-locally bounded factors]
  \label{lem:weak-product-locally-bounded}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.HasWeakDerivAlong.mul_of_locally_bounded}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:weak-product-local-bounded}
  Along one fixed direction $v$ (including $0$) in $\mathbb R^n$ (including $n=0$): if $f,g$ have weak derivatives $df,dg$ along $v$,
  $f$ and $g$ are essentially bounded on every compact set, and $df,dg$ are $L^2$ on every compact set, then $fg$ has the weak
  derivative $df\,g+f\,dg$ along $v$, which is $L^2$ on every compact set. No global bound, global $L^2$, continuity or $C^1/C^2$
  regularity of the factors is assumed.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  On a compact set the restricted measure is finite, so the density is locally $L^2$. For a test $\psi$, a $C^1$ cutoff $\chi$ equal to
  $1$ near $\operatorname{tsupport}\psi$ makes $\chi f$ and $\chi g$ globally essentially bounded; Lemma~\ref{lem:weak-product-local-bounded}
  applies to them, and $\chi=1$, $D\chi=0$ on $\operatorname{tsupport}\psi$ removes the cutoffs.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Weak derivative of a matrix inverse]
  \label{lem:weak-matrix-inverse-derivative}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.ae_weak_matrix_inverse_derivative}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:weak-product-locally-bounded, lem:weak-finite-linear-combination, lem:weak-derivative-ae-congr}
  Let $d,m\ge0$ be independent, $v$ any direction, and $A,R:\mathbb R^d\to\mathbb R^{m\times m}$ have entrywise weak derivatives
  $DA,DR$ along $v$, with $A,R$ essentially bounded and $DA,DR$ in $L^2$ on every compact set. If $RA=\mathrm{Id}$ almost everywhere,
  then $DR=-R\,DA\,R$ almost everywhere. No symmetry, positivity, $C^1$ regularity or global bound is assumed.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Lemmas~\ref{lem:weak-product-locally-bounded} and~\ref{lem:weak-finite-linear-combination} give the weak derivative
  $(DR\,A+R\,DA)_{ij}$ of $(RA)_{ij}$; since $RA=\mathrm{Id}$ almost everywhere, uniqueness of weak derivatives makes it vanish almost
  everywhere. There $AR=\mathrm{Id}$ for square matrices, and right multiplication by $R$ gives the formula.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Bounded Hessian adjugate and its weak derivative]
  \label{lem:hessian-adjugate-weak-derivative}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.memLp_hessian_adjugate_top,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_weak_hessian_adjugate_derivative}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:hessian-entry-measurable-bounded, lem:weak-determinant}
  If $\nabla u$ is Lipschitz, every adjugate entry of $\nabla^2u$ is globally essentially bounded. Separately, if the Hessian entries
  are globally essentially bounded and a supplied tensor $T$ gives every Hessian entry the weak coordinate derivative $T(\cdot,k,i,j)$,
  $L^2$ on compact sets, then every adjugate entry has some weak coordinate derivative, $L^2$ on compact sets; this second statement
  does not assume that $\nabla u$ is Lipschitz. No log-det exponent or Monge--Amp\`ere equation is assumed in either statement.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Each adjugate entry is a continuous function of the total derivative $D(\nabla u)(x)$, which is measurable and, for Lipschitz
  $\nabla u$, lies in a closed ball; this is the argument of Lemma~\ref{lem:hessian-entry-measurable-bounded}, applied to the adjugate
  through the Piola cofactor identities (with no exponent or Monge--Amp\`ere hypothesis). For the second statement,
  each adjugate entry is the determinant of a row-updated Hessian whose replaced row is constant, and Lemma~\ref{lem:weak-determinant}
  applies.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Intrinsic weak inverse-Hessian derivative]
  \label{lem:weak-inverse-hessian-of-logdet}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.hasWeakDerivAlong_inverse_hessian_of_logdet}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:weak-exp-neg-lipschitz, lem:hessian-adjugate-weak-derivative, lem:weak-product-locally-bounded,
        lem:weak-derivative-ae-congr, lem:weak-matrix-inverse-derivative}
  Let $\nabla u$ and $F$ be Lipschitz, $\nabla^2u$ positive definite with $\log\det\nabla^2u=F$ almost everywhere, and let a supplied
  tensor $T$ give every Hessian entry the weak coordinate derivative $T(\cdot,k,i,j)$, $L^2$ on compact sets. Then for all $k,i,j$,
  with $H=\nabla^2u$ and $T_k=(T(\cdot,k,a,b))_{ab}$, $(H^{-1})_{ij}$ has the weak derivative $-(H^{-1}T_kH^{-1})_{ij}$ along $e_k$,
  $L^2$ on compact sets, and $(H^{-1})_{ij}$ is essentially bounded on compact sets. This is the weak form of Letwin
  \cite{Letwin2026QuadraticKLS}, display~(2.5), not the second evolution identity~(2.6).
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Almost everywhere $H^{-1}=e^{-F}\operatorname{adj}H$. Lemmas~\ref{lem:weak-exp-neg-lipschitz},
  \ref{lem:hessian-adjugate-weak-derivative} and~\ref{lem:weak-product-locally-bounded} give a weak derivative of $e^{-F}\operatorname{adj}H$,
  which Lemma~\ref{lem:weak-derivative-ae-congr} transfers to $H^{-1}$; Lemma~\ref{lem:weak-matrix-inverse-derivative} identifies its
  density.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Regular moment potential: weak inverse-Hessian derivative]
  \label{lem:letwin-regular-moment-weak-inverse}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_regular_moment_weak_inverse}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:letwin-regular-moment-weak-first-ma, lem:weak-inverse-hessian-of-logdet}
  Under the hypotheses of Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-regular-moment-weak-tensor} there are $u$, $L$, $L_F$ and $T$ with every conclusion of
  Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-regular-moment-weak-first-ma} (the same witnesses, normalization and gradient pushforward) and, for all
  $k,i,j$, the weak derivative $-(H^{-1}T_kH^{-1})_{ij}$ of $(H^{-1})_{ij}$ along $e_k$, $L^2$ on compact sets, together with
  compact-local essential bounds on $(H^{-1})_{ij}$. No inverse formula, given inverse derivative, $C^2$ Hessian, global inverse bound,
  weighted $T^2$ bound or trace estimate is assumed as an input; the output includes the explicit weak inverse formula. The second
  evolution identity~(2.6), the energy comparison~(2.14) and
  Theorems~2.5 and~1.2 are not supplied.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Take the witnesses of Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-regular-moment-weak-first-ma}; its Lipschitz exponent, positive definiteness, log-det
  equation and weak tensor are the inputs of Lemma~\ref{lem:weak-inverse-hessian-of-logdet}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Common-support smooth approximation of two weak derivatives]
  \label{lem:weak-smooth-approximation}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_contDiff_compact_weak_deriv_approximation}
  \leanok
  Let $n\ge0$ and $v,w\in\mathbb R^n$ (zero allowed). If $\eta$ is compactly supported and has weak derivatives $a$ along $v$ and $b$
  along $w$ that are compactly supported and in $L^2$, then there are one compact set $S$ containing the supports of $\eta,a,b$ and
  smooth compactly supported $\theta_m$ such that $\theta_m$, $D\theta_m[v]$ and $D\theta_m[w]$ are supported in $S$, both directional
  derivatives are in $L^2$, and $D\theta_m[v]\to a$, $D\theta_m[w]\to b$ in $L^2$. No $C^1$ regularity or global $L^2$ of $\eta$ is
  assumed.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Mollify $\eta$ with normalized bumps of radius $1/(m+1)$. The directional derivatives of the mollification are the mollifications of
  $a$ and $b$, which converge in $L^2$; all supports lie in the union of the closed $1$-thickenings of the three supports.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Rough compact curl testing]
  \label{lem:weak-curl-rough-test}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.integral_weakDeriv_mul_eq_swap}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:weak-smooth-approximation}
  Let $p$ and $q$ be weak derivatives of the same $f$ along $v$ and $w$, each $L^2$ on every compact set, and let $\eta,a,b$ be as in
  Lemma~\ref{lem:weak-smooth-approximation}. Then $\int p\,b=\int q\,a$. Every finite dimension and both directions (zero included);
  no $C^1$ or $C^2$ regularity of $\eta$, global $L^2$ of $p,q$, fourth derivative of $f$ or the identity itself is assumed. This is a
  support for the weak form of Letwin \cite{Letwin2026QuadraticKLS}, display~(2.6), not that identity.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  For a smooth compactly supported $\chi$, testing the weak identity for $p$ with $D_w\chi$ and the one for $q$ with $D_v\chi$ gives
  $\int p\,D_w\chi=-\int f\,D_vD_w\chi=-\int f\,D_wD_v\chi=\int q\,D_v\chi$, by symmetry of second derivatives of $\chi$. Apply this to
  $\theta_m$ from Lemma~\ref{lem:weak-smooth-approximation}; restricting $p,q$ to the common compact set gives $L^2$ factors, and the
  $L^2$ convergence of the derivatives passes the identity to the limit.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Weak generator identity for $C^1$ multipliers]
  \label{lem:letwin-weak-generator-c1}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.integral_mul_generator_eq_neg_energy_of_contDiff_one}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:letwin-weak-generator-symmetry}
  Under the hypotheses of Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-weak-generator-symmetry}, its first (unprimed) identity
  $\int\eta\,(Lw)\,e^{-u}=-\int\langle(\nabla^2u)^{-1}\nabla\eta,\nabla w\rangle e^{-u}$ holds for every compactly supported
  $\eta\in C^1$. The primed form, with $\eta$ inside $L$, still requires $\eta\in C^2$. It applies to the
  height cutoffs of Letwin's Lemma~A.4 but does not construct them.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The proof of the unprimed identity uses only first derivatives of $\eta$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Mean Hessian at $C^{1,1}$]
  \label{lem:letwin-mean-hessian-c11}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.integral_inner_fderiv_gradient_tilted_eq_integral_fderiv_mul}
  \leanok
  Weak-route analytic part of Letwin \cite{Letwin2026QuadraticKLS}, Lemma~2.3. Let $\varphi\in C^1$ be convex with Lipschitz gradient,
  $e^{-\varphi}$ a probability density, $\nu=e^{-\varphi}\,dx$ and $\nabla\varphi\in L^2(\nu)$. Then for all $u,v$,
  $\int\langle D(\nabla\varphi)u,v\rangle\,d\nu=\int\partial_u\varphi\,\partial_v\varphi\,d\nu$, where $D(\nabla\varphi)$ is the
  almost-everywhere derivative of the Lipschitz map $\nabla\varphi$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Integration by parts against cutoffs for the Lipschitz function $\langle\nabla\varphi,v\rangle$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Regular moment potential: mean Hessian]
  \label{lem:letwin-regular-moment-weak-mean-hessian}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_regular_moment_weak_inverse_mean_hessian}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:letwin-regular-moment-weak-inverse, lem:letwin-mean-hessian-c11, lem:hessian-entry-measurable-bounded}
  Under the hypotheses of Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-regular-moment-weak-tensor} there are $u$, $L$, $L_F$ and $T$ with every conclusion of
  Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-regular-moment-weak-inverse} (the same witnesses) and, for all $i,j$, the Hessian entry $H_{ij}$ is integrable
  for $\nu=e^{-u}\,dx$ with $\int H_{ij}\,d\nu=\delta_{ij}$. This is Lemma~2.3 for the moment potential of Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-weak-regular-moment-potential} under
  $C^1$/Lipschitz-gradient hypotheses. The full smoothness and diffeomorphism properties of Lemma~2.2 are not asserted. No analytic
  premise, $C^2$ regularity, given mean or trace estimate is added.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The Hessian entries are bounded by the Lipschitz constant of $\nabla u$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:hessian-entry-measurable-bounded}) and $\nu$
  is a probability measure. Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-mean-hessian-c11} gives $\int H_{ij}\,d\nu=\int\partial_iu\,\partial_ju\,d\nu$, which
  is $\delta_{ij}$ because the gradient pushforward of $\nu$ is the isotropic source law.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Bounded all-coordinate common-support approximation]
  \label{lem:weak-bounded-all-coordinate-approximation}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_contDiff_compact_bounded_all_weak_deriv_approximation}
  \leanok
  Let $\eta$ on $\mathbb R^n$ (every $n$, including $n=0$) be locally integrable, compactly supported and bounded by $C$, and for every
  coordinate $i$ let $a_i$ be a weak derivative of $\eta$ along $e_i$ that is compactly supported and in $L^2$. Then there are one
  compact set $S$ containing the supports of $\eta$ and of every $a_i$, and one sequence $\theta_m$ of smooth compactly supported
  functions supported in $S$ with $|\theta_m|\le 3C$, such that every $\partial_i\theta_m$ is supported in $S$, lies in $L^2$ and
  converges to $a_i$ in $L^2$, and $\theta_m\to\eta$ almost everywhere along the full sequence. No continuity or $C^1$ regularity of
  $\eta$ is assumed. Support for the bounded test extension of the weak form of Letwin \cite{Letwin2026QuadraticKLS}, display~(2.6);
  Lemma~\ref{lem:weak-smooth-approximation} keeps its two-direction statement.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Mollify $\eta$ with normalized bumps of radius $1/(m+1)$. The normalized convolution of a bounded function is bounded by $3C$; the
  coordinate derivatives of the mollification are the mollifications of the $a_i$, which converge in $L^2$; the full-sequence almost
  everywhere convergence is the Lebesgue differentiation theorem for normalized bumps of bounded radius ratio; all supports lie in
  the union of closed $1$-thickenings of the finitely many supports.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Bounded weak-test extension of a divergence identity]
  \label{lem:weak-bounded-test-extension}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.integral_flux_eq_neg_forcing_of_bounded_weak_test}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:weak-bounded-all-coordinate-approximation}
  Let $J_i$ be $L^2$ on every compact set and $F$ locally integrable on $\mathbb R^n$ (every $n$, including $n=0$), with
  $\int\sum_iJ_i\,\partial_i\psi=-\int F\psi$ for every compactly supported $\psi\in C^1$. Then for every $\eta$ and $a_i$ as in
  Lemma~\ref{lem:weak-bounded-all-coordinate-approximation}, every $J_ia_i$ and $F\eta$ are integrable and
  $\int\sum_iJ_ia_i=-\int F\eta$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Apply the identity to the smooth sequence of Lemma~\ref{lem:weak-bounded-all-coordinate-approximation}. The flux terms converge by
  $L^2$ pairing on the common compact set; the forcing terms converge by dominated convergence with the integrable bound
  $C\,|F|$ on that set and the almost everywhere convergence.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Weak Piola row divergence of the inverse Hessian]
  \label{lem:weak-inverse-hessian-row-divergence}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.ae_inverse_hessian_row_divergence_of_weak_derivative}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:letwin-weak-inverse-hessian-integrability}
  Let $u\in C^1$ have Lipschitz gradient, $F$ be Lipschitz and $\det\nabla^2u=e^{F}$ almost everywhere, and let $T$ be a given tensor
  such that every $(\nabla^2u)^{-1}_{ij}$ has the weak derivative $-((\nabla^2u)^{-1}T_k(\nabla^2u)^{-1})_{ij}$ along $e_k$, $L^2$ on
  compact sets, and is essentially bounded on compact sets. Then for every row $b$ the function
  $\sum_a(\nabla^2u)^{-1}_{ba}\partial_aF$ is $L^2$ on compact sets and almost everywhere
  $\sum_a-((\nabla^2u)^{-1}T_a(\nabla^2u)^{-1})_{ba}=-\sum_a(\nabla^2u)^{-1}_{ba}\partial_aF$. Every finite dimension.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Test both sides against compactly supported smooth $\psi$: the weak inverse derivatives give the left side, and the weak Piola
  divergence identity of the inverse Hessian with $\det\nabla^2u=e^F$ gives the right side; both are locally integrable.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Weak derivative of a coordinate derivative composed with the gradient]
  \label{lem:weak-gradient-derivative-composition}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_lipschitzWith_weak_fderiv_comp_gradient}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:ae-mongeampere-exponent}
  Let $u\in C^1$ be Lipschitz with Lipschitz gradient and $W\in C^2$. For every coordinate $a$, the function $\partial_aW(\nabla u)$ is
  Lipschitz and, for every $j$, has the weak derivative $\sum_bH_{jb}\,\partial_{ba}W(\nabla u)$ along $e_j$, $L^2$ on compact sets,
  where $H$ is the Hessian of $u$. Every finite dimension.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  A Lipschitz function has its almost-everywhere derivative as weak derivative; the almost-everywhere chain formula of
  Lemma~\ref{lem:ae-mongeampere-exponent} identifies it with the stated density.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Weak derivative of the first Monge--Amp\`ere right-hand side]
  \label{lem:weak-monge-ampere-first-rhs}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.hasWeakDerivAlong_mongeAmpere_first_rhs}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:weak-gradient-derivative-composition, lem:weak-product-locally-bounded, lem:weak-finite-linear-combination, lem:hessian-entry-measurable-bounded}
  Let $u\in C^1$ be Lipschitz with Lipschitz gradient, $W\in C^2$, and $T$ a given tensor such that every Hessian entry $H_{ij}$ has
  the weak derivative $T_{kij}$ along $e_k$, $L^2$ on compact sets. Then for all $i,j$ the right-hand side
  $-\partial_iu+\sum_aH_{ia}\,\partial_aW(\nabla u)$ of the first Monge--Amp\`ere identity has the weak derivative
  $-H_{ji}+\sum_a\big(T_{jia}\,\partial_aW(\nabla u)+H_{ia}\sum_bH_{jb}\,\partial_{ba}W(\nabla u)\big)$ along $e_j$, $L^2$ on compact
  sets. Every finite dimension.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The weak derivative of $\partial_iu$ is the Hessian entry; each product uses Lemma~\ref{lem:weak-product-locally-bounded} with the
  compact-local bounds of the Hessian entries and of the continuous composition; finite linearity assembles the sum.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Local integrability of the Hessian flux and trace forcing]
  \label{lem:weak-hessian-flux-forcing-local}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.localLp_hessian_flux_trace_forcing}
  \leanok
  Let $R=(\nabla^2u)^{-1}$, $D_{kba}=-(RT_kR)_{ba}$ and $J_{ija}=\sum_bR_{ba}T_{bij}$. If $T$ and $D$ are $L^2$ on compact sets, $R$ is
  essentially bounded on compact sets and every $Q_{ij}$ is locally integrable, then every $J_{ija}$ is $L^2$ on compact sets and
  $C_{ij}=Q_{ij}-\sum_{a,b}T_{iab}D_{jba}+\sum_bT_{bij}\sum_aD_{aba}$ is locally integrable.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Products of an $L^2$ and an $L^\infty$ factor are $L^2$, and products of two $L^2$ factors are integrable, on every compact set.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Unweighted weak Hessian flux identity]
  \label{lem:weak-hessian-flux-identity}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.integral_weak_hessian_flux_eq_trace_derivative}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:weak-hessian-flux-forcing-local, lem:weak-curl-rough-test}
  Let $u\in C^1$ have Lipschitz gradient, $T$ be a weak Hessian tensor $L^2$ on compact sets, the inverse-Hessian entries have the weak
  derivatives $D$ of Lemma~\ref{lem:weak-hessian-flux-forcing-local} with compact-local bounds, and $Q_{ij}$ be a weak derivative of
  $\operatorname{tr}(RT_i)$ along $e_j$, $L^2$ on compact sets. Then, with $J$ and $C$ as in
  Lemma~\ref{lem:weak-hessian-flux-forcing-local}, $\int\sum_aJ_{ija}\,\partial_a\psi=-\int C_{ij}\,\psi$ for every compactly
  supported $\psi\in C^1$. Every finite dimension.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  For each pair of indices apply the rough curl identity of Lemma~\ref{lem:weak-curl-rough-test} to the Hessian entries with the
  compact rough test $\psi R_{ba}$, use the almost-everywhere symmetry of the weak third derivatives, and sum the resulting pair
  identities with the trace identity by a finite integral balance.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Weighted weak Hessian evolution]
  \label{lem:letwin-weak-hessian-evolution}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.integral_exp_neg_hessian_flux_eq_curvature_gram}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:weak-monge-ampere-first-rhs, lem:weak-hessian-flux-identity, lem:weak-inverse-hessian-row-divergence, lem:ae-mongeampere-exponent}
  Weak form of Letwin \cite{Letwin2026QuadraticKLS}, display~(2.6). Let $u\in C^1$ be Lipschitz with Lipschitz gradient, $W\in C^2$,
  $-u+W(\nabla u)$ Lipschitz with $\det\nabla^2u=e^{-u+W(\nabla u)}$ almost everywhere, $T$ a weak Hessian tensor $L^2$ on compact
  sets, the inverse entries with weak derivatives $-(RT_kR)_{ij}$ ($L^2$ on compact sets) and compact-local bounds, and almost
  everywhere $\operatorname{tr}(RT_i)=-\partial_iu+\sum_aH_{ia}\,\partial_aW(\nabla u)$. Then $J$ is $L^2$ on compact sets,
  $E_{ij}=-H_{ij}+(H\,\nabla^2W(\nabla u)\,H)_{ij}+\operatorname{tr}(RT_iRT_j)$ and $e^{-u}E_{ij}$ are locally integrable, and
  $\int e^{-u}\sum_aJ_{ija}\,\partial_a\psi=-\int e^{-u}E_{ij}\,\psi$ for every compactly supported $\psi\in C^1$. Every finite
  dimension. No $C^2$ regularity of $u$, global inverse bound, global $L^2$ bound of $T$ or energy estimate is assumed. The
  positivity statement of Letwin's Lemma~2.4 is not stated here.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Lemma~\ref{lem:weak-monge-ampere-first-rhs} and the first identity give the weak trace derivative needed by
  Lemma~\ref{lem:weak-hessian-flux-identity}. The row divergence of Lemma~\ref{lem:weak-inverse-hessian-row-divergence} with the
  almost-everywhere exponent formula and the symmetry of the weak third derivatives identify the forcing minus the drift
  $\sum_aJ_{ija}\partial_au$ with $E_{ij}$; multiplying the test by $e^{-u}\in C^1$ gives the weighted identity.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Weighted weak Hessian evolution for bounded tests]
  \label{lem:letwin-weak-hessian-evolution-bounded-test}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.integral_exp_neg_hessian_flux_eq_curvature_gram_of_bounded_weak_test}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:letwin-weak-hessian-evolution, lem:weak-bounded-test-extension}
  Under the hypotheses of Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-weak-hessian-evolution}, for every $\eta$ and $a_b$ as in
  Lemma~\ref{lem:weak-bounded-all-coordinate-approximation}, every $e^{-u}J_{ijb}a_b$ and $e^{-u}E_{ij}\eta$ are integrable and
  $\int e^{-u}\sum_bJ_{ijb}a_b=-\int e^{-u}E_{ij}\,\eta$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Apply Lemma~\ref{lem:weak-bounded-test-extension} to the weighted flux $e^{-u}J$, which is $L^2$ on compact sets, and the locally
  integrable forcing $e^{-u}E$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Regular moment potential: weak Hessian evolution]
  \label{lem:letwin-regular-moment-weak-hessian-evolution}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_regular_moment_weak_hessian_evolution}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:letwin-regular-moment-weak-mean-hessian, lem:weak-monge-ampere-first-rhs, lem:weak-hessian-flux-identity, lem:letwin-weak-hessian-evolution}
  Under the hypotheses of Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-regular-moment-weak-tensor} there are $u$, $L$, $L_F$ and $T$ with every conclusion of
  Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-regular-moment-weak-mean-hessian} (the same witnesses) and, in addition, for all $i,j$: the weak derivative of
  $\operatorname{tr}((\nabla^2u)^{-1}T_i)$ along $e_j$ given by Lemma~\ref{lem:weak-monge-ampere-first-rhs}, $L^2$ on compact sets; the
  unweighted identity of Lemma~\ref{lem:weak-hessian-flux-identity}; and the weighted weak~(2.6) of
  Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-weak-hessian-evolution}. No analytic premise is added.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The source fields supply every hypothesis of the intrinsic results; the first weak identity transports the right-hand-side weak
  derivative to the trace, and $\det\nabla^2u=e^{-u+W(\nabla u)}$ follows from the log-determinant field.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Regular moment potential: bounded Hessian testing]
  \label{lem:letwin-regular-moment-bounded-hessian-testing}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_regular_moment_bounded_weak_hessian_testing}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:letwin-regular-moment-weak-hessian-evolution, lem:letwin-weak-hessian-evolution-bounded-test, lem:hessian-entry-local-l2, lem:hessian-entry-measurable-bounded}
  Under the hypotheses of Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-regular-moment-weak-tensor} there are $u$, $L$, $L_F$ and $T$ with every conclusion of
  Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-regular-moment-weak-hessian-evolution} (the same witnesses) and, for every compactly supported $\psi\in C^1$ and
  all $l,k,i,j$, both sides are integrable and
  $\int e^{-u}\sum_bJ_{ijb}\,(\psi\,T_{blk}+\partial_b\psi\,H_{lk})=-\int e^{-u}E_{ij}\,\psi H_{lk}$: the bounded $H^1$ tests
  $\psi H_{lk}$. No global $L^2$ bound of $T$ and no new source premise is used.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  $\psi H_{lk}$ is bounded and compactly supported with the compact $L^2$ coordinate weak derivatives $\psi T_{blk}+\partial_b\psi H_{lk}$;
  apply Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-weak-hessian-evolution-bounded-test} at the same witness.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Weak inverse-Hessian height cutoffs, real parameter]
  \label{lem:letwin-weak-cutoff-real-family}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_scaled_weak_inverse_hessian_cutoffs}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:letwin-cutoff-profile, lem:letwin-a4-supports, lem:letwin-weak-inverse-hessian-integrability, lem:letwin-weak-generator-c1, lem:hessian-entry-measurable-bounded}
  $C^1$ counterpart of Letwin \cite{Letwin2026QuadraticKLS}, Lemma~A.4, for a weak source. Let $u\in C^1$ on $\mathbb R^n$ (every $n$,
  including $n=0$) be convex and Lipschitz with Lipschitz gradient and $\int e^{-u}=1$, let $V\in C^1$ with $-u+V(\nabla u)$ Lipschitz,
  and let $\det\nabla^2u=e^{-u+V(\nabla u)}$ with $\nabla^2u$ positive definite almost everywhere. Then there are $c$ and $C\ge0$ with
  $u-c\ge1$ such that $\chi_R=\eta(2-(u-c)/R)$, $\eta$ the smooth transition, satisfies for every real $R\ge1$: $\chi_R\in C^1$ is
  compactly supported with Lipschitz gradient and values in $[0,1]$; $\chi_R$ is monotone in $R$ with pointwise limit $1$; the literal
  generator $L\chi_R=\operatorname{tr}((\nabla^2u)^{-1}\nabla^2\chi_R)-DV(\nabla u)(\nabla\chi_R)$ is integrable for $\nu=e^{-u}dx$ with
  $\int|L\chi_R|\,d\nu\le C/R$, and this defect tends to $0$. The drift bound, sublevel coercivity, majorant and defect bound are
  derived, not assumed. This is not the $C^\infty$ cutoff family of the smooth source class (Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-a4-cutoffs}).
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Convexity and normalization give a minimum and compact sublevels. The composition $\eta(2-(u-c)/R)$ is $C^1$ with Lipschitz gradient,
  and its almost-everywhere Hessian is computed from the Hessian of $u$. The unprimed $C^1$ weak generator identity bounds the
  drift term, and the integrability of inverse-Hessian entries against bounded compact functions controls the trace term; integrating
  the profile majorant over the height range gives the $C/R$ bound.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Weak inverse-Hessian height cutoffs, natural index]
  \label{lem:letwin-weak-cutoff-natural-family}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_weak_inverse_hessian_cutoffs}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:letwin-weak-cutoff-real-family}
  Under the hypotheses of Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-weak-cutoff-real-family}, the same family at $R=m+1$: for every $m\in\mathbb N$,
  $\chi_m$ has the same regularity and range, is monotone in $m$ with pointwise limit $1$, $L\chi_m$ is integrable for $\nu$ with
  $\int|L\chi_m|\,d\nu\le C/(m+1)$, and this defect tends to $0$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Specialize Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-weak-cutoff-real-family} to $R=m+1\ge1$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Regular moment potential: weak inverse-Hessian cutoffs]
  \label{lem:letwin-regular-moment-weak-cutoffs}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_regular_moment_weak_cutoffs}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:letwin-regular-moment-bounded-hessian-testing, lem:letwin-weak-cutoff-real-family}
  Under the hypotheses of Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-regular-moment-weak-tensor} there are $u$, $L$, $L_F$ and $T$ with every conclusion of
  Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-regular-moment-bounded-hessian-testing} (the same witnesses), and one common $c$ and $C$ with the real-parameter
  cutoff family of Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-weak-cutoff-real-family} for this $u$ and the source potential $W$. No source premise is added.
  Hence the bounded weak~(2.6) testing applies to $\chi_R H_{lk}$ at this witness for every $R\ge1$ and all $l,k,i,j$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The log-determinant field gives $\det\nabla^2u=e^{-u+W(\nabla u)}$ almost everywhere; the remaining hypotheses of
  Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-weak-cutoff-real-family} are source fields.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Compact flux identity against bounded weak tests]
  \label{lem:weak-compact-flux-bounded-test}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.integral_compact_flux_eq_neg_forcing_of_global_bounded_weak_test,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.HasWeakDerivAlong.compact_mul_data,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.memLp_mul_of_hasCompactSupport,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.memLp_top_restrict_of_continuous,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.locallyIntegrable_mul_of_compact_L2,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.locallyIntegrable_of_compact_local_memLp_two}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:weak-bounded-test-extension}
  Let $J_i$ be compactly supported fluxes in $L^2$ on every compact set and $F$ a compactly supported locally integrable forcing on
  $\mathbb R^n$ with $\int\sum_i J_i\,\partial_i\psi=-\int F\psi$ for every compactly supported $C^1$ test $\psi$. Then every globally bounded,
  locally integrable $\eta$ whose coordinate weak derivatives $a_i$ are in $L^2$ on every compact set satisfies: each $J_ia_i$ and $F\eta$
  is integrable and $\int\sum_iJ_ia_i=-\int F\eta$, in every dimension. The proof uses products with compactly
  supported $L^2$ functions, compact $C^1$ multiplier data, compact $L^\infty$ bounds of continuous functions and local integrability
  from compact $L^2$ bounds.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Multiply by a compactly supported $C^1$ cutoff equal to $1$ on the supports, approximate the bounded weak test by smooth compactly
  supported functions with a uniform bound and $L^2$ convergence of the derivatives, and pass to the limit in both pairings.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Bounded weak trace-quadratic data and matrix products]
  \label{lem:weak-bounded-trace-quadratic}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.bounded_weak_matrix_product_entry,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.bounded_trace_quadratic_weak_derivative_data,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.trace_mul_mul_mul_eq_sum}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:weak-trace-quadratic}
  Let $A$ be a matrix field with measurable entries bounded by $M$ and coordinate weak derivatives $DA_k$ in $L^2$ on every compact set.
  For every real matrix $B$, $\operatorname{tr}(BABA)$ is globally bounded and locally integrable, with weak coordinate derivatives
  $2\operatorname{tr}(BABDA_k)$ in $L^2$ on every compact set. For every real matrix $S$ and every direction $v$ along which the entries of
  $H$ (bounded by $M$) have weak derivatives $DH$ that are $L^2$ on compact sets, every entry of $SHS$ is bounded explicitly and has weak
  derivative the corresponding entry of $S\,DH\,S$, again $L^2$ on compact sets. All finite dimensions.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Expand the trace and the matrix products as finite sums of products of bounded entries and apply the bounded weak product rule.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Rough weak tests against the $C^1$ inverse-Hessian cutoff generator]
  \label{lem:letwin-weak-rough-cutoff-generator}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.integral_rough_mul_generator_eq_neg_energy_of_contDiff_one}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:weak-compact-flux-bounded-test, lem:letwin-weak-generator-c1}
  Let $u$ be $C^1$ with Lipschitz gradient, $V$ be $C^1$, the exponent $-u+V(\nabla u)$ be globally Lipschitz,
  $\det\nabla^2u=e^{-u+V(\nabla u)}$ almost everywhere, and the inverse Hessian entries be essentially bounded on every compact set.
  Let $\chi$ be a compactly supported $C^1$ cutoff with Lipschitz gradient whose inverse-Hessian generator
  $L\chi=\operatorname{tr}((\nabla^2u)^{-1}\nabla^2\chi)-DV(\nabla u)(\nabla\chi)$, weighted by $e^{-u}$, is integrable. Then for every
  globally bounded, locally integrable $\eta$ with coordinate weak derivatives $a_i$ in $L^2$ on every compact set, both pairings below are
  integrable and $\int\eta\,L\chi\,e^{-u}=-\int\sum_{ij}(\nabla^2u)^{-1}_{ij}a_i\,\partial_j\chi\,e^{-u}$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The $C^1$ weak generator identity gives the compact flux identity for the weighted inverse-Hessian flux of $\chi$; apply
  Lemma~\ref{lem:weak-compact-flux-bounded-test}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Weak trace contraction (2.8)/(2.10)]
  \label{lem:letwin-weak-trace-contraction}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.integral_trace_quadratic_flux_eq_neg_density}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:weak-bounded-trace-quadratic, lem:letwin-weak-hessian-evolution-bounded-test}
  Weak counterpart of the trace contraction (2.8)/(2.10) in the proof of Letwin \cite{Letwin2026QuadraticKLS}, Theorem~2.5. Let $H$,
  $R$ and $E$ be matrix fields, $T$ a tensor field and $\rho$ a continuous weight. Assume $H$ has globally essentially bounded entries, the
  entries of $T$ and of the fluxes $J_{ija}=\sum_bR_{ba}T_{bij}$ are $L^2$ on every compact set, $E$ is locally integrable, and
  $\int\rho\sum_aJ_{ija}(\psi T_{alk}+\partial_a\psi\,H_{lk})=-\int\rho E_{ij}\psi H_{lk}$ for every compactly supported $C^1$ test $\psi$.
  Then for every real matrix $B$ the density $g=2\operatorname{tr}(BHBE)+2\sum_{ij}R_{ij}\operatorname{tr}(BT_iBT_j)$ is locally
  integrable and, for every compactly supported $C^1$ test $\psi$, the contracted flux
  $\sum_j(\sum_iR_{ij}\,2\operatorname{tr}(BHBT_i))\,\partial_j\psi$ weighted by $\rho$ integrates to $-\int\rho g\psi$, with all terms
  integrable. All finite dimensions; no Hessian, potential or source premise.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Contract the tested identities with the entries of $BHB$ and collect the finite sums.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Pointwise positivity of the trace density]
  \label{lem:letwin-trace-density-positivity}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.trace_forcing_add_twice_energy_nonneg}
  \leanok
  For positive semidefinite matrices $B$, $H$, $R$, $W$ and symmetric matrices $T_i$, with the trace Gram matrix
  $(G_R)_{ij}=\operatorname{tr}(RT_iRT_j)$, the quantity
  $2\operatorname{tr}(BHB(-H+HWH+G_R))+2\sum_{ij}R_{ij}\operatorname{tr}(BT_iBT_j)+2\operatorname{tr}(BHBH)$ is nonnegative.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The terms $\operatorname{tr}(BHBHWH)$, $\operatorname{tr}(BHBG_R)$ and $\sum_{ij}R_{ij}\operatorname{tr}(BT_iBT_j)$ are traces of products
  of positive semidefinite matrices, hence nonnegative, and the remaining terms cancel.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Trace-Gram comparison (2.12)]
  \label{lem:letwin-trace-gram-comparison}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.letwin_tensor_comparison_trace_gram,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.four_tensor_trace_le_density_add_twice_energy}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:letwin-support-identities, lem:letwin-trace-density-positivity}
  For a positive definite $H$, a symmetric $B$ and a fully symmetric three-tensor $T$,
  $\sum_{ij}(H^{-1})_{ij}\operatorname{tr}(BT_iBT_j)\le\operatorname{tr}(BHBG)$ with $G_{ij}=\operatorname{tr}(H^{-1}T_iH^{-1}T_j)$; this is
