  Letwin (2.12) in trace-Gram coordinates. For positive semidefinite $B$ and $W$ and positive definite $H$, the quantity
  $D=\sum_{ij}(H^{-1})_{ij}\operatorname{tr}(BT_iBT_j)$ satisfies $0\le D$ and $4D\le g+2\operatorname{tr}(BHBH)$, where
  $g=2\operatorname{tr}(BHB(-H+HWH+G))+2D$ is the weak trace density.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Diagonalize $H$ and apply the coordinate weight identity to the symmetric tensor; the second statement combines this with
  Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-trace-density-positivity}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Regular moment potential: rough cutoff testing]
  \label{lem:letwin-regular-moment-bounded-weak-cutoff-testing}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_regular_moment_bounded_weak_cutoff_testing}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:letwin-regular-moment-weak-cutoffs, lem:letwin-weak-rough-cutoff-generator}
  For the source law, under the hypotheses of Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-weak-regular-moment-potential} ($n>0$, isotropic $1_Ke^{-W}dx$ with $K$ compact and convex with
  nonempty interior, $W$ smooth and $\kappa$-uniformly convex), the same $u$, $L$, $L_F$, $T$ and the same common cutoff family as in
  Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-regular-moment-weak-cutoffs} (with all of its conclusions) satisfy, for every $r\ge1$ and every globally bounded,
  locally integrable $\eta$ with coordinate weak derivatives in $L^2$ on every compact set, the rough inverse-Hessian cutoff-generator
  identity of Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-weak-rough-cutoff-generator}. No source premise is added.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The source fields supply every hypothesis of Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-weak-rough-cutoff-generator} for each cutoff of the family.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Rough weak pairing with the weighted Laplacian]
  \label{lem:weak-ordinary-diffusion-pairing}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.integral_bounded_weak_mul_weightedLaplacian_of_contDiff_one}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:weak-compact-flux-bounded-test}
  Let $U$ be $C^1$ with $\int e^{-U}=1$, $v$ be smooth and compactly supported, and $\eta$ be globally bounded and locally integrable with
  coordinate weak derivatives $a_i$ in $L^2$ on every compact set. Then $\eta\,Lv$ and $\sum_ia_i\partial_iv$ are integrable for the
  tilted measure $\nu=e^{-U}dx$ and $\int\eta\,Lv\,d\nu=-\int\sum_ia_i\partial_iv\,d\nu$, where $L$ is the ordinary weighted Laplacian; no
  inverse-Hessian object is used.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Apply Lemma~\ref{lem:weak-compact-flux-bounded-test} to the flux $e^{-U}\nabla v$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Ordinary diffusion coercivity with Lipschitz gradient]
  \label{lem:ordinary-diffusion-coercivity-lipschitz}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.compact_ordinary_diffusion_coercivity_of_lipschitz_gradient}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:letwin-finite-energy-supports}
  On a finite-dimensional real inner product space, let $U$ be $C^1$ and globally Lipschitz with Lipschitz gradient and integrable
  $e^{-U}$. For every smooth compactly supported $f$, $\langle D(\nabla U)\nabla f,\nabla f\rangle$ and $(Lf)^2$ are integrable for the
  tilted measure and the integral of the first is at most the integral of the second. This is the Lipschitz-gradient sibling of
  \texttt{compact\_ordinary\_diffusion\_coercivity}.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Use the $C^{1,1}$ Bochner identity and drop the nonnegative Hessian-square term.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Weak finite-energy Brascamp--Lieb inequality (A.6)]
  \label{lem:letwin-weak-brascamp-lieb}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.scalar_brascamp_lieb_weak_finite_energy,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.compact_weak_variational_estimate,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.norm_sq_le_of_mem_closure_quadratic_bound,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.real_inner_toLp,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.inner_center_left_of_mean_zero}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:weak-ordinary-diffusion-pairing, lem:ordinary-diffusion-coercivity-lipschitz, thm:letwin-scalar-brascamp-lieb}
  Letwin \cite{Letwin2026QuadraticKLS}, Lemma~A.6, at $C^{1,1}$ regularity for a given potential. Let $\nu=e^{-U}dx$ be a log-concave
  probability measure with $U$ $C^1$, globally Lipschitz, with Lipschitz gradient, $\int e^{-U}=1$ and almost everywhere positive definite
  coordinate Hessian. For every globally bounded, locally integrable $\eta$ with coordinate weak derivatives $a$ in $L^2$ on every compact
  set and $\nu$-integrable inverse-Hessian energy $\langle a,(\nabla^2U)^{-1}a\rangle$,
  $\operatorname{Var}_\nu\eta\le\int\langle a,(\nabla^2U)^{-1}a\rangle\,d\nu$. The Hilbert-closure step is shared with the scalar
  Brascamp--Lieb inequality (Theorem~\ref{thm:letwin-scalar-brascamp-lieb}).
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Centered smooth diffusion images are dense; pair $\eta$ with them through Lemma~\ref{lem:weak-ordinary-diffusion-pairing}, bound the
  pairing by inverse-quadratic Young and Lemma~\ref{lem:ordinary-diffusion-coercivity-lipschitz}, and pass to the Hilbert closure.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Matrix product entries and traces]
  \label{lem:matrix-product-entry-trace}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.sum_matrix_product_entry_mul_eq_trace,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.sum_matrix_product_entry_quadratic_eq_trace}
  \leanok
  For symmetric $S$ and $D$ and arbitrary $A$, $\sum_{ij}(SAS)_{ij}(SDS)_{ij}=\operatorname{tr}((SS)A(SS)D)$. For symmetric $S$,
  arbitrary $R$ and symmetric $T_k$, the entrywise quadratic forms of the vectors $((ST_kS)_{ij})_k$ against $R$ sum to
  $\sum_{kl}R_{kl}\operatorname{tr}((SS)T_k(SS)T_l)$. All finite dimensions.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Expand both sides as finite sums and use the symmetry of $S$, $D$ and $T_k$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Random matrix variance trace]
  \label{lem:matrix-variance-trace}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Probability.sum_variance_entries_eq_integral_trace_sub_trace_mean}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:matrix-product-entry-trace}
  Under a probability measure, for an almost everywhere symmetric random matrix $H$ with $L^2$ entries and symmetric mean $M$,
  $\sum_{ij}\operatorname{Var}(H_{ij})=\int\operatorname{tr}(HH)-\operatorname{tr}(MM)$. All finite dimensions.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Sum the entrywise variance identities and use $\operatorname{tr}(HH)=\sum_{ij}H_{ij}^2$ for symmetric $H$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Weak Hessian trace bound for a given potential (Theorem 2.5, PSD $B$)]
  \label{thm:letwin-weak-hessian-trace-bound}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.integral_trace_quadratic_le_two_mul_trace_sq}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:letwin-weak-brascamp-lieb, lem:weak-bounded-trace-quadratic, lem:matrix-product-entry-trace, lem:matrix-variance-trace}
  Weak $C^{1,1}$ form of Letwin \cite{Letwin2026QuadraticKLS}, Theorem~2.5, for a given potential, with positive semidefinite $B$. Let
  $\nu=e^{-U}dx$ with $U$ convex, $C^1$, globally Lipschitz, with Lipschitz gradient, $\int e^{-U}=1$ and almost everywhere positive
  definite Hessian; let $T$ be a weak Hessian tensor in $L^2$ on every compact set, and let the inverse Hessian entries be essentially
  bounded on every compact set. Assume as hypotheses that the Hessian entries are $\nu$-integrable with mean $\mathrm{Id}$, that
  $D=\sum_{kl}(\nabla^2U)^{-1}_{kl}\operatorname{tr}(BT_kBT_l)$ is $\nu$-integrable and that
  $\int D\,d\nu\le\frac12\int\operatorname{tr}(B\nabla^2UB\nabla^2U)\,d\nu$. Then $\operatorname{tr}(B\nabla^2UB\nabla^2U)$ is
  $\nu$-integrable and its integral is at most $2\operatorname{tr}(BB)$. The mean and half-energy inputs remain hypotheses here;
  they hold for the regular moment potential of Theorem~\ref{thm:letwin-regular-moment-weak-stein-coupling}.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Apply Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-weak-brascamp-lieb} to each entry of $SHS$ with $S=B^{1/2}$, sum with Lemma~\ref{lem:matrix-product-entry-trace},
  and compare with the variance identity of Lemma~\ref{lem:matrix-variance-trace} and the half-energy hypothesis.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Almost-everywhere PSD symmetric trace extension]
  \label{lem:symmetric-trace-integral-ae}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.symmetric_trace_integral_extension_of_ae_posSemidef,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.symmetric_trace_integral_extension_mul_of_ae_posSemidef}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:letwin-symmetric-integral}
  For an arbitrary measure and finite index type, let $H$ have measurable entries and be positive semidefinite almost everywhere. If
  $\operatorname{tr}(AHAH)$ is integrable with integral at most $2\operatorname{tr}(AA)$ for every positive semidefinite $A$, then the same
  holds for every symmetric $B$, singular or indefinite. This is the almost-everywhere version of
  \texttt{symmetric\_trace\_integral\_extension}.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Split $B$ into its positive and negative parts and use the pointwise Jordan inequality almost everywhere.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Integrable weak derivatives have zero integral]
  \label{lem:integrable-weak-divergence}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.integral_eq_zero_of_integrable_weak_derivative}
  \leanok
  If $g$ is the weak directional derivative of $f$ along $v$ against compactly supported $C^1$ tests and $f$ and $g$ are globally
  integrable, then $\int g=0$. Every dimension, including zero, and every direction.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Test against a dilated compactly supported cutoff; the cutoff term tends to $\int g$ by dominated convergence and the derivative term
  vanishes since $f$ is integrable.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Tilted integration by parts for bounded Lipschitz tests]
  \label{lem:tilted-ibp-bounded-lipschitz}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.integral_fderiv_tilted_eq_integral_mul_fderiv_of_bounded_lipschitz}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:integrable-weak-divergence}
  Let $U$ be globally Lipschitz with $\int e^{-U}=1$ and $\eta$ be bounded and globally Lipschitz. For every direction $v$, $D_v\eta$ and
  $\eta\,D_vU$ are integrable for the tilted measure $\nu=e^{-U}dx$ and $\int D_v\eta\,d\nu=\int\eta\,D_vU\,d\nu$. All dimensions.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Apply Lemma~\ref{lem:integrable-weak-divergence} to $\eta e^{-U}$, whose weak derivative is $(D_v\eta-\eta D_vU)e^{-U}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[$C^1$ gradient chain coordinate]
  \label{lem:c1-gradient-chain}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Probability.Stein.chain_coordinate_of_differentiableAt_gradient}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:letwin-weak-hessian-identification}
  For $C^1$ functions $u$ and $f$ and a point $y$ where $\nabla u$ is differentiable,
  $\partial_i(f\circ\nabla u)(y)=\sum_j(\nabla^2u)_{ij}(y)\,(\nabla f)_j(\nabla u(y))$. This is the $C^1$ counterpart of
  \texttt{chain\_coordinate}; all dimensions.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Chain rule at $y$ and identification of the derivative of $\nabla u$ with the Hessian matrix.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Weak source Stein identity]
  \label{lem:weak-source-stein-identity}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.source_stein_identity_of_bounded_lipschitz_test}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:tilted-ibp-bounded-lipschitz, lem:c1-gradient-chain}
  Let $u$ be $C^1$ and globally Lipschitz with Lipschitz gradient and $\int e^{-u}=1$, and let $\nu=e^{-u}dx$. For every $C^1$, bounded and
  globally Lipschitz test $f$ and every coordinate $i$, $\partial_iu\,f(\nabla u)$ and $\sum_j(\nabla^2u)_{ij}\,\partial_jf(\nabla u)$ are
  $\nu$-integrable and $\int\partial_iu\,f(\nabla u)\,d\nu=\int\sum_j(\nabla^2u)_{ij}\,\partial_jf(\nabla u)\,d\nu$. This is the latent
  source coupling identity behind Letwin \cite{Letwin2026QuadraticKLS}, Lemma~2.7, at $C^{1,1}$ regularity; no Stein kernel is constructed
  and no target inverse is used.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Apply Lemma~\ref{lem:tilted-ibp-bounded-lipschitz} to $\eta=f\circ\nabla u$ and identify $\partial_i\eta$ almost everywhere by
  Lemma~\ref{lem:c1-gradient-chain}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Compact source dual bound]
  \label{lem:compact-source-dual-bound}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Probability.Stein.compact_linear_pairing_sq_le_of_source_identity}
  \leanok
  Let $P$ be a measure on a latent space, $Z$ a measurable map with $Z_\#P=\mu$ for a probability law $\mu$, and $F$ a latent field in
  $L^2(P)$. If a smooth compactly supported $h$ satisfies the latent source identity
  $\int\langle x,v\rangle h\,d\mu=\int\langle F,\nabla h(Z)\rangle\,dP$, then
  $(\int\langle x,v\rangle h\,d\mu)^2\le(\int|F|^2dP)(\int|Dh|^2d\mu)$. Arbitrary latent space and all dimensions. The source identity and
  the $L^2$ field remain inputs; this is the compact dual Cauchy--Schwarz step that supplies the dual bound directly, without a target
  Stein kernel.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Cauchy--Schwarz in $L^2(P)$ and the change of variables $Z_\#P=\mu$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Gaussian directional identity for $C^1$ tests]
  \label{lem:gaussian-directional-identity}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Probability.Gaussian.directional_identity_of_bounded_lipschitz_test}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:integrable-weak-divergence}
  Under \texttt{Gaussian.law s} with $s>0$, in every dimension, for every $C^1$, bounded and globally Lipschitz $f$ and every direction $v$,
  $\langle x,v\rangle f$ and $D_vf$ are integrable and $\int\langle x,v\rangle f=(2s)^{-1}\int D_vf$. This supports the Gaussian smoothing
  step; no product coupling is asserted.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Apply Lemma~\ref{lem:integrable-weak-divergence} to $f$ times the Gaussian density.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Regular moment potential: Theorem 2.5 with constant 2 and source Stein coupling]
  \label{thm:letwin-regular-moment-weak-stein-coupling}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_regular_moment_weak_stein_coupling,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_regular_moment_hessian_trace_bound}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:letwin-regular-moment-bounded-weak-cutoff-testing, lem:letwin-weak-trace-contraction, lem:letwin-trace-gram-comparison, thm:letwin-weak-hessian-trace-bound, lem:symmetric-trace-integral-ae, lem:tilted-ibp-bounded-lipschitz, lem:weak-source-stein-identity}
  For the source law, under the hypotheses of Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-weak-regular-moment-potential} ($n>0$, isotropic $1_Ke^{-W}dx$ with $K$ compact and convex with
  nonempty interior, $W$ smooth and $\kappa$-uniformly convex), there is a single weak normalized convex globally Lipschitz moment potential $u$,
  with constants $L$, $L_F$ and weak Hessian tensor $T$, that retains all earlier conclusions: the regularity of Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-weak-regular-moment-potential} ($C^1$, $16/\kappa$-Lipschitz
  gradient, $4/\kappa$ centered-difference bound, almost everywhere positive definite Hessian with log-det equation and $16/\kappa$ cap), the
  weak tensor, first Monge--Amp\`ere and inverse fields, mean Hessian $\mathrm{Id}$ under $\nu=e^{-u}dx$, the weak evolution identities,
  bounded testing, the weak cutoffs and rough cutoff testing, the weak trace contraction, A.5 conservativity $\int g\,d\nu=0$ and the half
  energy $\int D\,d\nu\le\frac12\int\operatorname{tr}(BHBH)\,d\nu$ for every positive semidefinite $B$. In addition: for every real symmetric
  $B$, $\operatorname{tr}(BHBH)$ is $\nu$-integrable and $\int\operatorname{tr}(BHBH)\,d\nu\le2\operatorname{tr}(BB)$ (Theorem~2.5 with the
  exact constant $2$, weak $C^{1,1}$ form for this witness); the weighted integration by parts $\int D_v\eta\,d\nu=\int\eta\,D_vu\,d\nu$ for
  every bounded globally Lipschitz $\eta$; and the coordinate source Stein identity for every $C^1$, bounded and globally Lipschitz test.
  This is not the smooth moment map of Lemma~2.2, not a constructed Stein kernel, and not Theorem~1.2 or the constant-8 bound.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  At the witness of Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-regular-moment-bounded-weak-cutoff-testing}, contract the tested evolution identities with
  Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-weak-trace-contraction}, use the cutoffs to obtain $\int g\,d\nu=0$, and combine
  Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-trace-gram-comparison} into the half energy. Theorem~\ref{thm:letwin-weak-hessian-trace-bound} gives the bound for
  positive semidefinite $B$, and Lemma~\ref{lem:symmetric-trace-integral-ae} extends it to every symmetric $B$. The weighted integration by
  parts and the source Stein identity are Lemmas~\ref{lem:tilted-ibp-bounded-lipschitz} and~\ref{lem:weak-source-stein-identity} at $u$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Gradient inverse and compact-local ellipticity at the regular-class moment witness]
  \label{lem:letwin-regular-moment-inverse-ellipticity}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.posSemidef_sub_scalar_of_det_lower,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.injective_gradient_of_strictConvexOn,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.ae_posSemidef_hessianMatrix_sub_of_log_det,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.measurable_inverse_gradient_of_map_gradient,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_regular_moment_potential_with_inverse,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_regular_moment_potential_locally_elliptic}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:letwin-weak-regular-moment-potential}
  Supports for Letwin \cite{Letwin2026QuadraticKLS}, Lemma~2.2, in the strong regular class.
  (i) For $n>0$, a real positive semidefinite $A$ with $A\le K\,\mathrm{Id}$ and $\delta\le\det A$ ($K,\delta>0$) satisfies
  $A\ge(\delta/K^{n-1})\,\mathrm{Id}$.
  (ii) On a finite-dimensional real inner product space, a differentiable strictly convex function has an injective gradient (every
  dimension, zero included).
  (iii) If a.e. $\nabla^2u$ is positive definite with $\log\det\nabla^2u=g$ for continuous $g$ and $\nabla^2u\le K\,\mathrm{Id}$, then on every
  compact $S$ there is $\delta_S>0$ with $\nabla^2u\ge\delta_S\,\mathrm{Id}$ a.e. on $S$.
  (iv) At the potential $u$ of Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-weak-regular-moment-potential}, with all of its fields at the same $u$ and $L$:
  $\nabla u$ is injective with a measurable everywhere left inverse, and $u$ is compact-locally uniformly elliptic in the sense of (iii).
  The given-potential form of (iv) applies to any normalized convex Lipschitz $C^1$ potential pushing $e^{-u}dx$ onto $1_Ke^{-W}dx$.
  No $C^2$, smoothness, everywhere positive definiteness, surjectivity onto $\operatorname{int}K$ or diffeomorphism is asserted.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  (i) The eigenvalues lie in $[0,K]$ and their product is at least $\delta$, so each is at least $\delta/K^{n-1}$. (ii) Restrict to the
  injective affine line through two points with equal gradients; strict convexity separates the endpoint derivatives. (iii) $g$ attains its
  minimum on $S$; apply (i) with $\delta=e^{\min_S g}$. (iv) The pushforward gives strict convexity
  (Caffarelli's argument, \texttt{strictConvexOn\_of\_map\_gradient}); (ii) and the measurable embedding of a continuous injective gradient give
  the inverse; (iii) with $g=-u+W(\nabla u)$ and $K=16/\kappa$ gives ellipticity.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Target Stein kernel of the strong regular class]
  \label{thm:letwin-regular-moment-target-stein-kernel}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Probability.Stein.hsNorm_sq_eq_trace_mul_self,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.Stein.hessianKernel_posDefAE_of_map,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.Stein.memLp_hsNorm_hessianKernel_of_map,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.weak_hessianKernel_isSteinKernel,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_regular_moment_target_stein_kernel}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:letwin-regular-moment-weak-stein-coupling, lem:letwin-regular-moment-inverse-ellipticity, lem:letwin-hessian-kernel, lem:weak-source-stein-identity}
  Weak counterpart of Letwin \cite{Letwin2026QuadraticKLS}, Lemma~2.7, with the Hilbert--Schmidt bound from Theorem~2.5. Under the regular-class
  hypotheses ($n>0$, isotropic $\mu=1_Ke^{-W}dx$, $K$ compact convex with nonempty interior, $W$ smooth and $\kappa$-uniformly convex), at the
  same $u$ and $L$ as Theorem~\ref{thm:letwin-regular-moment-weak-stein-coupling} (its whole flat trace-bound conjunction retained) and a
  measurable everywhere left inverse $\Psi$ of that $\nabla u$, the kernel $\tau=\nabla^2u\circ\Psi$ is a Stein kernel of $\mu$
  (Definition~2.6) that is $\mu$-a.e. positive definite and symmetric, has mean $\mathrm{Id}$, $|\tau|_{HS}\in L^2(\mu)$ and
  $\int|\tau|_{HS}^2\,d\mu\le2n$. More generally, a.e. positive definiteness and the Hilbert--Schmidt $L^2$ bound pass from $\nu$ to $\mu$ along
  $\Psi_\#\mu=\nu$ for $C^1$ $u$; the kernel is a Stein kernel for every $C^1$ potential with Lipschitz gradient and an a.e. left inverse. This is
  not the smooth moment map of Lemma~2.2, not everywhere positive definiteness, and not the merely convex source class.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  From $(\nabla u)_\#\nu=\mu$ and $\Psi\circ\nabla u=\mathrm{id}$, $\Psi_\#\mu=\nu$, which transports the a.e. statements and the $L^2$ bound. The
  Stein identity is the bounded-Lipschitz source identity tested with $f\circ\nabla u$ and pushed forward. Since $|H|_{HS}^2=\operatorname{tr}(HH)$
  for symmetric $H$, Theorem~2.5 at $B=\mathrm{Id}$ gives $\int|\tau|_{HS}^2\,d\mu=\int\operatorname{tr}(HH)\,d\nu\le2n$; the mean and symmetry
  transport the same way.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Directional weak subsolution and local coefficient bounds at the regular-class witness]
  \label{lem:letwin-regular-moment-directional-subsolution}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.integral_quadratic_flux_le_of_posSemidef,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.inverse_posSemidef_bounds,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_compact_inverse_hessian_coefficient_bounds,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.integral_directional_hessian_flux_le,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_regular_moment_directional_hessian_subsolution}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:letwin-regular-moment-weak-hessian-evolution, lem:letwin-weak-hessian-evolution, lem:letwin-regular-moment-inverse-ellipticity}
  Supporting step toward Letwin \cite{Letwin2026QuadraticKLS}, Lemma~2.2 (interior regularity of the moment map).
  (i) Under an arbitrary measure and finite index type, entrywise integrable matrix fields with $\int F_{ij}=-\int E_{ij}$ and $E+H$ a.e.
  positive semidefinite satisfy $\int v^{\top}Fv\le\int v^{\top}Hv$.
  (ii) For positive definite $A$ with $\alpha\,\mathrm{Id}\le A\le\beta\,\mathrm{Id}$ ($\alpha,\beta>0$, every dimension):
  $\beta^{-1}\mathrm{Id}\le A^{-1}\le\alpha^{-1}\mathrm{Id}$.
  (iii) For a continuous $u$ with a.e.\ positive definite Hessian, continuous $\log\det\nabla^2u=g$ and $\nabla^2u\le K\,\mathrm{Id}$, every compact
  $S$ has $c_0,c_1>0$ with $c_0\mathrm{Id}\le e^{-u}(\nabla^2u)^{-1}\le c_1\mathrm{Id}$ a.e.\ on $S$. For nonempty $S$:
  $c_0=\min_Se^{-u}/K$, $c_1=\max_Se^{-u}/\delta_S$; for empty $S$ the constants are $1$.
  (iv) Under the hypotheses of Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-weak-hessian-evolution}, with $W$ convex and $\nabla^2u$ positive definite a.e., every
  direction $v$ and every nonnegative compactly supported $C^1$ test $\psi$ satisfy
  \[\int e^{-u}\sum_a\Big(\sum_b(\nabla^2u)^{-1}_{ba}\,\partial_bh_v\Big)\partial_a\psi\le\int e^{-u}h_v\psi,
    \qquad h_v=v^{\top}\nabla^2u\,v,\quad \partial_bh_v=\sum_{ij}v_iv_jT_{bij}.\]
  (v) At the same $u,L,L_F,T$ as Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-regular-moment-weak-hessian-evolution}, all of its fields retained, (iv) holds, and so do
  the coefficient bounds of (iii) with $g=-u+W(\nabla u)$ and $K=16/\kappa$. Strong regular class, $n>0$. Not weak Harnack, oscillation
  decay, $C^{2}$ or Schauder regularity, nor the merely convex source class.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  (i) Contract each entry identity with $v$ and integrate the pointwise bound $-v^{\top}Ev\le v^{\top}Hv$. (ii) Conjugate by $A^{-1}$ and read off
  the eigenvalues. (iii) Combine the compact-local floor of Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-regular-moment-inverse-ellipticity} with (ii) and the extrema
  of the continuous weight on $S$. (iv) In the weighted weak (2.6), $E+\nabla^2u=\nabla^2u\,\nabla^2W(\nabla u)\,\nabla^2u+\mathrm{traceGram}$ is
  positive semidefinite a.e. (convex $W$; a.e.\ symmetric weak third derivatives); absorb $e^{-u}\psi\ge0$ and apply (i). (v) The source fields
  supply all hypotheses; $\det=\exp g$ follows from the log-det field and convexity of $W$ from strong convexity.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Nonlinear weak test tools at the regular-class weak data]
  \label{lem:letwin-rough-nonlinear-testing}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_smooth_compact_extension_on_nonnegative_interval,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_smooth_compact_log_shift_extension,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_smooth_compact_rpow_shift_extension,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.compact_bounded_test_domination,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.hasWeakDerivAlong_comp_of_compact_bounded,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.hasWeakDerivAlong_log_shift_of_ae_nonneg_compact_bounded,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.hasWeakDerivAlong_rpow_shift_of_ae_nonneg_compact_bounded,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.directional_cutoff_data,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.integral_directional_hessian_flux_le_of_bounded_weak_test,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.rough_square_test_of_weak_data,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.posSemidef_quadratic_young}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:letwin-regular-moment-directional-subsolution}
  Supporting tools toward the interior regularity step of Letwin \cite{Letwin2026QuadraticKLS}, Lemma~2.2.
  (i) A function smooth on $(-\delta,\infty)$ with $F(0)=0$ agrees, with its derivative, on $[0,B]$ with a smooth compactly supported
  Lipschitz $\Phi$, $\Phi(0)=0$; in particular for $\log(\delta+t)-\log\delta$ and $(\delta+t)^q-\delta^q$, every real $q$.
  (ii) For bounded compactly supported weak $H^1$ data $\eta$ (compactly supported, globally $L^2$ weak derivatives $a_i$) and $\Phi$ of class
  $C^1$, Lipschitz, $\Phi(0)=0$: $\Phi\circ\eta$ has weak derivatives $\Phi'(\eta)a_i$, compactly supported and in $L^2$; if $\eta\ge0$ a.e. and
  $\delta>0$, so do the normalized shifted logarithm and real powers with their physical derivatives.
  (iii) For $\nabla u$ Lipschitz with weak Hessian tensor $T$, $b=\chi\,v^{\top}\nabla^2u\,v$ ($\chi$ a compactly supported $C^1$ cutoff) is bounded,
  compactly supported, with weak derivatives $\chi\sum_{ij}v_iv_jT_{kij}+\partial_k\chi\,v^{\top}\nabla^2u\,v$.
  (iv) The directional weak subsolution of Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-regular-moment-directional-subsolution} holds against bounded compactly supported
  weak $H^1$ tests $\eta\ge0$ a.e., with both pairings integrable; in particular for the rough square $\eta=b^2$.
  (v) $2x^{\top}Ay\le\varepsilon\,x^{\top}Ax+\varepsilon^{-1}y^{\top}Ay$ for real positive semidefinite $A$, $\varepsilon>0$.
  Not the positive unnormalized power test under an outer cutoff, a Caccioppoli or Moser estimate, or weak Harnack.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  (i) Multiply by a bump function equal to $1$ near $[0,B]$ whose support, the closed interval $[-\delta/2,B+\delta/2]$, lies in $(-\delta,\infty)$.
  (ii) Approximate $\eta$ by smooth compactly supported functions with a common support and bound, a.e.\ convergence and $L^2$ convergence of the
  gradients. Apply the classical chain rule, and pass both sides of the testing identity to the limit by dominated convergence and an $L^2$
  pairing bound. The shifted logarithm and powers follow from (i) on the a.e.\ range $[0,\max(C,0)]$. (iii) Finite linearity and the compact
  cutoff product rule. (iv) The bounded weak-test weighted identity contracted with positive semidefinite forcing at $\eta\ge0$; the square by the
  bounded weak product rule. (v) Positive semidefiniteness at $\varepsilon x-y$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[$H^1$ energy and weak Sobolev tools for bounded weak supersolutions]
  \label{lem:letwin-h1-energy-sobolev}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.Mollification.support_convolution_subset_cthickening_of_rOut_le,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.HasWeakDerivAlong.const_add_mul,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_contDiff_compact_bounded_weak_deriv_approximation_of_support_subset,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.bounded_rpow_cutoff_weak_data,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.bounded_rpow_cutoff_weak_test,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.rpow_cutoff_quadratic_young,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.bounded_weak_supersolution_caccioppoli,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.eLpNorm_le_sum_eLpNorm_weak_deriv_of_compact_bounded,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.eLpNorm_six_le_sum_eLpNorm_weak_deriv_of_support_subset_two,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.eLpNorm_top_le_eLpNorm_weak_deriv_of_support_subset_one}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:letwin-rough-nonlinear-testing}
  Supporting $H^1$ tools toward Letwin \cite{Letwin2026QuadraticKLS}, Lemma~2.2, and Berman--Berndtsson
  \cite{BermanBerndtsson2013RealMA}, Theorem~2.24(iii), in the form of Gilbarg--Trudinger (2001), (8.48)--(8.49).
  Let $w$ be bounded with $w\ge0$ a.e., have coordinate weak derivatives $a_k$ in $L^2_{\mathrm{loc}}$ on
  $\mathbb R^n$ (every $n$), and set $s=\delta+\max(w,0)$ with $\delta>0$. Let $\chi$ be a compactly supported $C^1$ cutoff.
  (i) For every real $r$, $\chi s^r$ is bounded and compactly supported in $\operatorname{tsupport}\chi$, with weak
  derivatives $\partial_k\chi\,s^r+\chi\,r s^{r-1}a_k$ in $L^2$. The square test $\chi^2 s^q$ is the case of the cutoff
  $\chi^2$.
  (ii) Caccioppoli. Let $A$ be a.e.\ measurable with $\alpha\le A\le\beta$ a.e.\ on $\operatorname{tsupport}\chi$, and let
  $-F\int\eta\le\int a^{\top}A\nabla\eta$ hold for every bounded nonnegative weak test supported in
  $\operatorname{tsupport}\chi$. Then for $p>0$ the three integrands below are integrable and
  $\int\chi^2 s^{-p-1}|a|^2\le\frac{4\beta}{p^2\alpha}\int s^{1-p}|\nabla\chi|^2+\frac{2F}{p\alpha}\int\chi^2 s^{-p}$.
  (iii) Weak Sobolev for bounded compactly supported $\eta$ with compactly supported, globally $L^2$ coordinate weak
  derivatives $a_i$:
  \begin{itemize}
    \item for $n>2$, $\|\eta\|_q\le C_{\mathrm{GNS}}(2)\sum_i\|a_i\|_2$ with $1/q=1/2-1/n$;
    \item for $n=2$, with supports in a compact $K$ and $\varepsilon>0$, the single exponent
      $\|\eta\|_6\le C_{\mathrm{GNS}}(3/2)\,|K_\varepsilon|^{1/6}\sum_i\|a_i\|_2$, where $K_\varepsilon$ is the closed
      $\varepsilon$-thickening;
    \item for $n=1$, the essential bound $\|\eta\|_\infty\le|K_\varepsilon|^{1/2}\|a_0\|_2$.
  \end{itemize}
  These estimates apply at the strong regular-class witness as follows: the potential is fixed first; for each compact $S$, $\alpha,\beta$
  and a density bound $B$ are fixed; then every direction $v$ and every cutoff with $\operatorname{tsupport}\chi\subseteq S$ is admissible,
  with $w=(16/\kappa)|v|^2-v^{\top}\nabla^2u\,v$ and $F_v=(16/\kappa)|v|^2B$.
  None of this is Moser iteration, John--Nirenberg or weak Harnack (Gilbarg--Trudinger Theorem~8.18). It does not give
  every finite exponent for $n=2$, or full radius scaling.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  (i) Truncate $w$ by an outer bump equal to $1$ near $\operatorname{tsupport}\chi$, apply the shifted real-power chain
  rule of Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-rough-nonlinear-testing} and the affine rule, then the compact $C^1$ product rule.
  (ii) Test the supersolution inequality with $\chi^2 s^{-p}$. Absorb the cross term by the weighted Young inequality
  $2\chi s^{-p}a^{\top}Ab\le\frac p2\chi^2 s^{-p-1}a^{\top}Aa+\frac2p s^{1-p}b^{\top}Ab$, and use the ellipticity bounds.
  (iii) Mollify with bump radii at most $\varepsilon$, so all approximants are supported in one compact set inside
  $K_\varepsilon$. Apply Mathlib's Gagliardo--Nirenberg--Sobolev inequality, H\"older on $K_\varepsilon$ for $n=2$, or the
  compact fundamental theorem of calculus through the singleton-basis isometry for $n=1$. Pass to the limit by lower
  semicontinuity along the a.e.\ convergent sequence.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Moser inputs at one regular-class witness]
  \label{lem:letwin-moser-inputs}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.hasWeakDerivAlong_comp_of_bounded,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.hasWeakDerivAlong_log_max_shift_of_ae_nonneg_bounded,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.bounded_weak_supersolution_log_caccioppoli,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.bounded_weak_supersolution_power_energy,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.eLpNorm_finite_le_sum_eLpNorm_weak_deriv_of_support_subset_two,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_smooth_concentric_cutoff_norm_fderiv_le,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.tendsto_eLpNorm_of_exponents_tendsto_atTop,
        FunctionalInequalities.L2.sq_sum_eLpNorm_le_dim_mul_integral_sum_sq}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:letwin-h1-energy-sobolev}
  Inputs of a Moser iteration in the form of Gilbarg--Trudinger (2001), (8.48)--(8.49), supporting Letwin
  \cite{Letwin2026QuadraticKLS}, Lemma~2.2.
  (i) For bounded $\eta$ with coordinate weak derivatives in $L^2_{\mathrm{loc}}$ (no compact support) and $\Phi$ of class
  $C^1$ and Lipschitz, $\Phi\circ\eta$ has weak derivatives $\Phi'(\eta)a_i$; in particular $\log(\delta+\max(w,0))$ for
  $w\ge0$ a.e.
  (ii) With the hypotheses of Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-h1-energy-sobolev}(ii) and $s=\delta+\max(w,0)$:
  \begin{itemize}
    \item $\int\chi^2|\nabla\log s|^2\le\frac{4\beta}{\alpha}\int|\nabla\chi|^2+\frac{2F}{\alpha\delta}\int\chi^2$;
    \item for $r<1/2$ and $p=1-2r$, $\int|\nabla(\chi s^r)|^2\le\bigl(2+\frac{8r^2\beta}{p^2\alpha}\bigr)\int s^{2r}|\nabla\chi|^2
      +\frac{4r^2F}{p\alpha}\int\chi^2s^{2r-1}$.
  \end{itemize}
  (iii) For $n=2$ and every finite $q\ge2$,
  $\|\eta\|_q\le C_{\mathrm{GNS}}(\tfrac{2q}{q+2})\,|K_\varepsilon|^{1/q}\sum_i\|a_i\|_2$.
  (iv) In a finite-dimensional real inner product space one $D>0$ gives, for every center and radii $0\le r<R$, a smooth cutoff
  that equals $1$ on the inner ball, is supported in the outer ball and satisfies $|\nabla\chi|\le D/(R-r)$.
  (v) On a probability space, $\|f\|_{p_k}\to\|f\|_\infty$ (essential supremum) for essentially bounded $f$ and any exponents
  $p_k\to\infty$.
  (vi) $(\sum_i\|a_i\|_2)^2\le n\int\sum_ia_i^2$ for any measure.
  At the strong regular-class witness these inputs hold with a single potential and a single $D$; for each compact $S$ one choice of
  $\alpha,\beta,B$ serves every direction and every cutoff in $S$, including dyadic families with radii $R(1+2^{-m})$.
  At the same potential they give the normalized closed-ball limit of $(\delta+\max(w,0))^{-1}$.
  These are inputs, not the Moser recurrence, Morrey over all balls, John--Nirenberg or weak Harnack.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  (i) Test by test with an outer bump, the compact chain rule for $\Phi-\Phi(0)$ and the affine rule; the logarithm through
  a smooth profile on the a.e.\ range.
  (ii) Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-h1-energy-sobolev}(ii) at $p=1$ and at $p=1-2r$, with $(x+y)^2\le2x^2+2y^2$.
  (iii) The controlled approximation, the Gagliardo--Nirenberg--Sobolev inequality at $p=2q/(q+2)$, and H\"older on the
  support.
  (iv) A scalar profile composed with the normalized squared distance.
  (v) Exponent monotonicity, and Chebyshev at an intermediate level of positive mass.
  (vi) Finite Cauchy--Schwarz and $\|a_i\|_2^2=\int a_i^2$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Morrey and critical-potential inputs]
  \label{lem:letwin-morrey-potential-inputs}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Probability.integrable_exp_abs_of_moment_growth,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.integrable_exp_toReal_of_moment_growth,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.integrable_exp_toReal_of_scaled_high_moment_growth,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.measure_univ_le_of_ae_closedBall_ball_growth,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.restrict_ball_growth_of_local_ball_growth,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_morrey_bound_of_cutoff_energy,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.lintegral_norm_sub_rpow_neg_le_of_ae_closedBall_ball_growth,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.lintegral_critical_radial_potential_rpow_le,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.lintegral_density_critical_potential_rpow_le,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.lintegral_density_critical_morrey_potential_rpow_le,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.integrable_exp_critical_morrey_potential}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:letwin-moser-inputs}
  Supporting inputs for Letwin \cite{Letwin2026QuadraticKLS}, Lemma~2.2: the Morrey hypothesis (7.51) of
  Gilbarg--Trudinger (2001), Theorem~7.21, from the logarithmic energy (8.49), and the critical potential of Lemma~7.20 at
  $p=n\ge2$. Explicit constants below are legitimate choices, not the printed numerical values.
  (i) Ball growth. If $\nu$ is supported in $\overline B(x,D)$ with $\nu(B(x,r))\le Kr^d$, then $\nu(X)\le KD^d$. Local
  growth for centres within $2R$ and radii $\le R$ gives growth of $\nu\llcorner B_R$ for every ball.
  (ii) Morrey from cutoff energy. If $\int\chi^2|b|^2\le E_0\int|\nabla\chi|^2+E_1\int\chi^2$ for every $C^1$ cutoff in
  $[0,1]$ supported in $\overline B_{4R}$, then $\int_{B_R\cap B_r(c)}|b|\le\omega_n2^n(1+E_0nD^2+E_1R^2)\,r^{n-1}$ for every
  ball, where $D$ is the uniform concentric-cutoff constant.
  (iii) Potentials. Measures with ball growth have radial potentials $\le Kd/(d-a)\,D^{d-a}$. The critical moments are
  $\le A/(1-\mu)[((1-\mu)/\mu)Kq]^qD^N$ for $\mu\le1/2$, $q\ge2$. For a density with
  $\int_{S\cap B_r(x)}|f|\le Kr^{n-1}$ and $K>0$, the potential $P=\int_S|x-y|^{1-n}|f(y)|\,dy$ is finite a.e., and
  $\int_S\exp(P/(2e(n-1)K))\le2\omega_n\frac n{n-1}(1+4(n-1)^2)D^n$.
  (iv) Moment growth $\int|f|^k\le A(Ck)^k$ gives $\int\exp(\sigma|f|)\le2A$ for $\sigma Ce\le1/2$.
  At the strong regular-class witness, for a single potential, these inputs give the all-ball Morrey bound of the logarithmic
  gradient and, for $n\ge2$, the exponential integrability of its critical potential.
  These are not Lemma~7.16, John--Nirenberg, the full Lemma~7.20 for $p>1$, or Moser iteration.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  (i) A right limit of radii, and the triangle inequality.
  (ii) Concentric cutoffs from $r$ to $2r$, Haar volumes, and the pointwise Young inequality $\sqrt Q\le rQ+1/(4r)$; then (i).
  (iii) A layer-cake tail bound, H\"older interpolation of radial kernels and Tonelli, then (iv).
  (iv) The exponential series with $k^k/k!\le e^k$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Bounded weak convex representation and critical John--Nirenberg]
  \label{lem:letwin-bounded-jn-inputs}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.map_restrict_convex_ray_le,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.measure_mul_abs_sub_average_le_riesz_of_contDiffOn,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.lintegral_riesz_potential_le_diameter_mul_lintegral,
        FunctionalInequalities.L2.memLp_sqrt_sum_sq,
        FunctionalInequalities.L2.gradient_density_l1_of_coordinate_l2,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.measure_mul_abs_sub_average_le_riesz_of_bounded_weak_data,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.integrable_exp_abs_sub_average_of_bounded_weak_morrey,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.integral_rpow_mul_integral_rpow_neg_le_of_log_exp}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:letwin-morrey-potential-inputs}
  Supporting inputs for Letwin \cite{Letwin2026QuadraticKLS}, Lemma~2.2, from Gilbarg--Trudinger (2001), Lemma~7.16 and
  Theorem~7.21 at the critical exponent $p=n$.
  (i) Convex representation. For $u\in C^1$ on an open convex $\Omega$, a measurable $S\subseteq\Omega$ and $x\in\Omega$ with $S$
  within distance $D$ of $x$: $|S|\,|u(x)-u_S|\le\frac{D^n}{n}\int_\Omega|x-z|^{1-n}|Du(z)|\,dz$ (mass form, $|S|=0$ included).
  (ii) $L^1$ potential. On a set of diameter at most $D$ ($n\ge2$), the critical Riesz potential of $f\in L^1$ is finite a.e.\
  and its integral is at most $\omega_nnD\|f\|_1$; coordinate $L^2$ convergence gives $L^1$ convergence of Euclidean densities.
  (iii) Weak representation. The inequality of (i) holds a.e.\ for globally bounded weak $H^1_{\mathrm{loc}}$ data on a bounded
  open convex domain ($n\ge2$), with $|Du|$ replaced by $\sqrt{\sum_i a_i^2}$.
  (iv) John--Nirenberg. Under $\int_{\Omega\cap B(x,r)}\sqrt{\sum a_i^2}\le Kr^{n-1}$ for all balls and
  $\sigma=[2(n-1)e]^{-1}n|\Omega|/D^n$, $\int_\Omega\exp(\frac{\sigma}{K}|u-u_\Omega|)\le2\omega_n\frac n{n-1}(1+4(n-1)^2)D^n$.
  (v) Moments. $\int\exp(p|\log s-m|)\le A$ gives $\int s^p\int s^{-p}\le A^2$.
  At a fixed regular-class witness and for the local deficit, these inputs give the John--Nirenberg estimate of $\log s$ on $B_{3R}$
  with the radius-dependent exponent $\sigma/K$ and the power-moment product. These are not Lemma~7.16 for the domain-only
  $W^{1,1}(\Omega)$ class or John--Nirenberg for $n=1$; the bounded weak Harnack inequality is Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-bounded-weak-harnack}.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  (i) Segment integration, the homothety $y\mapsto x+t(y-x)$ with Jacobian $t^{-n}$ and the exact tail $\frac{D^N}{N}d^{1-N}$.
  (ii) Tonelli and the subcritical radial bound; H\"older on a finite measure. (iii) A cutoff, the smooth approximation of
  bounded compact weak data and an $L^1$-controlled subsequence of potential errors. (iv) (iii) at $S=\Omega$ and the critical
  potential of Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-morrey-potential-inputs}. (v) $s^{\pm p}\le e^{\pm pm}e^{p|\log s-m|}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Local bounded Moser inputs]
  \label{lem:letwin-local-moser-inputs}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.local_rpow_cutoff_weak_data,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.local_rpow_cutoff_weak_test,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_bounded_weak_log_extension,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.local_weak_supersolution_caccioppoli,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.local_weak_supersolution_power_energy,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.local_weak_supersolution_log_energy,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.weak_cutoff_sobolev_gain_of_energy,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.weak_cutoff_sobolev_gain_of_energy_of_bounded_log,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.restrict_ball_growth_of_small_ball_growth_and_mass,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_buffered_morrey_bound_of_cutoff_energy,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_centered_buffered_morrey_bound_of_cutoff_energy}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:letwin-morrey-potential-inputs, lem:letwin-bounded-jn-inputs}
  Supporting inputs for Letwin \cite{Letwin2026QuadraticKLS}, Lemma~2.2, in the locally nonnegative bounded class of
  Gilbarg--Trudinger (2001), Theorem~8.18: $w$ bounded with weak derivatives $a$ in $L^2_{\mathrm{loc}}$, but $w\ge0$ only
  a.e.\ on the support of the cutoff $\chi$; $s=\delta+\max(w,0)$, $\delta>0$.
  (i) Local tests. $\chi s^q$ and $\chi^2s^q$ (every real $q$) carry the exact weak derivatives, are compactly supported in
  $\operatorname{supp}\chi$, and $\chi^2s^q\ge0$ pointwise.
  (ii) Local energies. The Caccioppoli inequality with constants $4\beta/(p^2\alpha)$, $2F/(p\alpha)$, the power energy of
  $\chi s^r$ for $r<1/2$, and the logarithmic energy $\int\chi^2|s^{-1}a|^2\le(4\beta/\alpha)\int|\nabla\chi|^2+
  (2F/(\alpha\delta))\int\chi^2$, with no weak derivative of $\log s$ asserted.
  (iii) Weak extension. One bounded $H$ with weak derivatives $dH$ in $L^2_{\mathrm{loc}}$, chosen before every domain,
  equals $\log s$ with $dH=s^{-1}a$ a.e.\ on every measurable set where $w\ge0$ a.e.
  (iv) Buffered Morrey. Cutoff energy on $\overline B_{4R}$ gives $\int_{B_{3R}\cap B_r(c)}|b|\le\omega_n16^n
  (1+E_0nD^2+E_1R^2)\,r^{n-1}$ for every ball; the ball growth uses small balls and a separate mass bound.
  (v) One-step gain. For $n>2$ and $1/q=1/2-1/n$, a given power energy of $\chi s^r$ gives
  $\|s^r\|_{L^q(V)}^2\le C_{\mathrm{GNS}}^2n(\gamma nL^2+f/\delta)\int_Us^{2r}$.
  At a fixed regular-class witness, for the local deficit $M-h_v$ with the cap $h_v\le M$ only on
  $\overline B_{4R}$, these inputs give the local logarithmic energy, one weak extension, the all-ball Morrey bound on $B_{3R}$ and, for
  $n\ge2$, the exponential integrability of the critical potential on $B_{3R}$; and one global-class dyadic gain step for
  $n>2$; there the coefficients are chosen once per radius and the Morrey domain is centred at the origin.
  (vi) Centred and fixed-compact form. The bound (iv) holds at every domain centre $x_0$ with $D$ fixed first. At the regular-class witness one may also
  choose the coefficients once per compact $S$ before all centres and radii with $\overline B(x_0,4R)\subseteq S$, and keep
  all linear power data, the power and logarithmic energies, one weak extension and the centred Morrey bound; under
  $FR^2\le\alpha\delta$ one also obtains, inside the same extension, John--Nirenberg and the power moments at the uniform exponent
  $p_0=\tau n/(32^n(3+4\beta nD^2/\alpha))$. These are not the full Theorem~8.18 for unbounded $W^{1,2}$ data; the Moser iteration
  for bounded data is Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-bounded-weak-harnack}.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  (i) The compact smooth power profile on $[0,C]$ and the bounded noncompact chain rule applied to $w$ itself, identified
  with $\max(w,0)$ only on $\operatorname{supp}\chi$. (ii) Test with $\chi^2s^{-p}$ and absorb the cross term; $p=1$ and
  $s^{-1}\le\delta^{-1}$ for the logarithm. (iii) $H=\log\delta+\Phi\circ w$ for the compact log profile $\Phi$. (iv) Cutoffs
  from $r$ to $2r$ inside $B_{15R/4}$, the cutoff $3R\to4R$ for the mass, Young's inequality, then (i) of
  Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-morrey-potential-inputs} in its small-ball form. (v) Domination and the critical Gagliardo--Nirenberg--Sobolev inequality.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Bounded weak Harnack inequality]
  \label{lem:letwin-bounded-weak-harnack}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.geometric_exponential_recurrence_bound,

        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.finite_normalized_eLpNorm_sequence_bound,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.tendsto_eLpNorm_finite_measure,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.radius_normalized_norm_mono_exponent,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.inverse_power_eLpNorm_square,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.ofReal_integral_rpow_eq_eLpNorm_rpow,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.memLp_rpow_of_bounded_log,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.memLp_nonpos_power_of_log_measurable,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.inverse_norm_power_gain,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.positive_norm_power_gain,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.positive_power_eLpNorm_top_square,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.inverse_power_eLpNorm_top_square,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.eLpNorm_inverse_eq_inv_essInf,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.le_mul_essInf_of_inverse_norm_product,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.unshifted_bound_of_shifted_inverse_norm_products,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.mul_eLpNorm_inv_top_le_of_moment_product,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.weak_cutoff_sobolev_gain_of_energy_two_of_support,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.weak_cutoff_sobolev_gain_of_energy_two_of_support_of_bounded_log,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.volume_cthickening_le_of_subset_closedBall,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.scaled_weak_cutoff_sobolev_gain_two_of_bounded_log,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.weak_cutoff_sobolev_gain_of_energy_one_of_support,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.weak_cutoff_sobolev_gain_of_energy_one_of_support_of_bounded_log,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.measure_mul_abs_sub_average_le_of_bounded_weak_data_one,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.integrable_exp_abs_sub_average_one_of_density_mass,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.inverse_essential_bound_of_cutoff_power_energy,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.positive_norm_bound_of_cutoff_power_energy,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.inverse_essential_bound_two_of_cutoff_power_energy,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.positive_norm_bound_two_of_cutoff_power_energy,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.inverse_essential_bound_one_of_cutoff_power_energy,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.positive_essential_bound_one_of_cutoff_power_energy}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:letwin-local-moser-inputs, lem:letwin-bounded-jn-inputs}
  Supporting results for Letwin \cite{Letwin2026QuadraticKLS}, Lemma~2.2, from Gilbarg--Trudinger (2001), Theorem~8.18 for
  bounded data, by the Moser iteration.
  (i) Recurrences. If $x_{k+1}\le\exp(\frac{a^k}{p}(A+Bk))\,x_k$ with $0\le a<1$, then
  $x_k\le\exp((\frac{A}{1-a}+\frac{Ba}{(1-a)^2})/p)\,x_0$; the root form with multipliers $(C4^m)^{1/(p_0\kappa^m)}$ follows.
  (ii) Moser iteration. For measures $\mu_m$ dominating $\nu$, exponents $p_0\kappa^m$ ($\kappa>1$) and a physical radius
  exponent $a$ with $N/\kappa+a=N$, the one-step bounds
  $\|f\|_{p_0\kappa^{m+1},\mu_{m+1}}^{p_0\kappa^m}\le C4^mR^{-a}\|f\|_{p_0\kappa^m,\mu_m}^{p_0\kappa^m}$ give
  $\|f\|_{\infty,\nu}\le C'R^{-N/p_0}\|f\|_{p_0,\mu_0}$, and the same at every finite horizon.
  (iii) Endpoints. Given the cutoff power energies of $s\ge\delta$ on $\overline B(x_0,4R)$ under $FR^2\le\alpha\delta$, with $C$
  chosen before all centres, radii and data: $\|s^{-1}\|_{\infty,B(x_0,R)}\le C R^{-n/p_0}\|s^{-1}\|_{p_0,\overline B(x_0,2R)}$,
  and the positive bound $R^{-n/p}\|s\|_{p,B(x_0,2R)}\le CR^{-n/p_0}\|s\|_{p_0,B(x_0,3R)}$, for $n>2$ and $0<p<n/(n-2)$, and
  for $n=2$ and every finite $p>0$ with the physical exponent $a=2-2/g$; for $n=1$ direct one-step essential bounds.
  (iv) Products and shifts. Moment products $\int s^{p_0}\int s^{-p_0}\le(c(aR)^N)^2$ give the radius-free product
  $R^{-N/p_0}\|s\|_{p_0}\|s^{-1}\|_\infty\le C(ca^N)^{2/p_0}$; $\|s^{-1}\|_\infty=(\operatorname{ess\,inf}s)^{-1}$, and
  bounds for every shift $\delta>k$ give $A\|w\|_p\le C(\operatorname{ess\,inf}w+k)$ with $C<\infty$.
  (v) The case $n=1$. The kernel-one representation of bounded weak data and the density-mass bound give exponential
  integrability of $|u-u_\Omega|$ directly, without John--Nirenberg or the critical exponent.
  At a single fixed-compact regular-class witness, with all constants chosen per
  compact $S$ before every centre, radius, direction and cap, these estimates give the full-exponent same-witness products for $n>2$, $n=2$ and $n=1$, and
  for every $n>0$ the weak Harnack inequality for the deficit $w=M-v^{\top}\nabla^2u\,v\ge0$ a.e.\ on
  $\overline B(x_0,4R)\subseteq S$:
  $R^{-n/p}\|w\|_{p,B(x_0,2R)}\le C(p)\,(\operatorname{ess\,inf}_{B(x_0,R)}w+MBR^2/\alpha)$ for $0<p<n/(n-2)$ ($n>2$) and
  every finite $p>0$ ($n\le2$). These are not the full Theorem~8.18 for unbounded $W^{1,2}$ data or rough coefficients
  ($q>n$), a pointwise infimum or regular representative, oscillation decay, H\"older or $C^{2,\alpha}$ regularity, or the
  merely convex source class.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  (i) Summation of the exponents, $\sum_ka^k=\frac1{1-a}$ and $\sum_kka^k=\frac{a}{(1-a)^2}$. (ii) Radius normalisation by
  $N/\kappa+a=N$, then (i), finite-measure exponent monotonicity and the $L^p\to L^\infty$ limit. (iii) Dyadic cutoffs from
  $2R$ to $R$ (inverse) and $5R/2$ to $2R$ (positive), the weak Sobolev gains of
  Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-local-moser-inputs} and their $n=2$, $n=1$ forms, a backward geometric chain of exponents below $1$
  for the positive boost, then (ii). (iv) H\"older between the moment integrals and the inverse endpoint, an exact radius
  cancellation, and the limit $\delta\downarrow k$ by continuity of $C\cdot$ for finite $C$. (v) Segment integration with
  kernel $|z-x|^0=1$. At the regular-class witness one uses Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-bounded-jn-inputs} for $n\ge2$, (v) for $n=1$, and a case
  analysis on $n$ for the essential-infimum form.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Uniformly positive fixed finite rank-one frame]
  \label{lem:gt-uniform-rank-one-frame}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_uniform_positive_rank_one_frame,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.coefficient_le_cap,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_invertible_congruence_of_posDef}
  \leanok
  Gilbarg--Trudinger (2001), Lemma~17.13 (statement pp.~454--455, proof pp.~462--463), supporting Letwin
  \cite{Letwin2026QuadraticKLS}, Lemma~2.2. For $n>0$ and $0<\lambda<\Lambda$ there are finitely many unit vectors
  $\gamma_1,\dots,\gamma_N\in\mathbb R^n$, including every $e_i$ and every $(e_i+e_j)/\sqrt2$ ($i\ne j$), and $\lambda_*>0$
  with $\lambda_*<\Lambda$, all chosen before any matrix, such that every positive definite $A$ with
  $\lambda I\le A\le\Lambda I$ is $A=\sum_k\beta_k\gamma_k\gamma_k^{\top}$ with $\lambda_*\le\beta_k\le\Lambda$ for every $k$.
  Positive definite matrices of the same size are congruent, $A=SBS^{\top}$ with $S$ invertible (Eq.~(17.55)); a nonnegative
  combination of unit rank-one matrices with $A\le\Lambda I$ has every coefficient at most $\Lambda$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Transport the ordered-pair chart of a fixed positive definite center by the congruence to each $A$; its coefficients are
  positive on an open neighbourhood. Finitely many neighbourhoods cover the compact interval $[\lambda/2\,I,\Lambda I]$ in the
  ordinary entrywise topology; concatenate their normalized directions with the prescribed ones, represent
  $A-\varepsilon\sum_k\gamma_k\gamma_k^{\top}$ ($\varepsilon=\lambda/(2N)$) in one neighbourhood and add $\varepsilon$ to every
  coefficient. Testing $\Lambda I-A\succeq0$ against $\gamma_k$ bounds $\beta_k$ by $\Lambda$; representing $\lambda I$ gives
  $\lambda_*\le\lambda<\Lambda$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Scalar iteration lemma]
  \label{lem:gt-scalar-modulus-iteration}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.two_scale_modulus_bound,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.modulus_bound_of_scale_contraction,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_modulus_bound_of_scale_contraction}
  \leanok
  Gilbarg--Trudinger (2001), Lemma~8.23 (pp.~201--202). Let $0<\gamma,\tau<1$. There is $C=\max(\gamma^{-1},(1-\gamma)^{-1})$
  such that for every $\mu\in(0,1)$, with $\alpha=(1-\mu)\log\gamma/\log\tau>0$, all nondecreasing nonnegative $\omega,\sigma$ on
  $(0,R_0]$ with $\omega(\tau r)\le\gamma\,\omega(r)+\sigma(r)$ satisfy
  $\omega(R)\le C\bigl((R/R_0)^{\alpha}\omega(R_0)+\sigma(R^{\mu}R_0^{1-\mu})\bigr)$ for $0<R\le R_0$. A scalar lemma only; it is
  not an oscillation estimate for any equation.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Iterate the recurrence $k$ times with $\tau^{k+1}\le R/R_1<\tau^k$ and $R_1=R^{\mu}R_0^{1-\mu}$, then use monotonicity.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Essential infimum of a bounded deficit]
  \label{lem:essential-deficit-identity}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.essInf_ofReal_deficit_eq_sub_essSup}
  \leanok
  For a nonzero measure and a real function with $0\le f\le M$ a.e.,
  $\operatorname{ess\,inf}(M-f)=M-\operatorname{ess\,sup}f$ (as extended nonnegative values). Used for directional deficits as
  in Gilbarg--Trudinger (2001), \S17.4. No pointwise extremum or regular representative is asserted.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Monotone continuous image of the a.e.\ liminf and $\liminf(M-f)=M-\limsup f$ for bounded, cobounded $f$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Weak Harnack inequality for given bounded weak supersolutions]
  \label{lem:given-data-weak-harnack}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.bounded_weak_harnack_of_given_data,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.cutoff_power_data_of_weak_supersolution_on_set,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_weak_log_extension_with_energy_on_set}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:letwin-bounded-weak-harnack, lem:letwin-local-moser-inputs, lem:letwin-bounded-jn-inputs}
  Gilbarg--Trudinger (2001), Theorem~8.18 with (8.47)--(8.49), pp.~194--196, in the bounded symmetric pure-divergence case, for
  given data. For $n>0$ and $\alpha,\beta>0$ there is $C(p)>0$, chosen before every ball, coefficient matrix, function,
  derivative, bound and forcing, such that: if $w$ is globally bounded with weak derivatives $a$ in $L^2_{\mathrm{loc}}$, $A$ is
  a.e.\ measurable with $\alpha I\le A\le\beta I$ and $w\ge0$ a.e.\ on $\overline B(x_0,4R)$, $F\ge0$, and
  $-F\int\eta\le\int a^{\top}A\nabla\eta$ for every bounded nonnegative compactly supported weak test with function and
  derivative supported in $\overline B(x_0,4R)$, then
  $R^{-n/p}\|w\|_{L^p(B(x_0,2R))}\le C(p)\bigl(\operatorname{ess\,inf}_{B(x_0,R)}w+FR^2/\alpha\bigr)$ for every $p>0$ with
  $n\le2$ or $p<n/(n-2)$. The inequality holds at a single regular moment potential $u$ with all its regular-class data (convexity,
  normalization and pushforward, $C^1$ with Lipschitz gradient, Lipschitz right side, a.e.\ positive definite Hessian with the
  log-determinant identity and cap, weak Hessian tensor), with $\alpha,\beta,B$ and $C(p)$ chosen for each compact $S$
  before every center, radius, direction and cap, for every deficit $M-v^{\top}\nabla^2u\,v$ capped a.e.\ on
  $\overline B(x_0,4R)$, with forcing $MB$. Not the unbounded, nonsymmetric, lower-order or domain-only Theorem~8.18, and not an
  oscillation estimate.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The given-data cutoff power energies and one weak logarithm identified a.e.\ on the ball, the Morrey bound and
  John--Nirenberg ($n\ge2$) or the kernel-one representation ($n=1$), the Moser endpoints of
  Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-bounded-weak-harnack}, the moment-to-product bridge and the finite-constant shift removal.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Frame oscillation decay of a log-determinant matrix field]
  \label{lem:frame-log-det-oscillation}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.oscillation_of_fixed_frame_and_essential_harnack,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.pairwise_essential_deficit_comparison_of_fixed_frame,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.averaged_deficit_bound_of_essential_harnack,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.directional_deficit_le_sum_of_logdet,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.finite_essential_width_contraction,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.essential_width_power_of_quarter_contraction,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.power_modulus_of_quarter_scale_contraction}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:gt-uniform-rank-one-frame, lem:gt-scalar-modulus-iteration, lem:essential-deficit-identity}
  Rough-divergence form of Gilbarg--Trudinger (2001), Lemma~17.14 with the frame of Lemma~17.13. Let $P$ be a matrix field with
  $P\succ0$, $P\le K I$ and $\log\det P=V$ a.e.\ ($V$ Lipschitz), and let $\rho P^{-1}\in[\alpha,\beta]$ a.e.\ on $S$
  ($0\le\rho\le B$ on $S$) decompose in one fixed unit frame with coefficients in $[l_c,u_c]$. Assume an essential weak Harnack
  law at $p=1$ for the directional deficits $M-\gamma_k^{\top}P\gamma_k$. Then there are $0<\theta<1$ and $C>0$, chosen before
  every ball, such that the summed essential widths of the frame quadratic forms on $B(x_0,R)$ are at most $\theta$ times
  those on $B(x_0,4R)$ plus $C(R+R^2)$ whenever $\overline B(x_0,4R)\subseteq S$. A quarter-scale contraction then gives,
  through the scalar Lemma~8.23 at $\tau=1/4$, $\mu=1/2$, a power decay $Qr^{\eta}$ with $\eta,Q$ before every center and
  radius. Essential extrema only. The frame and the Harnack law are explicit premises; this is not the classical $C^4$
  Lemma~17.14.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The log-determinant supporting plane gives the pairwise deficit comparison; the $p=1$ Harnack law normalized by the ball
  mass bounds the averaged deficits; the finite width absorption contracts the summed widths.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Hölder representatives and $C^2$ from a continuous Hessian]
  \label{lem:holder-hessian-regularity}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_local_holder_representative_of_essential_power,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.ae_forall_open_real_essential_bounds,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.holderWith_dense_extension,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.glue_continuous_ae_representatives_on_balls,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_matrix_entry_holder_representatives,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.hasFDerivAt_of_lipschitz_continuous_ae_fderiv,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.contDiff_two_of_continuous_ae_hessian,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.everywhere_posDef_logdet_of_continuous_hessian_ae}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:frame-log-det-oscillation}
  The following holds without convexity assumptions.
  An a.e.\ bounded function whose essential widths on $B(x,r)$ are at most $Qr^{\eta}$ for every center of an open set $U$
  has a continuous Hölder representative on $U$. The proof uses a common good set built over a countable basis and a dense
  extension. Entrywise representatives of a matrix field follow from coordinate and normalized-pair quadratic forms.
  Continuous a.e.\ representatives on the balls $B(0,m+1)$ glue to one continuous function. A Lipschitz map whose derivative
  agrees a.e.\ with a continuous field is $C^1$ with that derivative (by shrinking mollifiers). A $C^1$ function with
  Lipschitz gradient and a continuous a.e.\ Hessian representative is $C^2$. If its Hessian is continuous, an a.e.\ positive
  definite log-determinant equation with cap holds at every point, by the quantitative determinant floor, the closed
  positive semidefinite cone and the support of Lebesgue measure.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Dense Hölder extension, open-overlap compatibility of continuous a.e.\ representatives, the locally uniform derivative
  limit theorem, and and the quantitative determinant floor
  \texttt{ae\_posSemidef\_hessianMatrix\_sub\_of\_log\_det}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Regular moment potential with Hölder Hessian]
  \label{lem:regular-moment-hessian-holder}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_regular_moment_potential_holder_hessian}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:given-data-weak-harnack, lem:frame-log-det-oscillation, lem:holder-hessian-regularity}
  Letwin, arXiv:2607.24164v1, Lemma~2.2, support in the strong class. Setting: $n>0$, smooth $\kappa$-uniformly convex $W$
  ($\kappa>0$), compact convex $K$ with nonempty interior, and the normalized isotropic law $1_K e^{-W}$. One convex potential
  $u$ keeps all of its regular-class data: Lipschitz bound, $\int e^{-u}=1$, $(\nabla u)_{\#}e^{-u}=\mu$, $C^1$ with a
  $16/\kappa$-Lipschitz gradient, Lipschitz right side, the a.e.\ positive definite Hessian with
  $\log\det\nabla^2u=-u+W(\nabla u)$ and cap $16/\kappa$, and the weak third derivative. The same $u$ is $C^2$ with continuous
  Hessian. Its Hessian is positive definite with the log-determinant equation and cap at every point. On every ball
  $B(0,s)$ all Hessian entries are Hölder with one exponent and constant chosen before the entries. Not Schauder/$C^\infty$
  regularity or the gradient diffeomorphism. The merely convex source class is not covered here; it is treated
  separately by a $\kappa$-free limit argument (Lemma~\ref{lem:convex-source-smooth-moment-potential}) producing a possibly different potential, and no paper-target
  certificate is asserted.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  At the regular moment potential with weak Hessian tensor, the given-data weak Harnack law gives, per compact set, the
  frame oscillation decay of Lemma~\ref{lem:frame-log-det-oscillation} with the fixed frame of Gilbarg--Trudinger,
  Lemma~17.13. The resulting power decay yields Hölder representatives of every Hessian entry, which glue to one continuous
  representative. Lemma~\ref{lem:holder-hessian-regularity} then gives $C^2$ and the everywhere equation.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Averaged-inverse coefficients of the log-determinant]
  \label{lem:log-det-averaged-inverse}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.log_det_sub_eq_integral_trace,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.log_det_sub_eq_integral_trace_of_posDef,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.log_det_sub_eq_sum_averaged_inverse,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.log_det_sub_eq_sum_averaged_inverse_of_posDef,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.contDiffAt_log_det,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.contDiffAt_log_det_of_posDef,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.abs_inv_sub_inv_entry_le,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.holderWith_inverse_entries,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.holderWith_averaged_inverse_entries,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_uniform_inverse_entry_bound_on_compact,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_holder_averaged_inverse_entries_of_posDef,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.holderWith_probability_mean,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.holderWith_unit_interval_mean}
  \leanok
  Gilbarg--Trudinger (2001), (17.56) and the coefficients of Lemma~17.16. For real square matrices with
  $\det(A+t(B-A))\ne0$ on $[0,1]$,
  $\log\det B-\log\det A=\int_0^1\operatorname{tr}((A+t(B-A))^{-1}(B-A))\,dt
  =\sum_{i,j}\bigl(\int_0^1(A+t(B-A))^{-1}_{ij}\,dt\bigr)(B-A)_{ji}$; for positive definite endpoints the nonsingularity of
  the segment is derived, and $M\mapsto\log\det M$ is $C^k$ at every nonsingular matrix. Inverse entries satisfy
  $|A^{-1}_{ij}-B^{-1}_{ij}|\le n^2L^2d$. On a compact space, positive definite fields $P,Q$ with $(C,r)$-Hölder entries,
  $r>0$, have a uniform inverse cap $L>0$ (derived), and every averaged inverse entry
  $x\mapsto\int_0^1((1-t)P(x)+tQ(x))^{-1}_{ij}\,dt$ is $(n^2L^2C,r)$-Hölder; Hölder moduli survive probability and unit-interval
  averages. Prerequisites for the linear Schauder estimates only; no Schauder estimate is claimed.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Fundamental theorem of calculus with Jacobi's formula, continuity of the matrix inverse at nonsingular matrices, the
  resolvent identity $A^{-1}-B^{-1}=A^{-1}(B-A)B^{-1}$, and compactness of $X\times[0,1]$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Gradient image and inverse of a strictly convex function]
  \label{lem:strict-convex-gradient-inverse}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.mem_range_gradient_iff_tilt_min,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.convex_range_gradient,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.isOpen_range_gradient,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_linear_lower_bound_of_strict_min,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_min_of_linear_lower_bound,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.isOpenMap_gradient_of_strictConvexOn,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_gradient_homeomorph_range,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.contDiffOn_invFun_of_injective_of_hasFDerivAt_equiv,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.hasFDerivAt_invFun_of_injective,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_gradient_deriv_equiv_of_posDef,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.contDiffOn_invFun_gradient_of_posDef,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.fderiv_invFun_gradient_comp_fderiv}
  \leanok
  For a differentiable convex $u$, $p\in\operatorname{range}\nabla u$ if and only if $u-\langle p,\cdot\rangle$ attains its
  minimum. If $u$ is strictly convex on a proper space, such tilts grow linearly, so the range of $\nabla u$ is convex and open;
  in finite dimension $\nabla u$ is an open map and a homeomorphism onto its range (compactness and coercive tilts; no invariance
  of domain and no invertible Hessian). If $\nabla^2u(x)$ is positive definite, $D\nabla u(x)$ is an isomorphism whose
  standard-basis matrix is $\nabla^2u(x)^{\top}$. For $C^{k+1}$ strictly convex $u$, $k\ne0$, with positive definite Hessian
  everywhere, the inverse of $\nabla u$ is $C^k$ on the range (inverse function theorem), and
  $D(\nabla u)^{-1}(\nabla u(x))\circ D\nabla u(x)=\mathrm{id}$. No regularity of $u$ is bootstrapped.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Supporting planes and Fermat's rule; linear growth from a strict minimum on the unit sphere; coercive minimization; the
  closed image of a compact set; the local inverse of the inverse function theorem agrees with \texttt{invFun} near each image
  point.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Gradient range of a transporting potential]
  \label{lem:transport-gradient-range}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Probability.support_map_eq_closure_range,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.support_restrict_eq_of_convex,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.support_withDensity_eq_of_ae_ne_zero,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.support_indicator_exp_neg_eq,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.MomentMeasure.range_gradient_eq_interior_of_map_support,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.MomentMeasure.range_gradient_eq_interior_of_tilted_map,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.MomentMeasure.range_gradient_eq_interior_of_map_gradient,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.MomentMeasure.exists_gradient_homeomorph_interior_of_map_gradient,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.MomentMeasure.exists_regular_inverse_of_map_gradient}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:strict-convex-gradient-inverse}
  For an open-positive measure, the support of a continuous pushforward is the closure of the range, and $1_Ke^{-W}\mu$ has
  support $K$ for closed convex $K$ with nonempty interior. Let $u$ on $\mathbb R^n$, $n>0$, be $C^1$, convex and Lipschitz with
  $\int e^{-u}=1$, and let $\nabla u$ transport $e^{-u}dx$ to $1_Ke^{-W}dx$ ($K$ compact convex with nonempty interior, $W$
  continuous). Then $u$ is strictly convex (Caffarelli's argument, \texttt{strictConvexOn\_of\_map\_gradient}),
  $\operatorname{range}\nabla u=\operatorname{int}K$, $\nabla u$ is a homeomorphism $\mathbb R^n\to\operatorname{int}K$, and
  under $C^{k+1}$ regularity with positive definite Hessian it has a $C^k$ inverse on $\operatorname{int}K$. The potential is
  a hypothesis here.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The range is open and convex with closure equal to the support $K$ of the transported law, so it equals
  $\operatorname{int}K$; Lemma~\ref{lem:strict-convex-gradient-inverse} gives the homeomorphism and the inverse.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Regular moment potential with a gradient inverse]
  \label{lem:regular-moment-gradient-inverse}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_regular_moment_potential_holder_hessian_inverse}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:regular-moment-hessian-holder, lem:transport-gradient-range}
  Letwin, arXiv:2607.24164v1, Lemma~2.2, support in the strong class: $n>0$, smooth $\kappa$-uniformly convex $W$ ($\kappa>0$),
  compact convex $K$ with nonempty interior and the normalized isotropic law $1_Ke^{-W}$. For the same $u$, $L$, $LF$ and $T$,
  every field of Lemma~\ref{lem:regular-moment-hessian-holder} in the same order, followed by a homeomorphism
  $\nabla u:\mathbb R^n\to\operatorname{int}K$ and a $C^1$ left inverse $\Psi$ of $\nabla u$ with $\nabla u(\Psi(y))=y$ on
  $\operatorname{int}K$. Not Schauder/$C^\infty$ regularity, a smooth diffeomorphism or the merely convex source class, so
  Lemma~2.2 remains partial.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Take the potential of Lemma~\ref{lem:regular-moment-hessian-holder}; its transport composed with the law equation,
  its $C^2$ field and its everywhere positive definite Hessian feed Lemma~\ref{lem:transport-gradient-range} at $k=1$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Smooth regular moment potential]
  \label{lem:regular-moment-smooth}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.locHolderDeriv_succ_succ_succ_of_linear_equation,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.coordinate_linear_equation_of_mongeAmpere,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.locHolderDeriv_all_of_mongeAmpere,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.contDiff_infty_of_mongeAmpere,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.contDiff_infty_of_mongeAmpere_one,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_regular_moment_potential_smooth}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:regular-moment-gradient-inverse, lem:log-det-averaged-inverse}
  Letwin, arXiv:2607.24164v1, Lemma~2.2 in the strong regular class: $n>0$, smooth globally
  $\kappa$-uniformly convex $W$ ($\kappa>0$), compact convex $K$ with nonempty interior, and
  normalized isotropic law $1_K e^{-W}$. The same $u,L,LF,T$ retain every field of
  Lemma~\ref{lem:regular-moment-gradient-inverse}, and additionally $u\in C^\infty(\mathbb R^n)$,
  every finite derivative is locally H\"older, and $\nabla u$ has a smooth left inverse $\Psi$ on
  $\operatorname{int}K$ with $\nabla u(\Psi(y))=y$ there. Together with the retained homeomorphism,
  this is a smooth gradient diffeomorphism onto $\operatorname{int}K$.
  The literal source class, where $W$ is merely convex near $\overline K$, is not covered by this
  lemma; it is treated separately by a $\kappa$-free limit argument (Lemma~\ref{lem:convex-source-smooth-moment-potential}) producing a possibly different
  potential. No paper-target certificate is asserted.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  For $n\ge2$, the first difference-quotient step gives the same potential in
  $C^{3,\alpha}_{\mathrm{loc}}$. Jacobi's formula needs only $C^3$ and gives the linear equation
  for each coordinate derivative, with inverse-Hessian coefficients and drift
  $-\nabla W(\nabla u)$. Theorem~6.2 and the method of Lemma~17.16 of Gilbarg--Trudinger
  (Classics 2001, pp.~90--92 and 463--464) yield the iterated interior Schauder gain;
  local H\"older calculus closes the induction through every finite order.
  For $n=1$, $u''=\exp(-u+W(u'))$ is a smooth autonomous system for $(u,u')$.
  The regular inverse theorem applied at $k=\infty$ supplies the smooth inverse, without
  changing the potential or its transport.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Smooth moment-map Stein kernel]
  \label{lem:regular-smooth-moment-stein}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_regular_smooth_moment_stein_kernel}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:regular-moment-smooth, lem:letwin-hessian-kernel}
  Letwin, arXiv:2607.24164v1, Lemma~2.7 in the strong regular class, every $n>0$.
  At the same smooth normalized transporting potential $u$, a measurable everywhere left
  inverse $\Psi$ of $\nabla u$ is smooth on $\operatorname{int}K$ and a right inverse there.
  Then $\tau=\nabla^2u\circ\Psi$ is a symmetric Stein kernel of $\mu$, positive definite with
  smooth entries on $\operatorname{int}K$, and $\tau(\nabla u(y))=\nabla^2u(y)$ at every $y$.
  The literal merely convex source class and the separate Hilbert--Schmidt estimate of
  Theorem~2.5 are not inferred.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Choose the potential once from Lemma~\ref{lem:regular-moment-smooth}. A measurable left
  inverse agrees on $\operatorname{int}K$ with its smooth inverse. The given-potential
  Hessian--Stein theorem applies to this same $u$; positivity, smooth entries and the exact
  coupling follow from its everywhere Hessian data and the left-inverse identity.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Trace identities for noise shifts, involutions and conjugations]
  \label{lem:matrix-noise-trace-identities}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.trace_transpose_mul_shift_one,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.trace_transpose_mul_right_involution,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.trace_symmetric_conjugation,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.trace_transpose_conjugation_sq}
  \leanok
  For real square matrices over any finite index type of cardinality $n$:
  $\operatorname{tr}((A+t I)^{\top}(A+t I))=\operatorname{tr}(A^{\top}A)+2t\operatorname{tr}A+nt^2$;
  $\operatorname{tr}((AQ)^{\top}AQ)=\operatorname{tr}(A^{\top}A)$ for symmetric $Q$ with $Q^2=I$;
  $\operatorname{tr}(SHS)=\operatorname{tr}(S^2H)$; and $\operatorname{tr}((SHS)^{\top}SHS)=\operatorname{tr}(S^2HS^2H)$ for
  symmetric $S,H$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Expand the products and use cyclicity of the trace.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Spectral geometry of a symmetric invertible matrix]
  \label{lem:symmetric-matrix-spectral-geometry}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.symmetric_matrix_spectral_geometry}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:letwin-mapped-covariance}
  Let $M\in\mathbb R^{n\times n}$ be symmetric and invertible, $B=\sqrt{M^2}$, $S=\sqrt B$ and $Q=\operatorname{sign}M$
  (continuous functional calculus). Then $B$ and $S$ are positive semidefinite, $Q$ is symmetric, $B^2=M^2$, $S^2=B$,
  $SQS=M$, $Q^2=I$, $S$ is invertible, the Euclidean operator of $Q$ is self-adjoint, and $S$ defines a self-adjoint
  continuous linear equivalence of $\mathbb R^n$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The sign factorization gives $S^2=B$, $SQS=M$ and $Q^2=I$; invertibility of $S$ follows from $\det M\ne0$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Invertible symmetric approximation]
  \label{lem:symmetric-invertible-approximation}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_symmetric_isUnit_matrix_sequence}
  \leanok
  Every symmetric real matrix $A$ over a finite index type is the limit of the symmetric invertible matrices
  $A-r_kI$, where $0<r_k<1/(k+1)$ avoids the finite real spectrum of $A$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The spectrum is finite, so each interval $(0,1/(k+1))$ contains an admissible $r_k$; then $r_k\to0$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Trace against a mean-identity matrix field]
  \label{lem:matrix-trace-mean-identity}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.integral_trace_mul_of_mean_one}
  \leanok
  For a measure $\rho$ and a matrix field $H$ with integrable entries and $\int H_{ij}\,d\rho=\delta_{ij}$, and every
  matrix $B$, the function $\operatorname{tr}(BH)$ is integrable and $\int\operatorname{tr}(BH)\,d\rho=\operatorname{tr}B$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Linearity of the integral over the finite trace sum.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Hilbert--Schmidt sum of a symmetric operator]
  \label{lem:symmetric-hilbert-schmidt-trace}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.sum_inner_apply_sq_eq_trace_comp_of_isSymmetric}
  \leanok
  For a symmetric continuous linear operator $A$ on a real inner product space and any finite orthonormal basis $(b_i)$,
  $\sum_{i,j}\langle Ab_i,b_j\rangle^2=\operatorname{tr}(A\circ A)$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Parseval in each column and symmetry of $A$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Gradient of an affine precomposition]
  \label{lem:gradient-affine-precomposition}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.gradient_comp_affine}
  \leanok
  For complete real inner product spaces, a continuous linear $S:E\to F$, $z\in F$ and $f$ differentiable at $Sx+z$,
  $\nabla\bigl(y\mapsto f(Sy+z)\bigr)(x)=S^{*}\nabla f(Sx+z)$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Chain rule and the defining property of the adjoint.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Algebra of the matrix action]
  \label{lem:matrix-action-algebra}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Probability.Stein.action_eq_toEuclideanLin,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.Stein.action_mul,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.Stein.action_add,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.Stein.action_smul,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.Stein.action_one,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.Stein.action_basis_apply,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.Stein.sum_norm_action_basis_sq_eq_trace_transpose_mul}
  \leanok
  The Euclidean action of real $n\times n$ matrices agrees with the Euclidean linear map of the matrix and is
  multiplicative, additive, homogeneous and unital; its value on the $i$th basis vector has $j$th coordinate $A_{ji}$, and
  $\sum_i\|Ae_i\|^2=\operatorname{tr}(A^{\top}A)$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Coordinate computations with matrix--vector products.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The Hessian action is square integrable]
