  \label{lem:hessian-action-l2}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Probability.Stein.memLp_hessian_action_of_lipschitz_gradient}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:hessian-entry-measurable-bounded}
  If $\nabla u$ is $G$-Lipschitz on $\mathbb R^n$, then $y\mapsto\nabla^2u(y)\,v$ lies in $L^2(\mu)$ for every finite measure
  $\mu$ and every $v$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Each Hessian entry is measurable and bounded by $G$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Quadratic forms of the matrix action]
  \label{lem:quadratic-action-identities}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Probability.Stein.quadratic_matrix_sum_eq_inner_action,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.Stein.inner_action_sandwich,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.Stein.latent_field_eq_action_conjugation,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.Stein.variance_quadratic_map_sandwich}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:matrix-action-algebra}
  For real $n\times n$ matrices: $\sum_{i,j}A_{ij}x_ix_j=\langle Ax,x\rangle$; for symmetric $S$,
  $\langle Q(Sx),Sx\rangle=\langle(SQS)x,x\rangle$ and the variance of $x\mapsto\langle Qx,x\rangle$ under the image of $\mu$
  by $S$ equals the variance of $x\mapsto\langle(SQS)x,x\rangle$ under $\mu$; and if a continuous linear equivalence $e$ has
  the operator of $S$ and is self-adjoint, then $e(H(e^{*}v))+tv=(SHS+tI)v$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Coordinates, multiplicativity of the action and the change of variables for variances.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Spectral sign/noise identity]
  \label{lem:spectral-sign-noise-identity}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.sum_norm_action_spectral_sign_noise}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:symmetric-matrix-spectral-geometry, lem:matrix-action-algebra, lem:matrix-noise-trace-identities}
  For symmetric invertible $M$ with $B=\sqrt{M^2}$, $S=\sqrt B$, $Q=\operatorname{sign}M$, every symmetric $H$ and every real
  $t$:
  $\sum_i\|(SHS+tI)Qe_i\|^2=\operatorname{tr}(BHBH)+2t\operatorname{tr}(BH)+nt^2$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Write the sum as $\operatorname{tr}(X^{\top}X)$ for $X=(SHS+tI)Q$, remove the involution $Q$, expand the noise shift and
  use $S^2=B$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Integrated spectral noise energy]
  \label{lem:spectral-noise-energy-integral}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.sum_integral_spectral_noise_energy_le}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:spectral-sign-noise-identity, lem:matrix-trace-mean-identity, lem:symmetric-matrix-spectral-geometry}
  Let $\rho$ and $\gamma$ be probability measures on arbitrary measurable spaces and $H$ a measurable matrix field that is
  $\rho$-a.e.\ symmetric, has $\rho$-integrable entries with $\int H\,d\rho=I$, and satisfies, as an input,
  $\int\operatorname{tr}(B'HB'H)\,d\rho\le2\operatorname{tr}(B'^2)$ with integrability for every symmetric $B'$. Then for
  symmetric invertible $M$, with $B,S,Q$ as above and every $t$, each $\|(SHS+tI)Qe_i\|^2$ is $\rho$-integrable and
  $\sum_i\int\|(SH(y)S+tI)Qe_i\|^2\,d(\rho\otimes\gamma)(y,z)\le2\operatorname{tr}(M^2)+2t\operatorname{tr}B+nt^2$. No
  log-concavity or Hessian hypothesis is used.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Integrate the pointwise identity, use the mean-identity trace and the trace input with $B'=B$, and project the product
  measure to its first factor.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Quadratic forms of a fourth-moment vector]
  \label{lem:quadratic-form-fourth-moment}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Probability.memLp_quadratic_of_memLp_four}
  \leanok
  If $f\in L^4(\mu)$ takes values in a real inner product space and $A$ is continuous linear, then
  $\langle Af,f\rangle\in L^2(\mu)$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  H\"older with exponents $(4,4,2)$ and $|\langle Af,f\rangle|\le\|A\|\,\|f\|^2$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Variance continuity of finite linear combinations]
  \label{lem:finite-combination-variance-continuity}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Probability.tendsto_variance_finite_linear_combination}
  \leanok
  For a probability measure and finitely many real $L^2$ functions $f_i$, if $c_{k,i}\to b_i$ for every $i$, then
  $\operatorname{Var}(\sum_ic_{k,i}f_i)\to\operatorname{Var}(\sum_ib_if_i)$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Represent the combinations in $L^2$ and use continuity of the $L^2$ variance.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Variance continuity of quadratic matrix forms]
  \label{lem:quadratic-matrix-variance-continuity}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Probability.tendsto_variance_quadratic_matrix_forms}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:finite-combination-variance-continuity}
  For a probability law on $\mathbb R^n$ with finite fourth moment and matrices $A_k\to A$,
  $\operatorname{Var}(\sum_{i,j}(A_k)_{ij}x_ix_j)\to\operatorname{Var}(\sum_{i,j}A_{ij}x_ix_j)$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The products $x_ix_j$ are in $L^2$ by H\"older; apply the finite-combination continuity.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Letwin: singular extension (Lemma A.2, stronger form)]
  \label{lem:letwin-singular-extension}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Probability.symmetric_matrix_variance_bound_of_isUnit}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:symmetric-invertible-approximation, lem:quadratic-matrix-variance-continuity}
  Let $\mu$ be a probability law on $\mathbb R^n$ (every $n$) with finite fourth moment and $C$ any real constant. If
  $\operatorname{Var}_\mu(\sum_{i,j}B_{ij}x_ix_j)\le C\operatorname{tr}(B^2)$ for every symmetric invertible $B$, then the same
  bound holds for every symmetric $B$. This is a stronger form of Letwin \cite{Letwin2026QuadraticKLS}, Lemma~A.2 (no
  isotropy or log-concavity, arbitrary $C$), proved through $B-r_kI$ with $r_k$ outside the spectrum rather than the source's
  $M+\varepsilon P_0$. It does not establish the bound.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Approximate $B$ by invertible symmetric matrices and pass to the limit on both sides.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Centered noise does not decrease quadratic variance]
  \label{lem:centered-noise-quadratic-variance}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Probability.quadratic_variance_le_centered_noise_convolution}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:quadratic-form-fourth-moment}
  For probability laws $\mu,\nu$ on a complete separable real inner product space with finite fourth moments, $\nu$ centered,
  and continuous linear $A$, $\operatorname{Var}_\mu\langle Ax,x\rangle\le\operatorname{Var}_{\mu*\nu}\langle Ax,x\rangle$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Decompose the variance on the product; the noise average equals the quadratic form plus a constant.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Fourth moments of Gaussian, smoothed and linear-image laws]
  \label{lem:gaussian-fourth-moments}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Probability.Gaussian.memLp_four,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.GaussianSmoothing.smoothedLaw_memLp_four,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.memLp_four_map_linear}
  \leanok
  On a finite-dimensional real inner product space: \texttt{Gaussian.law s} ($s>0$) has a fourth moment; if a probability
  measure $\mu$ has a fourth moment, so do its Gaussian smoothing and its image under any continuous linear map.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Exponential moments of the Gaussian; the smoothing is the image of a product.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Translated Gaussian directional identity]
  \label{lem:gaussian-translated-directional-identity}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Probability.Gaussian.translated_directional_identity_of_bounded_lipschitz_test}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:gaussian-directional-identity}
  For $s>0$, a bounded globally Lipschitz $h\in C^1$ and $a,v\in\mathbb R^n$, under $z\sim$ \texttt{Gaussian.law s}:
  $\int\langle z,v\rangle h(a+z)=(2s)^{-1}\int D_vh(a+z)$, both integrands being integrable.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Apply the Gaussian directional identity to $z\mapsto h(a+z)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Product latent map onto the smoothed linear image]
  \label{lem:gaussian-product-latent-map}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.GaussianSmoothing.gaussian_product_latent_map}
  \leanok
  If $\rho$ is a probability measure with $g_\#\rho=\mu$ ($g$ measurable), $S$ is continuous linear and $s>0$, then
  $(\rho\otimes\mathrm{Gaussian.law}\,s)$ pushed by $(y,z)\mapsto S(g(y))+z$ is the Gaussian smoothing of $S_\#\mu$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Images of product measures and the convolution as the image of a product.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Smooth convex potential of a smoothed linear image]
  \label{lem:gaussian-smoothed-linear-image-potential}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.GaussianSmoothing.exists_convex_smooth_potential_smoothed_linearEquiv}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:borell-logconcave-density}
  Let $\mu$ be a log-concave probability law on a finite-dimensional real inner product space with full affine support, $S$ a
  continuous linear equivalence and $s>0$. Then the Gaussian smoothing of $S_\#\mu$ is $e^{-W}dx$ (tilted Lebesgue measure)
  for some $W\in C^\infty$ convex, and the smoothed probability law is log-concave.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The image is log-concave with full affine support, so its support has nonempty interior and it has a Borell density;
  its Gaussian smoothing has a smooth convex potential.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Directional and affine source Stein identities]
  \label{lem:source-directional-affine-stein}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.source_directional_stein_identity_of_bounded_lipschitz_test,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.source_affine_stein_identity_of_bounded_lipschitz_test}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:weak-source-stein-identity, lem:gradient-affine-precomposition}
  Let $u\in C^1$ be globally Lipschitz with Lipschitz gradient and $\int e^{-u}=1$, $\nu=e^{-u}dx$. For bounded globally
  Lipschitz $C^1$ tests $f$ and every direction $v$:
  $\int\langle\nabla u,v\rangle f(\nabla u)\,d\nu=\int\langle v,\nabla^2u\,\nabla f(\nabla u)\rangle\,d\nu$; and for a continuous
  linear $S$ and $z$, with $h$ bounded Lipschitz $C^1$,
  $\int\langle S\nabla u,v\rangle h(S\nabla u+z)\,d\nu=\int\langle S^{*}v,\nabla^2u\,S^{*}\nabla h(S\nabla u+z)\rangle\,d\nu$; all
  integrands integrable.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Linear combinations of the coordinate source Stein identity, and the affine gradient chain.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Gaussian product source Stein identity]
  \label{lem:gaussian-product-source-stein}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.GaussianSmoothing.gaussian_product_source_stein_pairings_integrable,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.GaussianSmoothing.gaussian_product_source_stein_identity}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:hessian-action-l2, lem:gaussian-translated-directional-identity, lem:source-directional-affine-stein, lem:gaussian-fourth-moments}
  With $u$ as above, $s>0$, $P=\nu\otimes\mathrm{Gaussian.law}\,s$, a continuous linear $S$, $Z(y,z)=S\nabla u(y)+z$, a bounded
  Lipschitz $C^1$ test $h$ and $v$: the latent fields are in $L^2(P)$, the source and noise pairings are integrable, and
  $\int\langle Z,v\rangle h(Z)\,dP=\int\bigl(\langle S^{*}v,\nabla^2u\,S^{*}\nabla h(Z)\rangle+(2s)^{-1}D_vh(Z)\bigr)dP$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Split $\langle Z,v\rangle$; use the affine source Stein identity in $y$ and the translated Gaussian identity in $z$, and
  Fubini.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Compact dual bound for the Gaussian product coupling]
  \label{lem:gaussian-product-compact-dual}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.GaussianSmoothing.gaussian_product_compact_dual_bound}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:gaussian-product-source-stein, lem:gaussian-product-latent-map, lem:compact-source-dual-bound, lem:letwin-weak-hessian-identification}
  With $u$ as above, $\nabla u_\#\nu=\mu$, $S$ continuous linear, $s>0$ and $\nu_s$ the Gaussian smoothing of $S_\#\mu$:
  for every $v$ and every smooth compactly supported $h$,
  $\bigl(\int\langle x,v\rangle h\,d\nu_s\bigr)^2\le\int\|S\nabla^2u\,S^{*}v+(2s)^{-1}v\|^2\,dP\cdot\int\|Dh\|^2\,d\nu_s$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The product source identity gives a latent source identity for the latent field; Cauchy--Schwarz in $L^2(P)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Removal of a vanishing noise error]
  \label{lem:gaussian-noise-limit}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.GaussianSmoothing.le_of_forall_inverse_noise_quadratic_error}
  \leanok
  If $a\le b+c(2s)^{-1}+d((2s)^{-1})^2$ for every $s>0$, then $a\le b$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Let $s\to\infty$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Gaussian smoothing does not decrease quadratic variance]
  \label{lem:gaussian-smoothing-quadratic-variance}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.GaussianSmoothing.variance_quadratic_le_smoothedLaw}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:centered-noise-quadratic-variance, lem:gaussian-fourth-moments}
  For a probability law with finite fourth moment on a finite-dimensional real inner product space, continuous linear $A$
  and $s>0$, the variance of $\langle Ax,x\rangle$ is at most its variance under the Gaussian smoothing.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The Gaussian is centered with a fourth moment.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Smoothed quadratic variance via the $H^{-1}$ inequality]
  \label{lem:gaussian-smoothed-quadratic-h-minus-one}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.GaussianSmoothing.gaussian_smoothed_quadratic_variance_le_latent_energy}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:gaussian-product-compact-dual, lem:gaussian-smoothed-linear-image-potential, lem:quadratic-form-fourth-moment, lem:gaussian-fourth-moments}
  Let $\mu$ be log-concave with full affine support and $\mu=\nabla u_\#\nu$ for $u$ as above. For a continuous linear
  equivalence $S$, a self-adjoint $Q$ and $s>0$, the variance of $\langle Qx,x\rangle$ under the Gaussian smoothing of
  $S_\#\mu$ is at most $4\sum_i\int\|S\nabla^2u\,S^{*}Qe_i+(2s)^{-1}Qe_i\|^2\,dP$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The smoothed law is $e^{-W}dx$ with $W$ smooth and convex; apply the $H^{-1}$ variance inequality
  (\texttt{variance\_le\_sum\_of\_partial\_dual\_bounds}) with the compact dual bounds for the partial derivatives
  $2\langle x,Qe_i\rangle$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Letwin Proposition~2.8 for log-concave laws]
  \label{thm:letwin-h-minus-one-variance}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.variance_le_sum_dualNorm_sq,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.variance_le_sum_dualNorm_sq_of_conjecture,
        FunctionalInequalities.Energy.Dual.integral_eq_zero_of_dualNorm_ne_top,
        FunctionalInequalities.Energy.Dual.dualNorm_le_of_variance_le,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.IsBorellDensity.exists_smooth_convex_lower_approximation}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:letwin-stein-dual, conj:kls}
  Letwin \cite{Letwin2026QuadraticKLS}, Proposition~2.8 (Barthe--Klartag, Proposition~10), displays (2.19)--(2.20).
  Let $\mu$ be a log-concave probability law on $\R^n$ whose affine support is $\R^n$, and let $f$ be locally
  Lipschitz with $f,\partial_if\in L^2(\mu)$, where $\partial_if$ is the Fr\'echet partial derivative. Then
  \[
    \Var_\mu f\le\sum_{i=1}^n\norm{\partial_if}_{H^{-1}(\mu)}^2,
  \]
  with the extended $H^{-1}$ norm of Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-stein-dual} over all locally Lipschitz unit-energy tests.
  The source premise $\int\partial_if\dd\mu=0$ is not assumed: a finite $H^{-1}$ norm forces it, and otherwise the
  right side is infinite. Full affine support is needed: for a Gaussian law on $\{x_n=0\}$ and $f=x_1+|x_n|$, $f$ is
  nowhere Fr\'echet differentiable on the support, so every ambient partial derivative vanishes there, while
  $\Var f=1$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Approximate $\mu$ from below by laws $e^{-W_k}dx$ with smooth convex $W_k$ (mollified Lipschitz envelopes of the
  potential) whose normalizing masses tend to $1$ and whose second moments are bounded. The KLS bound gives uniform
  Poincar\'e constants for these laws, which control the transfer of dual norms; the convex $C^2$ case applies to
  each law, and smooth approximation of $f$ with centered partial derivatives passes to the limit.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Restricted exponential densities are log-concave]
  \label{lem:restricted-exp-logconcave}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.isBorellDensity_of_eq_restricted_exp,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.logConcave_of_eq_restricted_exp,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.logConcave_of_eq_regularClass}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:borell-logconcave-density}
  If a probability law $\mu$ on a finite-dimensional space has density $1_Ke^{-W}$ with respect to Lebesgue measure,
  with $K$ closed convex and $W$ continuous convex, then $1_Ke^{-W}$ is a Borell density of $\mu$, so $\mu$ is
  log-concave; in particular every regular-class probability law ($W-\tfrac\kappa2\|\cdot\|^2$ convex, $\kappa>0$) is
  log-concave.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Upper semicontinuity, log-concavity of the restricted density, and the Borell characterization.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Operator and matrix forms of the quadratic bound]
  \label{lem:letwin-operator-matrix-identification}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.letwinQuadraticBound_of_matrix_variance_bound}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:symmetric-hilbert-schmidt-trace, lem:quadratic-action-identities, lem:bkl-third-cumulant-conditional}
  For every measure $\mu$ on $\mathbb R^n$: if $\operatorname{Var}_\mu(\sum_{i,j}M_{ij}x_ix_j)\le8\operatorname{tr}(M^2)$ for every
  symmetric $M$, then $\mu$ satisfies \texttt{LetwinQuadraticBound}.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Take the standard matrix of a self-adjoint operator; its quadratic form and Hilbert--Schmidt sum are the matrix ones.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Letwin: constant 8 for the regular class]
  \label{thm:letwin-regular-class-constant-eight}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.regularClass_matrix_variance_le_eight,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.regularClass_letwinQuadraticBound}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:letwin-regular-moment-weak-stein-coupling, lem:restricted-exp-logconcave, lem:gaussian-smoothed-quadratic-h-minus-one, lem:spectral-noise-energy-integral, lem:quadratic-action-identities, lem:letwin-singular-extension, lem:gaussian-smoothing-quadratic-variance, lem:gaussian-noise-limit, lem:symmetric-matrix-spectral-geometry, lem:letwin-operator-matrix-identification}
  Let $n>0$, $K\subset\mathbb R^n$ compact convex with nonempty interior, $W\in C^\infty$ with $W-\tfrac\kappa2\|\cdot\|^2$
  convex ($\kappa>0$), and $\mu=1_Ke^{-W}dx$ an isotropic probability law. Then
  $\operatorname{Var}_\mu(\sum_{i,j}M_{ij}x_ix_j)\le8\operatorname{tr}(M^2)$ for every symmetric $M$, and $\mu$ satisfies
  \texttt{LetwinQuadraticBound}. This is the regular-class case of Letwin \cite{Letwin2026QuadraticKLS}, Theorem~1.2,
  under exactly the stated hypotheses.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Fix the weak Stein witness $u$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:letwin-regular-moment-weak-stein-coupling}; its bounded
  gradient pushes the normalized law $\rho=e^{-u}dx$ to $\mu$. The given law $\mu$ is log-concave
  (Lemma~\ref{lem:restricted-exp-logconcave}), has full affine support by isotropy and has a finite fourth moment as the
  image of $\rho$ under the bounded gradient. By Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-singular-extension} it suffices to treat invertible
  $M$. With $S=\sqrt{|M|}$ and $Q=\operatorname{sign}M$, put $\eta=S_\#\mu$: the variance of $\langle Qx,x\rangle$ under
  $\eta$ equals that of $\sum_{i,j}M_{ij}x_ix_j$ under $\mu$ and is at most its variance under the Gaussian smoothing of
  $\eta$. Bound the smoothed variance by $4$ times the latent energy, evaluate the energy by the spectral noise identity
  and the trace bound of the witness, and let the noise parameter tend to infinity.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Letwin: regular approximation]
  \label{lem:letwin-regular-approximation}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.exists_regular_approximation,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.exists_regular_approximation_eps,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.exists_regularClass_seq_of_compact,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.Suspension.exists_compact_moment_approximation}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:bkl-regular-approximation, lem:bkl-isotropic-reduction, lem:restricted-exp-logconcave}
  Letwin \cite{Letwin2026QuadraticKLS}, Lemma~A.1. Let $\mu$ be an isotropic log-concave probability measure on
  $\mathbb R^n$. There are isotropic log-concave probability measures $\eta_j=e^{-V_j}1_{K_j}\,dx$, with exactly this
  density, $K_j$ bounded open convex and nonempty, and $V_j$ smooth and convex on an open neighbourhood of
  $\overline{K_j}$ (indeed smooth and uniformly convex on $\mathbb R^n$), such that
  $\int\prod_{i<d}\langle v_i,x\rangle\,d\eta_j\to\int\prod_{i<d}\langle v_i,x\rangle\,d\mu$ for every $d\le4$ and all
  $v_1,\dots,v_d$. Hence $\mathbb E_{\eta_j}p\to\mathbb E_\mu p$ for every polynomial $p$ of degree at most four, written
  as a finite sum of such mixed linear monomials; no multivariate-polynomial interface is stated. The $\varepsilon$-form
  gives, for each $\varepsilon>0$, one such law whose coordinate monomials of degree at most four are within
  $\varepsilon$. The source assumes $n>0$; the proof does not use it.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Centered, covariance-whitened growing-ball truncations of $\mu$ are compactly supported isotropic log-concave laws
  with converging mixed moments of degree at most four. Each of them is approximated by whitened, fixed-ball-conditioned
  Gaussian smoothings, which are isotropic laws $1_Ke^{-W}dx$ with $K$ compact convex with nonempty interior and $W$
  smooth and uniformly convex; every term is isotropic after a cofinal shift. A diagonal choice over the finitely many
  coordinate monomials of degree at most four, extended to all mixed linear moments by multilinearity, gives the
  sequence, and the convex body is replaced by its interior because its frontier is null.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Letwin: compact quadratic-form bound]
  \label{thm:letwin-compact-quadratic-bound}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.Suspension.letwinCompactBound,
        Solutions.ThmLetwinCompactQuadraticBound.certificate,
        Solutions.ThmLetwinCompactQuadraticBound.bound}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:letwin-regular-class-constant-eight, lem:letwin-regular-approximation}
  For every $n>0$, every compactly supported isotropic log-concave probability measure $\mu$ on $\mathbb R^n$ and every
  self-adjoint operator $M$ (singular and indefinite included),
  \[
    \int\Bigl(\langle Mx,x\rangle-\int\langle My,y\rangle\,d\mu(y)\Bigr)^2d\mu(x)\le8\sum_{i,j}\langle Me_i,e_j\rangle^2
  \]
  in the standard orthonormal basis. This is the exact compact counterpart of Letwin \cite{Letwin2026QuadraticKLS},
  Theorem~1.2 (arXiv:2607.24164v1), the input of Bizeul--Klartag--Lehec \cite{BizeulKlartagLehec2026}, Lemma~4.2. The
  noncompact Theorem~1.2 has the separate certificate in Theorem~\ref{thm:letwin-quadratic-bound}.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The compact reduction \texttt{letwinCompactBound\_of\_regularClass} approximates the law by the compact regular
  approximants of Lemma~\ref{lem:letwin-regular-approximation} and passes the bound of
  Theorem~\ref{thm:letwin-regular-class-constant-eight} to the limit through moments of degree at most four.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Letwin: quadratic-form bound without compact support]
  \label{thm:letwin-quadratic-bound}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.letwinQuadraticBound,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.matrix_variance_le_eight,
        Solutions.ThmLetwinQuadraticBound.certificate}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:letwin-compact-quadratic-bound, lem:letwin-operator-matrix-identification}
  For every $n>0$, every isotropic log-concave probability measure $\mu$ on $\mathbb R^n$
  and every real symmetric matrix $M$, including singular and indefinite matrices,
  \[
    \operatorname{Var}_\mu\!\left(\sum_{i,j}M_{ij}X_iX_j\right)
      \le 8\operatorname{tr}(M^2).
  \]
  This is Letwin \cite{Letwin2026QuadraticKLS}, arXiv:2607.24164v1, Theorem~1.2,
  with the isotropic normalization $8\operatorname{tr}(M^2)
  =2\mathbb E_\mu\|\nabla\langle MX,X\rangle\|^2$.
  No compact-support or additional moment premise is imposed. The compact certificate
  remains the input to BKL; the logarithmic-quarter conclusion has the separate
  alternative-route certificate of Theorem~\ref{thm:letwin-log-quarter}.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Center and covariance-whiten growing-ball truncations of $\mu$. Log-concavity supplies
  the finite moments and mixed-moment convergence through degree four. Apply
  Theorem~\ref{thm:letwin-compact-quadratic-bound} to each compact isotropic approximant
  and pass its constant-eight inequality to the limit.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[BKL equation (95), finite-horizon cutoff form]
  \label{lem:bkl-cutoff-energy}
  \lean{Solutions.LemBklCutoffEnergy.certificate,
        Solutions.LemBklCutoffEnergy.bound,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.BKL.Localization.CutoffEnergyClaim,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.BKL.Localization.cutoffEnergyClaim_of_letwinCompact,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.BKL.Localization.cutoffEnergyClaim_153}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:letwin-compact-quadratic-bound, lem:bkl-cumulant-envelope, lem:bkl-second-cumulant-base,
        lem:bkl-grouped-drift-expansion, lem:bkl-canonical-standardisation, lem:bkl-coefficients-regularity,
        lem:bkl-posterior-generator}
  \cite{BizeulKlartagLehec2026}, Sections~4--6, equation~(95), in a finite-horizon cutoff form. Let $d\ge1$ (the
  order $m=d+1\ge2$), $n\ge0$, $\mu$ a compactly supported isotropic log-concave probability measure on $\mathbb R^n$,
  $u$ a unit vector, $\varepsilon>0$ and $T\ge0$. Then there is $R_0$ such that for every $R\ge R_0$ and every cutoff
  Eldan process $p_t=(c_t,Q_t)$ on any probability space carrying an $n$-dimensional Brownian motion with a usual
  filtration --- the strong solution of~(67) with drift $\gamma_R^2(A^{-1}a,A^{-1})$ and noise
  $\gamma_R(A^{-1/2}e_k,0)$, $\gamma_R(p)=\max(0,\min(1,R+1-|p|))$, started at $0$ ---
  \[
    E_{d+1}(0)+\frac12\int\!\!\int_0^T\gamma_R(p_s)^2\,E_{d+2}(p_s)\,ds\,dP\le(d!)^2\,153^d+\varepsilon,
  \]
  where $E_{r+1}(p)=|\kappa_{r+1}(\mu_p)(u)|^2_{A(p)}$ is the cumulant energy (75)--(76) of the posterior $\mu_p$.
  The factor $\frac12$ and the normalization $b_{d+1}=K^d(d!)^2$ of~(94) are those of the
  source. The explicit choice $K=153$ is supplied by the local generator estimate $C=17$, rather
  than stated numerically in the paper; $d\ge1$ includes the base order $m=2$.
  This is a restricted counterpart, with a different proof, of~(95), which
  the source states for the uncut Eldan process on the infinite horizon. The uncut process (strong solution,
  uniqueness, non-explosion) and the literal stochastic identities (68)--(73) are not constructed here. BKL
  Theorem~4.1 and KLS are certified separately in Theorems~\ref{thm:bkl-cumulant-bound} and~\ref{thm:bkl-poincare}; they are not conclusions of this cutoff-energy lemma.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The cutoff coefficients are globally Lipschitz and bounded, so the cutoff equation has a strong solution on every
  Brownian setup with a usual filtration. Dynkin's identity $\mathbb EF(p_T)-F(0)=\mathbb E\int_0^T\gamma^2\mathcal
  LF(p_s)\,ds$ holds for every $C^2$ function $F$ of the parameter, and $Q_t$ stays positive semidefinite. Lemma~4.3
  enters through the generator identity $\mathcal L\kappa_m=-(m\kappa_m+L_m)$
  (Lemma~\ref{lem:bkl-grouped-drift-expansion}). Lemma~5.1 enters through its generator form
  $\mathcal LE_m\ge\frac12E_{m+1}-17m^2E_m-2\langle\kappa_m(u),L_m(u)\rangle$, with $C=17$; it uses the third-order bound
  (79), supplied by the compact constant-8 theorem (Theorem~\ref{thm:letwin-compact-quadratic-bound}).
  Dynkin's identity for $E_m$ gives Lemma~5.2 in cutoff-weighted form. The base order $m=2$ follows from Dynkin's
  identity for $\langle Au,u\rangle$, as in~(78), with $K\ge5$. For $m\ge3$ the strong induction of Section~6 bounds
  the middle term by (99)--(106), using the envelope arithmetic (Lemma~\ref{lem:bkl-cumulant-envelope}) and the
  standardized-posterior bound (77). A Lyapunov moment bound with a Markov tail (its generator bound again uses (79))
  and a mean-value choice of the horizon remove the cutoff boundary terms as $R\to\infty$. Finally,
  $K=153=9\cdot17\ge\max\{5,9C,64\}$. No inequality, induction step, Lyapunov estimate or process existence is
  assumed.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[BKL Theorem 4.1]
  \label{thm:bkl-cumulant-bound}
  \lean{Solutions.ThmBklCumulantBound.certificate,
        Solutions.ThmBklCumulantBound.bound,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.Suspension.cumulantBound_of_compact,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.BKL.Localization.cumulantBound_of_letwinCompact,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.BKL.Localization.cumulantBound_153}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:bkl-cutoff-energy, lem:bkl-cumulant-envelope}
  \cite{BizeulKlartagLehec2026}, Theorem~4.1, (65)--(66), in the source form. There is a universal $K\ge1$ such that
  for every order $m\ge2$, every $n$, every isotropic log-concave probability measure $\mu$ on $\mathbb R^n$ and every
  $u\in\mathbb R^n$,
  \[
    \sum_{i_2,\dots,i_m=1}^n\kappa^\mu_m(u,e_{i_2},\dots,e_{i_m})^2\le K^{m-1}\bigl((m-1)!\bigr)^2|u|^2,
  \]
  that is, $R^\mu_m\le K^{m-1}((m-1)!)^2\,\mathrm{Id}$, with $\kappa^\mu_m=\nabla^m\Lambda_\mu(0)$. There is no
  compactness, Letwin, moment, energy or induction premise. The Lean statement uses $m=d+1$ with $d\ge1$, so the base
  order $m=2$ is included; it holds with $K=153$, and also for $n=0$, where it is trivial. The proof uses
  cutoff localization. The uncut Eldan process and the literal stochastic identities
  (68)--(73) are not constructed. BKL Theorem~1.1 and KLS are the separate conclusions of Theorem~\ref{thm:bkl-poincare}.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  For every $d\ge1$ the cutoff energy induction (Lemma~\ref{lem:bkl-cutoff-energy}, with the compact constant-8
  theorem) gives, at $T=0$ and as $\varepsilon\to0$, the compact bound $R^\nu_{d+1}\le(d!)^2153^d\,\mathrm{Id}$ for
  every compactly supported isotropic log-concave $\nu$; here $E_{d+1}(0)=\langle R^\nu_{d+1}u,u\rangle$ and the
  envelope $b_{d+1}=(d!)^2K^d$ is that of Lemma~\ref{lem:bkl-cumulant-envelope}. The compact-to-general passage
  \texttt{cumulantBound\_of\_compact} extends the bound to every isotropic log-concave law with the same constant. It
  restricts the law to large balls, standardizes it, applies the compact bound, and passes to the limit, which is the
  approximation argument on p.~19 of the source.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[BKL Theorem 1.1]
  \label{thm:bkl-poincare}
  \lean{Solutions.ThmBklPoincare.certificate,
        Solutions.ThmBklPoincare.bound,
        FunctionalInequalities.KLS.BKL.Localization.exists_isotropicBound}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:bkl-cumulant-bound, prop:bkl-suspension, thm:bkl-taylor-criterion, lem:bkl-regular-reduction,
        lem:bkl-regular-approximation, lem:bkl-isotropic-reduction, thm:letwin-compact-quadratic-bound}
  \cite{BizeulKlartagLehec2026}, Theorem~1.1, p.~2, in the source form. There is a universal constant $C>0$ such that
  for every $n\ge1$ and every isotropic log-concave probability measure $\mu$ on $\mathbb R^n$,
  \[
    \CPLit(\mu)\le C,
  \]
  where $\CPLit$ is the literature local-slope Poincaré constant (for log-concave laws the source constant of~(5)). The
  constant is chosen before the dimension and the law; there is no compactness, regularity or moment premise beyond
  isotropy. The named form \texttt{bound} states the same bound on every finite-dimensional real inner product space.
  The right-hand inequality of the equivalent form~(6), for every log-concave law with the operator norm of its
  covariance, is the project target, Conjecture~\ref{conj:kls}, which follows through the affine/isotropic reduction
  (Theorem~\ref{thm:affine-isotropic-reduction}). The proof uses cutoff localization.
  The uncut Eldan process and the literal stochastic identities (68)--(73) are not constructed, and the
  intermediate source statements keep their own recorded scopes.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Proof on p.~30. Theorem~\ref{thm:bkl-cumulant-bound} gives a universal $K\ge1$ with
  $R^\nu_{d+1}\le(d!)^2K^d\,\mathrm{Id}$ for every isotropic log-concave $\nu$ in every dimension and every $d\ge2$; in
  Lean this is \texttt{exists_cumulantBound_of_letwinCompact} applied to the proved compact Letwin bound
  (Theorem~\ref{thm:letwin-compact-quadratic-bound}). Lemma~\ref{lem:bkl-regular-reduction}
  (Proposition~\ref{prop:bkl-suspension} with $b=(d!)^2K^d$, isotropy for $d=1$, and
  Theorem~\ref{thm:bkl-taylor-criterion} with $R=\sqrt{2K}$) gives $\CPLit(\mu)\le2CK$ for every isotropic regular law,
  where the constant $C$ of Theorem~3.1 is chosen before $K$. Lemma~\ref{lem:bkl-isotropic-reduction} (the regular
  approximation of Section~2, Lemma~\ref{lem:bkl-regular-approximation}, and the weak-limit passage) extends this to
  every isotropic log-concave law in every finite-dimensional space. Hence $2CK$ is a universal constant.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Smooth source Hessian calculus and conservative height cutoffs]
  \label{lem:regular-smooth-moment-calculus}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_regular_smooth_moment_calculus}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:regular-moment-smooth, lem:letwin-monge-ampere-second, lem:letwin-a4-cutoffs, lem:letwin-a5-cutoffs}
  In the strong regular class of Lemma~\ref{lem:regular-moment-smooth}, every $n>0$ admits
  one smooth convex normalized transporting potential $u$ with the pointwise Hessian-generator
  identity of Letwin Lemma~2.4 and smooth compact height cutoffs $\chi_N\in[0,1]$,
  increasing pointwise to $1$, with
  $\int|L\chi_N|\,d\nu\le C/(N+1)\to0$, $\nu=e^{-u}dx$.
  At this same $u$, every bounded smooth $f$ with $Lf+cf\ge0$, $c>0$, satisfies
  $Lf\in L^1(\nu)$ and $\int Lf\,d\nu=0$. No cutoff or generator-integrability premise
  is supplied. This is the strong-class source consumption of Lemmas~A.4/A.5;
  the merely convex source class is not covered here (it is treated separately by a $\kappa$-free
  limit argument (Lemma~\ref{lem:convex-source-smooth-moment-potential}) producing a possibly different potential); the sharp smooth estimates are supplied below.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Choose $u$ once from Lemma~\ref{lem:regular-moment-smooth}. Hessian positivity and the
  log-determinant relation pass from the coordinate basis to the standard orthonormal basis.
  The existing pointwise Hessian evolution identity, smooth height-cutoff producer and
  cutoff-defect conservativity theorem then apply to this same normalized transporting $u$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The sharp-trace moment potential is smooth]
  \label{lem:regular-smooth-sharp-trace}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.holder_hessian_of_weak_evolution_fields,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.contDiff_three_of_regular_fields,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.smooth_of_regular_moment_fields,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_regular_moment_potential_smooth_trace}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:letwin-regular-moment-weak-stein-coupling, lem:regular-moment-smooth}
  In the strong regular class (globally smooth $\kappa$-uniformly convex $W$, compact convex
  $K$ with nonempty interior and isotropic density $1_K e^{-W}$), every $n>0$ admits one
  normalized transporting moment potential $u$ that is smooth, has a smooth gradient inverse
  onto $\operatorname{int}K$, retains the $16/\kappa$ cap and mean Hessian $\mathrm{Id}$, and satisfies
  \[
    \int\operatorname{tr}(B D^2u B D^2u)\,d\nu\le2\operatorname{tr}(B^2),
    \qquad \nu=e^{-u}dx,
  \]
  for every real symmetric $B$, including singular and indefinite matrices; the integrand
  is integrable. No sharp-estimate hypothesis is supplied. The merely convex source class is
  treated in Theorem~\ref{thm:convex-source-sharp-trace} and, for every moment potential,
  Theorem~\ref{thm:letwin-every-moment-potential}.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Choose the weak sharp-trace witness once. The given-potential weak-evolution to H\"older-Hessian
  bridge proves this very $u$ is $C^2$. The given-potential $C^3$ and all-order bridges,
  including the one-dimensional ODE branch, then prove it smooth. No equality or uniqueness
  of independently chosen existential witnesses is used.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Smooth Stein kernel with the sharp square-integrability estimate]
  \label{lem:regular-smooth-stein-l2}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_regular_smooth_moment_stein_kernel_L2}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:regular-smooth-sharp-trace}
  For the same strong-class source law and every $n>0$, one actual smooth potential $u$
  has a globally measurable inverse $\Psi$ that is smooth on $\operatorname{int}K$.
  The symmetric positive definite Stein kernel $\tau=D^2u\circ\Psi$ has smooth entries there,
  $\tau(\nabla u(y))=D^2u(y)$ at every $y$, mean $\mathrm{Id}$, and
  $\int|\tau|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2\,d\mu\le2n$. This same $u$ retains every symmetric trace bound
  of Lemma~\ref{lem:regular-smooth-sharp-trace}.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Use the measurable extension of the smooth gradient inverse at the sharp smooth witness.
  The smooth Hessian-kernel construction supplies Stein's identity and mean $\mathrm{Id}$.
  Push the trace estimate at $B=\mathrm{Id}$ through the gradient transport, using
  $|H|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2=\operatorname{tr}(H^2)$ for the symmetric Hessian.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Literal convex-body source: a C1 moment potential and its equation]
  \label{lem:convex-source-moment-equation}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_moment_potential_of_continuousOn,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_moment_potential_of_bounded_open_convex}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:cek-bounded-moment-map, lem:transport-gradient-range}
  Let $n>0$, $K$ bounded open convex and nonempty, $W$ continuous on $\overline K$, and
  $\mu=1_K e^{-W}dx$ an isotropic probability law. There is one normalized Lipschitz
  strictly convex $C^1$ potential $u$ transporting $e^{-u}dx$ to $\mu$, with
  $\nabla u:\mathbb R^n\simeq K$ a homeomorphism. Its continuous positive density
  $f=\exp(-u+W(\nabla u))$ satisfies the Alexandrov equation on every open set and
  both pointwise viscosity touching inequalities. Almost everywhere $u$ has a positive
  definite quadratic expansion matrix $B$ with $\det B=f$; no identification with a
  total Hessian is asserted without differentiability of $\nabla u$.
  This includes the original smooth convex-near-$\overline K$ inputs of Letwin Lemma~2.2,
  with no uniform convexity. Quantitative $C^{1,\alpha}$ is supplied separately by
  Lemma~\ref{lem:convex-gradient-holder}. $W^{2,p}$ and smoothness of this witness are not proved;
  a smooth moment potential for the same source class is produced separately by a $\kappa$-free
  limit argument (Lemma~\ref{lem:convex-source-smooth-moment-potential}) and is not identified with this witness.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Extend $W|_{\overline K}$ continuously by Tietze, choose the bounded CEK moment witness,
  and apply strictness, $C^1$ and the gradient-range theorem at this witness. The actual
  Alexandrov equation gives the quadratic expansions and both viscosity tests.
  The open and closed body laws agree because the convex frontier has zero volume.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[A $\kappa$-free Hessian bound for smooth Monge--Amp\`ere potentials]
  \label{lem:moment-potential-laplacian-bound}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Probability.MomentMeasure.hessianInverseOperator_fderiv_apply,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.MomentMeasure.trace_sub_two_mul_sq_le_hessianInverseOperator,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.MomentMeasure.trace_hessian_add_sq_le}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:letwin-monge-ampere-second, lem:letwin-inverse-hessian-derivative}
  Let $K$ be open with $|y|\le R$ on $K$, let $V$ be smooth near $\overline K$ and convex on $K$, and
  let $\varphi$ be smooth with positive definite Hessian, $\nabla\varphi(\mathbb R^n)\subseteq K$ and
  $\log\det D^2\varphi=-\varphi+V(\nabla\varphi)$ everywhere. If $\Delta\varphi$ is bounded above and
  $\varphi\to\infty$ at infinity, then
  \[
    \Delta\varphi+|\nabla\varphi|^2\le3R^2
  \]
  everywhere. This supports Klartag (arXiv:1309.2767v1) Theorem~1.1, with $3R^2$ in place of $2R^2$
  and a different proof; it is not that theorem.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  With $L=\sum_{ij}(D^2\varphi)^{-1}_{ij}\partial_{ij}-\sum_i\partial_i\varphi\,\partial_i$, the first
  derivatives satisfy $L\partial_i\varphi=-\partial_i\varphi$, and the second Monge--Amp\`ere identity
  with convexity of $V$ gives $LG\ge\Delta\varphi-2|\nabla\varphi|^2$ for
  $G=\Delta\varphi+|\nabla\varphi|^2$. Coercivity and the upper bound on $\Delta\varphi$ give a global
  maximum of $G$, where $LG\le0$; hence $\Delta\varphi\le2|\nabla\varphi|^2\le2R^2$ there and $G\le3R^2$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Klartag: two-sided linear bounds and the limit moment potential]
  \label{lem:klartag-moment-potential-limit}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Probability.MomentMeasure.lintegral_line_half_ge,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.MomentMeasure.exists_lintegral_line_abs_inner_gradient,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.MomentMeasure.abs_le_of_mem_levelSet_of_centered,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.MomentMeasure.exists_two_sided_linear_bounds,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.MomentMeasure.tendsto_integral_comp_gradient_mul_exp_neg,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.MomentMeasure.exists_limit_potential,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.MomentMeasure.inv_le_integral_abs_inner_gradient_of_isotropic}
  \leanok
  Klartag (arXiv:1309.2767v1) Lemma~2.3 for $C^1$ potentials: for $L,\varepsilon>0$ there are
  $\alpha,\beta,\gamma>0$ such that every convex, $C^1$, $L$-Lipschitz, centered $\psi$ satisfying the
  nondegeneracy condition~(13) with $\varepsilon$ obeys $\alpha|x|-\beta\le\psi(x)\le L|x|+\gamma$.
  The direction (i)$\Rightarrow$(ii) of Proposition~2.1 holds for such potentials converging pointwise.
  Conversely (the compactness step of (ii)$\Rightarrow$(i), without uniqueness), if centered convex
  $L$-Lipschitz $C^1$ potentials of laws $\mu_l\to\nu$ have uniform two-sided linear bounds and
  second differences at most $C|h|^2$, then some convex $L$-Lipschitz $C^1$ centered potential with
  $4C$-Lipschitz gradient has moment measure $\nu$. Condition~(13) holds with $\varepsilon=1/R$ for
  the potential of an isotropic law whose gradient is bounded by $R$. Lemma~2.3 for merely Lipschitz
  potentials and the uniqueness half of Proposition~2.1 are not formalized.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Fubini along lines gives $\int|\langle\nabla\psi,\theta\rangle|e^{-\psi}=2\int_{\theta^\perp}e^{-\inf_s\psi(z+s\theta)}dz$
  up to the line constant; centering and (13) bound the level sets in each direction, which gives the
  linear lower bound. For the limit, pointwise compactness of convex functions with linear bounds,
  convergence of gradients of convex functions and dominated convergence pass the moment measures
  to the limit; second-difference bounds give differentiability and the Lipschitz gradient.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[A moment potential with Lipschitz gradient is smooth]
  \label{lem:moment-potential-smooth-given-gradient}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.smooth_moment_potential_of_lipschitzWith_gradient,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_regular_moment_potential_smooth_on_range}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:regular-moment-smooth, lem:regular-moment-hessian-holder, lem:letwin-weak-third-derivative}
  Let $n>0$, $K$ compact convex with nonempty interior, $W$ smooth and convex on
  $\operatorname{int}K$, and $\mu=1_K e^{-W}dx$ a probability measure. A given convex Lipschitz
  normalized potential $u$ with $(\nabla u)_\#e^{-u}dx=\mu$ and Lipschitz gradient is smooth with locally
  H\"older derivatives of every order, has positive definite Hessian with
  $\log\det D^2u=-u+W(\nabla u)$ everywhere, $\nabla u:\mathbb R^n\simeq\operatorname{int}K$, and a
  smooth left inverse of $\nabla u$ on $\operatorname{int}K$. No uniform convexity of $W$ and no isotropy
  is assumed. The strong regular class needs uniform convexity of $W$ only on $\operatorname{int}K$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Uniform convexity entered the strong-class chain only through the Lipschitz gradient and through
  convexity of $W$ on the range of $\nabla u$; with both given, the weak third derivatives,
  H\"older Hessian and all-order bridges of Lemma~\ref{lem:regular-moment-smooth} apply to this $u$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Letwin Lemma~2.2 for the merely convex source class, existence form]
  \label{lem:convex-source-smooth-moment-potential}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.KLS.LogConcave.exists_regularClass_seq_tendsto_of_compact,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.MomentMeasure.exists_centered_regular_potential,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.MomentMeasure.exists_smooth_moment_potential_of_bounded_open_convex}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:letwin-regular-approximation, lem:moment-potential-laplacian-bound,
        lem:klartag-moment-potential-limit, lem:moment-potential-smooth-given-gradient}
  Let $n>0$, $K$ bounded open convex and nonempty, $V$ smooth on an open neighbourhood of
  $\overline K$ and convex on $K$, and $\mu=1_Ke^{-V}dx$ isotropic. There is a normalized smooth convex
  $\varphi$ with $(\nabla\varphi)_\#e^{-\varphi}dx=\mu$, positive definite Hessian,
  $\log\det D^2\varphi=-\varphi+V(\nabla\varphi)$ everywhere, $\nabla\varphi:\mathbb R^n\simeq K$ with a
  smooth inverse on $K$, and $D^2\varphi\le C\,\mathrm{Id}$ for some $C$ (the proof gives $C=12R^2$ with
  $K\subseteq B(0,R)$, $R\ge1$). This potential is produced; Theorem~\ref{thm:letwin-every-moment-potential}
  transfers every property to every moment potential of $\mu$, including the moment map of Letwin Lemma~2.1. It is
  also not identified with the witness of Lemma~\ref{lem:convex-source-moment-equation}.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Approximate $\mu$ by strong-class laws supported in one ball with converging moments. Their
  centered potentials are $R$-Lipschitz and, by Lemma~\ref{lem:moment-potential-laplacian-bound},
  have second differences at most $3R^2|h|^2$, uniformly in the convexity constant.
  Lemma~\ref{lem:klartag-moment-potential-limit} gives a limit potential of $\mu$ with Lipschitz
  gradient. Replace $V$ by a globally smooth $W$ agreeing with it on $\overline K$; then
  Lemma~\ref{lem:moment-potential-smooth-given-gradient} makes this potential smooth with every
  stated property.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Letwin Theorem~2.5 for the merely convex source class, existence form]
  \label{thm:convex-source-sharp-trace}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Probability.MomentMeasure.integral_trace_hessian_quadratic_le,
        FunctionalInequalities.Probability.MomentMeasure.exists_smooth_moment_potential_trace_le_of_bounded_open_convex}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:convex-source-smooth-moment-potential}
  Under the hypotheses of Lemma~\ref{lem:convex-source-smooth-moment-potential}, the potential
  produced there also satisfies, with $\nu=e^{-\varphi}dx$,
  \[
    \int\operatorname{tr}(B D^2\varphi B D^2\varphi)\,d\nu\le2\operatorname{tr}(B^2)
  \]
  for every real symmetric $B$, and the integrand is $\nu$-integrable. The paper's argument is
  formalized for any smooth potential with the Lemma~2.2 properties and a uniform Hessian bound;
  Theorem~\ref{thm:letwin-every-moment-potential} states the bound for every moment potential of $\mu$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Apply the conditional Theorem~2.5 argument to the potential of
  Lemma~\ref{lem:convex-source-smooth-moment-potential}: the positive semidefinite case first, then the
  symmetric extension.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Letwin Lemmas~2.3, 2.4, A.4--A.6 at the merely convex source potential]
  \label{lem:convex-source-smooth-moment-calculus}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Probability.MomentMeasure.exists_smooth_moment_calculus_of_bounded_open_convex}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:convex-source-smooth-moment-potential, lem:letwin-mean-hessian, lem:letwin-monge-ampere-second,
        lem:letwin-a4-cutoffs, lem:letwin-a5-cutoffs, thm:letwin-scalar-brascamp-lieb}
  Under the hypotheses of Lemma~\ref{lem:convex-source-smooth-moment-potential}, one potential $\varphi$
  with every property stated there, $\varphi$ and $\nabla\varphi$ Lipschitz, and $\nu=e^{-\varphi}dx$ also
  satisfies: every Hessian form is $\nu$-integrable with $\int D^2\varphi(u,v)\,d\nu=\langle u,v\rangle$
  (Lemma~2.3); the pointwise identity $L(D^2\varphi)=-D^2\varphi+D^2\varphi\,D^2V(\nabla\varphi)\,D^2\varphi
  +\Gamma_3$ with both right-hand matrices positive semidefinite (Lemma~2.4); smooth compactly supported
  cutoffs $\chi_N\in[0,1]$ increasing to $1$ with $\int|L\chi_N|\,d\nu\le D/(N+1)$ (Lemma~A.4); $Lf\in
  L^1(\nu)$ and $\int Lf\,d\nu=0$ for every bounded smooth $f$ with $Lf+cf\ge0$, $c>0$ (Lemma~A.5); and
  $\operatorname{Var}_\nu f\le\int\langle\nabla f,(D^2\varphi)^{-1}\nabla f\rangle\,d\nu$ for every
  $C^1$ $f\in L^2(\nu)$ (Lemma~A.6). Theorem~\ref{thm:letwin-every-moment-potential} states the same for every
  moment potential of $\mu$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Choose $\varphi$ once from Lemma~\ref{lem:convex-source-smooth-moment-potential}. Boundedness of
  $\nabla\varphi(\mathbb R^n)\subseteq K$ and the uniform Hessian bound give the Lipschitz constants; the
  given-potential mean-Hessian, Monge--Amp\`ere, cutoff, conservativity and Brascamp--Lieb results then
  apply to this $\varphi$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Letwin Lemmas~2.7 and 2.9 at the merely convex source potential]
  \label{lem:convex-source-smooth-stein-kernel}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Probability.MomentMeasure.exists_smooth_moment_stein_kernel_of_bounded_open_convex}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:convex-source-sharp-trace, lem:regular-smooth-stein-l2, lem:letwin-hessian-kernel,
        lem:letwin-stein-dual}
  Under the hypotheses of Lemma~\ref{lem:convex-source-smooth-moment-potential}, one potential $\varphi$
  with those properties and a measurable $\Psi$, smooth on $K$ with $\nabla\varphi(\Psi(y))=y$ there and
  $\Psi\circ\nabla\varphi=\mathrm{id}$, give a symmetric Stein kernel $\tau=D^2\varphi\circ\Psi$ of $\mu$
  that is positive definite everywhere, has smooth entries on $K$, satisfies
  $\tau(\nabla\varphi(y))=D^2\varphi(y)$, has mean $\mathrm{Id}$ and $\int|\tau|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2\,d\mu\le2n$
  (Lemma~2.7). The same $\varphi$ satisfies every trace bound of
  Theorem~\ref{thm:convex-source-sharp-trace}, and for every $v$ the centered linear function
  $x\mapsto\langle v,x\rangle$ has finite $H^{-1}(\mu)$ norm with square at most
  $\int|\tau(x)v|^2\,d\mu$ (Lemma~2.9). Theorem~\ref{thm:letwin-every-moment-potential} states the same for
  every moment potential of $\mu$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Replace $V$ by a globally smooth $W$ equal to it on $\overline K$; the frontier of $K$ is null, so
  $\mu=1_{\overline K}e^{-W}dx$ with $\operatorname{int}\overline K=K$. The smooth Stein-kernel construction
  of Lemma~\ref{lem:regular-smooth-stein-l2}, which uses only the given-potential properties and the
  trace bound, applies at the potential of Theorem~\ref{thm:convex-source-sharp-trace}; the kernel-based
  dual estimate gives the $H^{-1}$ bound.
\end{proof}

  Under the hypotheses of Lemma~\ref{lem:convex-source-smooth-moment-potential} ($n>0$, $K$ bounded open convex

  remaining conclusions are given-potential consequences of them.

\begin{lemma}[DOMAIN Alexandrov maximum and boundary controls]
  \label{lem:domain-alexandrov-maximum}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.subgradient_le_on_of_isOpen,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_pos_alexandrov_maximum_principle_domain,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_pos_alexandrov_maximum_principle,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.mem_subgradientImage_of_affine_le_of_nonneg_frontier,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.subgradientImageOn_const_subset_singleton,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_pos_boundary_power_bound_domain,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_common_lower_boundary_modulus_domain,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.infDist_frontier_le_diam_of_mem}
  \leanok
  Guti\'errez (2001), Theorem~1.4.2, as support for Letwin v1 Lemma~2.2.
  In every nontrivial finite-dimensional real inner-product space, there is
  $c>0$ before every $u,\Omega$, such that a function convex and continuous
  ONLY on $\overline\Omega$, with $\Omega$ bounded, open and convex and
  $u\ge0$ on the frontier, satisfies at each $x\in\Omega$ with $u(x)<0$
  \[
    c(-u(x))^n\le |\partial_\Omega u(\Omega)|
       \operatorname{diam}(\Omega)^{n-1}\operatorname{dist}(x,\partial\Omega).
  \]
  The image is the actual DOMAIN supporting image, not an assumed extension
  or image agreement. No global convexity, differentiability, desired support
  inclusion or regularity estimate is input. The old global maximum interface
  keeps nondifferentiable sources; its sliding interface keeps nonconvex
  working domains. The generic pointwise support lift includes dimension zero
  without completeness, finite dimension, measurability or differentiability;
  the maximum bound itself retains the nontrivial-space restriction.
For an actual domain MA upper bound $\operatorname{MA}_\Omega u\le\Lambda\,dx$,
  $\Lambda\ge0$, a positive universal $C$ gives
  \[
  (\max(0,-u(x)))^n\le C\Lambda|\Omega|\operatorname{diam}(\Omega)^{n-1}
       \operatorname{dist}(x,\partial\Omega).
  \]
  Hence a common lower boundary modulus has its radius before every unknown
  function. These estimates retain the nontrivial-space restriction and need
  no differentiability, global extension or desired bound as input.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  A real compact minimum of the function minus an affine form produces
  interior supports. Mathlib linear concavity and convex local minima give
  one shared support-extension proof, also consumed by the existing compact
  maximum, gradient-identification and local-agreement results. One geometric
  cone-volume proof with the existing frontier/diameter supports serves the
  true GLOBAL and DOMAIN source producers. No arbitrary volume is cancelled. Boundedness supplies finite domain volume;
  the real boundary power cancels only the positive finite geometric constant.
  A single-use numerical power argument gives the common lower modulus.
  Actual quadratic, nondifferentiable shifted-absolute-value, nonconvex
  sliding and zero-dimensional support clients check the scopes. This bound
  does not construct a constant-density Dirichlet/Pogorelov reference or close
  the nonlinear $W^{2,p}$ and literal Lemma~2.2 gaps.
\end{proof}


\begin{lemma}[Quantitative section estimates and growth-to-gradient regularity]
  \label{lem:convex-section-growth}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.norm_gradient_sub_le_of_growth,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_holder_gradient_of_growth,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.growth_of_closedBall_subset_section,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_section_estimate_of_continuous_density}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:convex-source-moment-equation, lem:domain-alexandrov-maximum}
  For a convex continuous function with continuous Alexandrov density, every compact $Q$
  has $b>0$ such that every section $S=\{u<u(x)+\langle p,\cdot-x\rangle+h\}\subset Q$,
  $h>0$, satisfies
  \[
    c(n)h^n\le b|S|\operatorname{diam}(S)^{n-1}\operatorname{dist}(x,\partial S).
  \]
  The constants precede the centre, slope and height. In particular this estimate applies to
  the actual literal-source $C^1$ potential of Lemma~\ref{lem:convex-source-moment-equation}.
  Separately, section inclusions $\overline B(x,ch^\theta)\subset\{u\le\ell_x+h\}$ give
  tangent-plane growth of order $1/\theta$. For a differentiable convex function, growth
  at most $C|z-x|^{1+\alpha}$ gives
  $|\nabla u(y)-\nabla u(x)|\le2^{1+\alpha}C|y-x|^\alpha$ on half the growth radius;
  uniform growth on a compact set gives a finite H\"older gradient bound there.
  These are supporting quantitative statements for Berman--Berndtsson v1 Theorem~2.24(i).
  Compact-uniform small-section localization and two-sided density bounds provide the
  inputs to the closed power-rate gain of Lemma~\ref{lem:convex-gradient-holder}.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Apply the Alexandrov maximum principle to the affine-shifted section function. A compact
  upper bound for the continuous density controls the subgradient-image volume. Invert the
  section radius-height relation for the growth implication. For the gradient implication,
  test the supporting plane at $y$ in the direction $\nabla u(y)-\nabla u(x)$ and use the
  growth inequality at $x$.
\end{proof}


\begin{lemma}[Upper section volume from a lower Alexandrov density]
  \label{lem:convex-section-volume}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_section_volume_upper_bound,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.subgradient_volume_lower_bound_of_compact,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_section_volume_bound_of_continuous_density}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:convex-source-moment-equation}
  For every $n>0$ there is $C(n)>0$ such that a bounded convex section
  $S_h(x)=\{u<u(x)+\langle p,\cdot-x\rangle+h\}$, with $p$ a supporting slope,
  $h>0$ and lower Alexandrov density $a>0$ on its open subsets, satisfies
  $|S_h(x)|\le C(n)\sqrt{h^n/a}$. The dimensional constant precedes $u,x,p,h,a$.
  A continuous positive Alexandrov density gives a common floor $a$ on each compact $Q$,
  before the centres, slopes and heights of all sections contained there. The actual
  literal convex-body moment source supplies such a potential and density without a
  volume-estimate premise. This supports BB v1 Theorem~2.24(i); its normalized section
  power-rate and $C^{1,\alpha}$ gain is supplied by Lemma~\ref{lem:convex-gradient-holder};
  $W^{2,p}$ remains pending.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Apply affine normalization to the closed section and dilate by two so the normalized
  open section contains the closed unit ball. The lower MA density transforms by the
  inverse square of the affine determinant. The normalized quadratic comparison bounds
  that inverse determinant in terms of $h$ and $a$; the outer normalization ball then
  controls section volume. Inner regularity passes compact-set lower bounds to open sets.
\end{proof}


\begin{lemma}[Literal convex-body source: Holder gradient and section power radii]
  \label{lem:convex-gradient-holder}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_holder_gradient_of_alexandrov_density,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_section_power_inclusion_of_alexandrov_density,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_moment_potential_holder_gradient_of_bounded_open_convex}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:convex-source-moment-equation, lem:convex-section-growth}
  For every $n>0$, bounded open convex nonempty $K$, continuous $W$ on $\overline K$
  and isotropic probability law $1_K e^{-W}dx$, one normalized Lipschitz strictly convex
  $C^1$ moment potential $u$ retains its transport, gradient homeomorphism and every
  Alexandrov/viscosity field. On every compact $Q$, its gradient is $\alpha$-Holder
  for some $0<\alpha\le1$ with a finite common constant. At this same $u$, for each
  compact $Q$ there are $0<\alpha\le1$ and $c>0$ such that
  $\overline B(x,ch^{1/(1+\alpha)})\subset\{u\le\ell_x+h\}$ for every $x\in Q$
  and $0<h\le1$. Constants precede every centre, pair and height.
  No uniform convexity, section estimate or Holder conclusion is a source premise.
  The $W^{2,p}$ and second-order gains, and full Letwin Lemma~2.2, remain separate.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Strict convexity on spheres gives a common small-section height on compact sets;
  positivity and continuity of the actual density give common two-sided bounds.
  John normalization and the Alexandrov maximum/minimum estimates yield a uniform
  boundary margin for half-height sections. The affine determinant factors cancel
  in the density ratio, giving a two-direction Bregman comparison. Along rays the
  quotient of the tangent error by $t^{1+1/\kappa}$ is monotone, the constructive
  argument of Forzani--Maldonado (2004), Theorem~7, equation~(4.18). This gives
  tangent growth and hence a Holder gradient. A larger compact set supplies the
  uniform section power-radius inclusion. The original moment witness is chosen once.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Pure convex frontier maximum]

\begin{lemma}[Finite total DOMAIN MA boundary moduli]

  One private power argument gives the lower modulus; the pure convex frontier

  reference existence, or prove nonlinear $W^{2,p}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[C0 convex DOMAIN subgradients and compact slope bounds]

\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Closure equicontinuity of actual Dirichlet MA families]
  \label{lem:domain-dirichlet-equicontinuity}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.equicontinuousOn_of_domain_alexandrov_bounds,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_uniform_bound_equicontinuousOn_of_domain_mass,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.volume_subgradientImageOn_le_of_density_le}

        lem:domain-ma-total-boundary}
  Let $\Omega$ be bounded, open and convex in a nontrivial finite-dimensional
  real inner-product space. An arbitrarily indexed family, convex and continuous
  ONLY on $\overline\Omega$, zero on its frontier, with actual uniform finite
  TOTAL DOMAIN supporting-image mass bound $M\ge0$, has a generated common
  positive value cap and is equicontinuous on $\overline\Omega$. Empty domains
  and index classes are allowed. The old actual uniform positive lower and
  nonnegative upper DENSITY interface is retained with its exact scope.
  No cap, Lipschitz estimate, equicontinuity, subsequence, global extension,
  differentiability, reference existence or Hessian/Sobolev estimate is input.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  A single private substantial value/interior-Lipschitz/boundary/neighborhood
  implementation uses actual maximum and total boundary controls. The genuine
  TOTAL source derives nonpositivity from the pure convex frontier maximum.
  The old density source derives actual total mass using bounded volume and
  the MA upper bound, and genuinely uses its positive lower MA comparison for
  nonpositivity. One density-to-mass proof has three real production consumers;
  its previous private type/proof is retained under the shared public name,
  with no alias. Numerical powers use Mathlib rather than repeated induction.
  Actual $|x|-1$ has constructed total support mass $2$ and supplies a nonsmooth
  family client, with no density ceiling or positive lower density premise.
  This is not a convergent subsequence, MA measure/continuity or reference
  existence, and finite-mass class estimates do not manufacture a Perron-family
  mass bound when only the MA lower bound is known.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Uniform subsequences of actual finite-total MA families]
  \label{lem:domain-dirichlet-subsequence}

        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_convex_subseq_uniform_of_domain_mass}
  \leanok

  No cap, equicontinuity, subsequence, limit, source regularity or MA stability
  is supplied as input.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Actual finite-total estimates generate a common value cap and equicontinuity.
  Bounded continuous functions on the genuinely compact closure have their
  actual values in $[-B,B]$. Range equicontinuity, Mathlib Arzela--Ascoli and
