  sup-norm pointwise bound proves uniform convergence. The actual $|x|-1$
  family has constructed total support mass $2$. Actual uniform convergence
  gives pointwise convergence of an ambient representative; closed order passes
  each convex inequality to the limit and uniqueness gives zero frontier values.
  The old continuous-map subsequence producer is called once; no Ascoli proof
  is copied. No MA equation of the limit or approximating solutions/reference
  existence is constructed; MA weak continuity and Pogorelov remain missing.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Small-section engulfing and disjoint enlargement covering]
  \label{lem:convex-section-covering}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.SoftEngulfing.exists_section_engulfing,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_section_engulfing_of_alexandrov_density,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.SoftEngulfing.section_subset_of_intersection,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.SoftEngulfing.exists_disjoint_section_cover,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_disjoint_section_cover_of_alexandrov_density}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:convex-source-moment-equation, lem:convex-section-growth}
  For every $n>0$, a strictly convex $C^1$ Alexandrov solution with continuous positive
  density has compact-uniform constants $C>1$, $H>0$ with
  $y\in S_h(x)\Rightarrow S_h(x)\subset S_{Ch}(y)$ for $0<h\le H$.
  On each compact set there are $A>1$, $h_0>0$, chosen before any indexed family of
  centres and heights, such that every family with heights in $(0,h_0]$ admits a
  countable disjoint subfamily whose height-$A$ enlargements cover every original
  section. The literal-source client retains normalization, transport, homeomorphism
  and Holder gradients at the same potential. No engulfing estimate is a source input.
  This is not a bounded-overlap, contact-density or $W^{2,p}$ theorem.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The normalized dual-slope estimate applies to every point of the outer section.
  Strict sublevel membership gives strict full engulfing at twice the original height.
  Three engulfing applications show that intersecting comparable-height sections are
  contained in a fixed height enlargement. The general Vitali selection theorem with
  height as size selects disjoint originals. Their nonempty open interiors in a
  separable Euclidean space give countability. A larger compact set localizes every
  original section and supplies the common engulfing data, including at intersection points.
\end{proof}


\begin{lemma}[Height-dilation volume and quantitative weak-type section covering]
  \label{lem:convex-section-weak-type}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.section_volume_mul_le,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_quantitative_weak_type_section_cover}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:convex-section-covering}
  A globally finite convex function satisfies $|S_{Ah}(x,p)|\le A^n|S_h(x,p)|$
  for $A\ge1$, arbitrary slopes $p$, all real heights $h$, including $n=0$ and
  infinite volume. For an actual strictly convex $C^1$ Alexandrov solution with
  continuous positive density, each compact set admits $M,h_0>0$ before every
  measurable set $E$, positive $\delta$ and indexed family of sections of heights
  in $(0,h_0]$, such that $\delta|S_i|\le|E\cap S_i|$ for each section implies
  $\delta|\bigcup_i S_i|\le M|E|$. The actual literal-source client retains its
  normalized transporting Holder-gradient witness. No contact-density improvement
  or $W^{2,p}$ conclusion is asserted.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Convexity sends every point of $S_{Ah}$ into $S_h$ by the homothety of ratio
  $A^{-1}$ about the centre. Haar scaling bounds volume by $A^n$ times the original.
  Apply the countable disjoint enlargement cover and sum the density inequalities
  over selected originals. The ENNReal calculation uses no cancellation or
  finite-volume hypothesis.
\end{proof}


\begin{lemma}[Domain-local uniform perturbation contacts]
  \label{lem:convex-contact-perturbation}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.gradient_tangent_gap_pos_of_strictConvexOn,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.gradient_tangent_gap_pos,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_contact_of_uniform_close_on_open,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_contact_of_uniform_close,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_residual_contact_of_alexandrov_density_close,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_residual_contact_of_domain_alexandrov_density_close}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:alexandrov-density-comparison, lem:convex-section-growth}
  On bounded open $\Omega$, let $v$ be convex and continuous on
  $\overline\Omega$, strictly convex ONLY on $\Omega$ and $C^1$ there.
  A compact $K\subset\Omega$ admits a positive tolerance before every unknown
  $w$ continuous on the closure and differentiable only on $\Omega$.
  Uniform $C^0$ closeness produces, for each $z\in K$, an interior supporting
  contact with gradient $\nabla v(z)$ and support on ALL $\overline\Omega$.
  No global extension, boundary strictness, desired gap/contact or convexity of
  $w$ is assumed. This includes dimension zero. The lower strict tangent lemma
  needs only completeness, strict-set membership and actual differentiability at
  one base point; it has no finite-dimension or measurability premise.

  The exact old global-$C^1$ interface still permits NONCONVEX working $\Omega$.
  Its separate global strictness producer supplies the same private contact
  implementation. The actual density residual consumer now also accepts a GIVEN
  reference convex and continuous on the closure, strictly convex and $C^1$ ONLY
  inside, with zero boundary and actual DOMAIN unit MA. For $n>0$, $\delta>0$,
  choose $\epsilon\in(0,1)$ before unknown globally convex $C^1$ $u$; real
  density brackets produce contacts of $u-(1-\delta)v$ with gradient
  $\delta\nabla v(z)$ and support on ALL the closure. One private compact norm,
  tolerance, comparison, closure and local gradient assembly serves both real
  global and domain sources. No outside reference extension or boundary strictness.
  Reference existence, DOMAIN compact volume gain and $W^{2,p}$ remain open.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  A single strict affine-line slope proof on $[0,1]$ gives the generic tangent
  lemma, shared with the exact-type old SectionGrowth theorem. For a boundary
  point, an actual interior midpoint gives strictness and closure convexity
  passes it to the boundary. The true positive tangent gap on
  $K\times\partial\Omega$ has a compact positive minimum. Minimize the perturbed
  function minus the affine slope on the compact closure; exclude boundary
  minimizers and use actual local differentiability and Fermat's rule.
  This compact-gap/minimum implementation is private and shared once by the two
  true gap producers. The old global theorem is NOT a direct specialization of
  the local theorem, since its domain may be nonconvex. Actual audits construct
  the reference quadratic only inside $B_2$ and zero outside, for all dimensions,
  and a disconnected nonconvex real domain for the old interface. The previous
  actual density comparison and quadratic unit-MA clients remain consumers.
\end{proof}


\begin{lemma}[Matrix determinant AM--GM, Minkowski and the residual bound]
  \label{lem:matrix-determinant-minkowski}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.det_root_le_trace_div,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.det_root_add_le,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.det_le_pow_of_det_add_smul_le}
  \leanok
  In every dimension $n>0$, a real positive semidefinite matrix satisfies
  $\det(A)^{1/n}\le\operatorname{tr}(A)/n$, including singular $A$.
  If $A,B$ are positive semidefinite and $A+B$ is positive definite, then
  $\det(A)^{1/n}+\det(B)^{1/n}\le\det(A+B)^{1/n}$; no commutation is required.
  For $A\succeq0$, $B\succ0$, $\det B=1$, $0<\delta<1$, $\epsilon\ge0$,
  the actual bound $\det(A+(1-\delta)B)\le(1+\epsilon)^n$ implies
  $\det A\le(\epsilon+\delta)^n$. Independent clients construct a noncommuting
  shear-Gram reference and singular residual and check the scalar $n=1$ case.
  This supports the contact-density step of BB v1 Theorem~2.24(ii); no contact
  Monge--Ampere measure estimate, Dirichlet existence or $W^{2,p}$ is asserted.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Equal-weight scalar AM--GM applies to the actual nonnegative eigenvalues.
  With $S=A+B$, set $Q=\sqrt S$ using the continuous functional calculus and
  $R=Q^{-1}$. Congruence gives positive semidefinite $X=RAR$, $Y=RBR$ with
  $X+Y=I$. Apply AM--GM to both, sum traces, and use
  $(\det R)^2\det S=1$ to remove the positive normalization factor.
  Positive scaling of the unit-determinant reference and monotonicity of
  nonnegative real roots and integer powers give the exact residual bound.
\end{proof}


\begin{lemma}[Actual contact quadratic matrices and a.e. residual determinant control]
  \label{lem:convex-contact-determinant}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.posSemidef_of_contact_expansion,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.det_residual_le_of_contact_expansions,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.ae_det_residual_le_on_contact_of_alexandrov_density}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:matrix-determinant-minkowski, lem:convex-gradient-holder}
  An actual supporting affine contact of a function with a genuine quadratic
  expansion forces its quadratic matrix to be positive semidefinite; the function
  need not be convex or $C^2$. Actual expansions of $u,v$ then derive positivity
  of $U-(1-\delta)B$ at residual contacts and the exact determinant bound
  $\det(U-(1-\delta)B)\le(\epsilon+\delta)^n$, $n>0$, $0<\delta<1$,
  from $B\succ0$, $\det B=1$, and $\det U\le(1+\epsilon)^n$.
  Actual local Alexandrov equations provide both matrices and expansions almost
  everywhere, with the contact-point bound for every affine support there.
  Clients construct the unit quadratic reference, a singular residual and $n=1$,
  and retain the same literal-source normalized transporting Holder witness.
  The reference is given. No contact-gradient-image measure control, reference
  existence or $W^{2,p}$ theorem is claimed.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Every positive identity shift of the quadratic expansion is an upper touching
  test for the supporting affine function, which is convex. The existing viscosity
  Hessian inequality and positive-shift closure give PSD. Combine the two actual
  expansions by an error-sum estimate and apply the proved matrix Minkowski
  consequence. Alexandrov second-order differentiability and the genuine local
  equations identify the matrices, positivity and determinants a.e. The source
  client chooses its moment witness once; it supplies no MA/regularity estimate.
\end{proof}


\begin{lemma}[Null residual-gradient images of quadratic contacts]
  \label{lem:convex-contact-null-image}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.volume_image_residual_eq_zero_of_quadratic_contacts}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:convex-gradient-holder}
  Let $u$ be globally convex and $C^1$ on a finite-dimensional real inner-product
  space, including dimension zero. Suppose every open $G\subset\Omega$ satisfies
  $|\partial u(G)|\le C|G|$, with finite $C\ge0$ and open $\Omega$.
  If $t>0$ and $\Gamma\subset\Omega$ consists of actual supporting contacts of
  $u-t\|x\|^2/2$ relative to $\Omega$, every arbitrary null subset $N\subset\Gamma$
  has $|(\nabla u-t\mathrm{Id})(N)|=0$. No measurability of $N$ or $\Gamma$,
  residual convexity, gradient null-image property or Lipschitz factor is assumed.
  The literal-source client derives the local finite MA bound from its continuous
  density at the same normalized transporting Holder witness. General reference
  null images, the full contact-image determinant bound and $W^{2,p}$ remain open.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Outer regularity and the actual open-set MA bound give $|\nabla u(N)|=0$.
  The two contact inequalities imply monotonicity of $p=\nabla u-t\mathrm{Id}$
  on $\Gamma$, hence $\|p(x)-p(y)\|\le\|\nabla u(x)-\nabla u(y)\|$.
  Since $t>0$, $\nabla u$ is injective there. Define the actual factor on
  $\nabla u(\Gamma)$; it is $1$-Lipschitz, so the existing equidimensional
  Lipschitz null-image theorem gives the result. The common convex tangent
  support is reused in Subgradient, SecondDifference, SectionGrowth and here.
\end{proof}


\begin{lemma}[Actual local gradient derivative with the same Alexandrov matrix]
  \label{lem:convex-gradient-derivative}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.ae_exists_subgradient_secondOrder_expansion,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.ae_exists_gradient_secondOrder_expansion_on_open,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.ae_exists_gradient_quadraticAt_expansion_on_open,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.ae_exists_gradient_posDef_det_eq_on_open,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.ae_exists_gradient_secondOrder_expansion,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.ae_exists_gradient_quadraticAt_expansion,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.ae_exists_gradient_posDef_det_eq}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:convex-gradient-holder}
  Let $u$ be globally finite convex and differentiable only on open $\Omega$.
  Almost everywhere for volume restricted to $\Omega$, $\nabla u$ genuinely has
  derivative $B$ and the value expansion uses the SAME symmetric $B$. Coordinate
  conversion retains this SAME matrix; actual local open-set MA with continuous
  positive density gives PD and determinant $f(x)$ at that derivative/expansion
  witness. Generic and coordinate primitives include dimension zero. No global
  differentiability or smooth extension, $C^{1,1}$, Hessian, $W^{2,p}$ or desired
  derivative/expansion is supplied. Global convexity remains explicit; weak
  derivatives and domain-only Dirichlet production remain separate steps in this earlier
  variational route. The paper-source conclusion of Lemma~2.2 is proved independently by
  Lemma~\ref{lem:convex-source-smooth-moment-potential}.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The retained actual strong subgradient/value expansion is restricted to the
  open domain. Actual local differentiability and openness produce the gradient
  supporting affine forms at the centre and nearby points. Uniqueness gives
  $p=\nabla u(x)$, and the true strong first-order bound gives the derivative
  by the little-o norm criterion with the SAME $B$ as the value expansion.
  Reuse the existing private coordinate conversion unchanged, then apply the
  actual pointwise MA equation to that SAME matrix. The local gradient,
  coordinate and MA proofs are single producers; three old global interfaces
  keep exact types as meaningful univ/on-domain specializations. The actual abs
  positive-half-line client formally fails global differentiability, so cannot
  meet the old input. Actual unit-MA quadratic/open-ball/n0 and the same literal
  normalized transporting Holder source witness are also clients.
\end{proof}


\begin{lemma}[Actual quadratic contact image-measure upper bound]
  \label{lem:convex-quadratic-contact-measure}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.measurableSet_quadratic_contacts,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.volume_image_residual_le_of_quadratic_contacts,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.full_quadratic_contact_image_bound}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:convex-contact-null-image, lem:convex-contact-determinant,
        lem:convex-gradient-derivative}
  Let $u$ be globally convex and $C^1$, with actual continuous positive local
  Alexandrov density $f\le(1+\epsilon)^n$ on an open $\Omega$, $\epsilon\ge0$,
  $n>0$, $0<\delta<1$. For any measurable actual supporting contact set $\Gamma$
  of $u-(1-\delta)\|x\|^2/2$ relative to $\Omega$,
  \[
    |(\nabla u-(1-\delta)\mathrm{Id})(\Gamma)|
       \le (\epsilon+\delta)^n |\Gamma|.
  \]
  No finite-volume or measurable-image premise is required. The full actual
  contact set is Borel by $C^1$ continuity (this generic measurability includes
  dimension zero), and the actual source client retains the same normalized
  transporting Holder moment potential. This is a quadratic-reference image
  estimate, not the general contact volume-density gain, reference existence
  or a $W^{2,p}$ conclusion.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The genuine a.e. gradient derivative and its value expansion share one MA
  matrix. Actual contacts force residual PSD and the exact determinant bound.
  A measurable null cover of the failure predicate separates measurable good
  contacts from null bad contacts. Jacobian bounds the good image without any
  injectivity or global $C^1$ derivative premise; the true quadratic-contact
  null-image theorem kills the bad image. Subadditivity and ENNReal monotonicity
  finish even for infinite volume. The full contact set is an open-domain
  intersection with arbitrary intersections of closed supporting inequalities.
\end{proof}


\begin{lemma}[Unit-ball quadratic contact volume gain]
  \label{lem:convex-unit-ball-contact-gain}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_contact_volume_gain_unitBall,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.strictConvexOn_quadraticAt,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.gradient_quadraticAt}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:convex-reference-contact-gain}
  Fix $n>0$, $0<r<1$, $0<\delta,\eta<1$. There exists $0<\epsilon<1$
  before the unknown globally convex $C^1$ potential $u$ and density $f$.
  If $u=0$ on the unit-ball boundary and its genuine positive continuous
  local Alexandrov density satisfies $(1-\epsilon)^n\le f\le(1+\epsilon)^n$,
  then the full actual supporting contact set $\Gamma$ of
  $u-(1-\delta)\|x\|^2/2$ relative to the unit ball satisfies
  $|\Gamma|\ge(1-\eta)|\overline B_r|$.
  This is the unit-ball quadratic model, not a general normalized-domain gain,
  reference-existence theorem or $W^{2,p}$ conclusion.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Construct the unit-density reference $v=(\|x\|^2-1)/2$ using the shared
  positive-definite quadratic strictness and symmetric gradient formulas, with
  genuine C2, positive Hessian, zero frontier and actual unit MA. Specialize the
  given-reference compact-interior gain to $G=B_r$, whose actual compact closure
  lies inside the unit ball. The gradient formula and cancellation of the same
  constant on both sides identify the original quadratic contact set.
  Actual Haar equality $|B_r|=|\overline B_r|$, with the Nontrivial instance
  constructed from $n>0$, restores the exact closed-ball conclusion. The shared
  general proof cancels only a positive finite scalar coefficient, never a measure.
\end{proof}


\begin{lemma}[Finite Alexandrov upper bounds give gradient null images]
  \label{lem:convex-gradient-null-image}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.volume_gradient_image_eq_zero_of_alexandrov_upper}
  \leanok

\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Convex gradient tangents place the actual gradient image in subgradient images
  of arbitrarily small open supersets. Outer regularity and the finite MA bound
  imply nullity. The original quadratic-contact proof is shared once with the
  new comparison-reference consumer.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Local C2 compact positive-Hessian reference gradient bounds]
  \label{lem:compact-posDef-gradient-bounds}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.contDiffOn_gradient,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.bilinearIteratedFDerivTwo_eq_inner_hessianOperator_at,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.exists_uniform_gradient_bounds_of_posDef_hessianOn,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_residual_gradient_bound_of_posDef_referenceOn,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.BakryEmery.exists_uniform_gradient_bounds_of_posDef_hessian,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_residual_gradient_bound_of_posDef_reference}
  \leanok
  Let $v$ be $C^2$ only on open $U$, with actual positive Hessian on compact
  convex $K\subset U$. Uniform positive strong-monotonicity and finite gradient
  Lipschitz constants follow. Actual supports of $u-tv$, $t>0$, give
  $\|dp\|\le C\|d\nabla u\|$ with $C$ before arbitrary unknown $u,t,\Gamma$.
  No global reference extension, reference floor/cap, unknown-$u$ regularity
  or desired bound is supplied. Generic dimension zero is included.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Pointwise $C^2$ differentiates the actual gradient and identifies its derivative
  with the bilinear second derivative. This bridge is shared once with the old
  global bridge. Compact positivity on the unit sphere and a compact derivative
  norm bound give the Hessian constants; normalization and mean value on actual
  segments give the gradient bounds. Actual supports imply
  $0\le\langle dp,dx\rangle$; write $d\nabla u=dp+t\,d\nabla v$ and handle
  zero distance separately. The local proofs are common producers: old global
  interfaces are exact-type $U=\mathbb R^n$ specializations. The actual interval
  reference equals a quadratic inside $(-2,2)$ and zero outside; no global $C^2$
  hypothesis is used.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Null contact images for a given local C2 positive-Hessian reference]
  \label{lem:convex-reference-contact-null-image}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.volume_image_residual_eq_zero_of_posDef_referenceOn,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.volume_image_residual_eq_zero_of_posDef_reference}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:convex-gradient-null-image, lem:compact-posDef-gradient-bounds}
  Let $u$ be globally convex $C^1$ with finite actual open-set MA upper bound,
  and let $v$ be $C^2$ only on open $\Omega$ with positive Hessian there.
  For $t>0$, every arbitrary null subset of actual supports of $u-tv$ has null
  image under $\nabla u-t\nabla v$. No reference global smooth extension or
  outside-domain convexity, N property, factor, cover, injectivity, measurable
  contact/image set or unknown-$u$ Hessian/W2p bound is supplied. The open domain
  need not be convex; dimension zero is included.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  On actual contained compact balls, the local relative bound makes the residual
  gradient constant on gradient-$u$ fibres and constructs a true Lipschitz factor.
  The actual MA gradient null-image theorem kills the local image. Choose actual
  positive-radius contained closed balls indexed by points of $\Omega$; Lindelof
  gives a countable open-ball subcover. Countable subadditivity finishes. The old
  global theorem is an exact-type specialization of this single local proof.
  Actual clients use a reference equal to a quadratic inside $B_2$ and zero
  outside, and retain the same normalized transporting Holder literal-source
  potential with finite MA upper derived from continuous density. General
  contact-image upper now allows a GIVEN globally convex reference $C^2$ only

\begin{lemma}[Classical Pogorelov height and compact-interior estimates]

  Savin, Proposition4.1 proof(10)-(15), pp13-14,

  local nonzero constant determinant, negative value and a genuine logarithmic
  local maximum satisfies the original-coordinate directional height bound.
  Positive definiteness GENERATES axis-fixing diagonal coordinates.

  On a bounded OPEN domain (convexity is not required), C4 at every interior
  point and AMBIENT C2 at EVERY closure point, negative interior values,
  zero WHOLE frontier, positive Hessian and nonzero constant determinant
  generate a positive closure gradient cap B and the ELEMENTWISE estimate
  $\|D^2u(x)\|(-u(x))\le(n+B)\exp(B^2/2)$, together with a positive
  Hessian cap on every compact interior set. Empty domains and n=0 are included.
  This does not construct classical solutions or C2/PD of the actual C1 UNIT
  reference, and it is not full Proposition4.1, W2p or full Letwin Lemma2.2.

  constant-determinant trace identities. Actual logarithmic-test calculus
  and the canonical upper-touch Hessian theorem generate the first/second
  maximum conditions; positive weighted finite sums give the cancellation.
  The generated frame fixes the distinguished direction, preserving its
  derivative and value data under the SAME actual affine Hessian congruence.
  Thus the diagonal point bound yields the original-coordinate point bound.

  Actual compactness bounds the closure gradient and chooses a maximum of
  the positive height--Hessian product. Zero WHOLE frontier forces this
  maximum into the interior. Its actual logarithmic local maximum feeds the
  point estimate. PSD entry bounds yield the full elementwise matrix norm;
  the positive minimum of -u on a compact interior set gives its cap.
  Coordinates, maximizer, derivative identities and caps are all generated,

\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Symmetric normalization of quadratic coefficients]
  \label{lem:symmetric-quadratic-coefficient}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.posSemidef_symmetricPart_of_quadratic_nonneg,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.norm_symmetricPart_le_of_quadratic_le}
  \leanok
  Every finite real matrix with nonnegative quadratic form generates the PSD
  symmetric part $S=(A+A^T)/2$, with the SAME quadratic form and trace pairing
  against every Hermitian matrix. A quadratic ceiling $M\ge0$ bounds the
  ELEMENTWISE norm of S, including empty indices. No raw skew-part bound or
  raw-A PSD is asserted. This supports CNS1984 (5.1).
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Double-sum reindexing gives both identities. The quadratic form supplies
  positivity; canonical PSD Young at coordinate vectors bounds each entry.
  Actual arbitrarily large skew matrices exercise the distinction in clients.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Strict drift maximum, weak maximum and quadratic barrier]
  \label{lem:elliptic-maximum}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.strict_drift_maximum_principle,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.linearized_maximum_principle,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.abs_le_linearized_c0_barrier,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.strict_drift_maximum_principle_of_quadratic_nonneg}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:pointwise-hessian-linearity, lem:symmetric-quadratic-coefficient}
  CNS1984 section2 pp374-376 and the Lemma5.1 comparison pp385-387 use these
  ordinary tools on bounded OPEN domains, without convexity, in all n=0 and
  empty cases. C2 inside and C0 on the closure, a whole-frontier threshold,
  PSD coefficients and arbitrary drift give a maximum bound when the actual
  operator is strictly positive only above the threshold. Coefficients need
  neither continuity nor boundedness. Quadratic nonnegativity alone suffices
  in the nonsymmetric caller. The weak zero-drift theorem needs positive
  trace; a trace floor yields
  $|u(x)|\le B+F( R^2-|x-c|^2)/(2\tau)$.
  These do not prove full Lemma5.1, an inverse, classical existence or U-C2.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  ONE compact-maximum proof generates an interior local maximum; its actual
  first derivative vanishes and the canonical upper-touch PSD/trace theorem
  controls the Hessian. The weak theorem calls that strict core with the
  actual quadratic perturbation. The barrier applies the weak theorem to
  both signs. The nonsymmetric caller generates actual local-C2 Hessian
  symmetry and the SAME symmetric-part trace identity before calling the
  strict theorem once. Real nonzero-drift, unbounded skew, Fin0 one-point and
  empty-domain clients construct their data and apply these sources.
\end{proof}


\begin{lemma}[Convex support from a derivative within the domain]
  \label{lem:convex-within-support}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.convex_support_of_hasFDerivWithinAt}
  \leanok
  A convex function on a convex set in any real normed space lies above
  the affine functional supplied by its Frechet derivative WITHIN that set.
  The set may be lower dimensional. No inner product, finite dimension,
  completeness, nontriviality, UniqueDiff or ambient derivative is needed.
  This is the ordinary support prerequisite for CNS1984 Lemma5.1 (5.3).
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Restrict to the actual affine segment and apply Mathlib's within-chain
  and one-dimensional convex slope comparison. ONE generic proof replaces
  the old global gradient tangent proof by its actual univ specialization,
  with the exact old complete-inner-product contract retained. Absolute-value
  boundary, true Fin0 singleton and universe1 clients apply the new source.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Half-ball closure and one-sided normal derivative]
  \label{lem:halfball-within-normal}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.interior_closedHalfBall,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.convex_closedHalfBall,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.closure_halfBall,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.uniqueDiffOn_closure_halfBall,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.normal_derivative_halfBall}
  \leanok
  In ambient dimension m+1, for every m=0 or higher and positive radius,
  the open half-ball has the actual convex closed half-ball as its closure.
  A function C1 WITHIN that FULL closure has the actual inward one-sided
  normal derivative and positive-ray slope limit at every flat point of
  norm less than the radius. No ambient C1 or default gradient is substituted.
  This supports CNS1984 Lemma5.1 p385, not its full modulus or solvability.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Convexity, the open last-coordinate projection and an actual interior point
  generate the closure and unique-differentiability facts. The same inward
  ray stays in that closure on the generated interval [0,R-normz]. ONE within
  chain derivative serves both conclusions. The absolute-normal-coordinate
  client generates within derivative1, ambient nondifferentiability and
  default ambient derivative0; ambientFin1 and nonzero-flat affine clients
  apply the same source. Ambient m=0 means dimension1, not a Fin0 carrier.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Generated normalized support of the convex flat trace]
  \label{lem:normalized-flat-support}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.normalized_flat_support}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:convex-within-support}
  C1 WITHIN the full closed half-ball, convex flat trace, zero value and
  tangential derivative at0, and the ordinary tangential derivative boundC
  generate the actual tangential coordinatesg, alpha=normg at mostC and flat
  nonnegativity. Either alpha is zero or a unit tangent zeta and an intercept
  beta between0 and normzbar give the actual max-support on the flat trace.
  All m=0 or higher are included. No support plane/intercept/desired estimate
  or PDE data are inputs. This is CNS1984 (5.3)/(5.3)prime support only.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Call the generic within-support source at0 and zbar. Actual basis
  coordinates identify the tangential functional, its norm bound normalizes
  g, and support/nonnegativity generate beta. A genuine quartic in Fin2 at
  (1/4,0) produces alpha1/16 and beta3/16 before applying the theorem; the
  ambientFin1 client uses the genuine zero-tangent branch. No unknown
  regularity, barrier comparison, boundary modulus or existence follows.
\end{proof}


\begin{lemma}[Radial/logarithmic profile calculus and values]
  \label{lem:log-radial-calculus}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.hasFDerivAt_log_radial_profile_of_pos,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.contDiffAt_fderiv_fderiv_log_radial_profile,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.fderiv_log_radial_profile_norm_le,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.continuous_log_radial_barrier,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.tendsto_log_radial_positive_normal_slope,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.log_radial_profile_flat_value,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.flat_log_radial_profile_normal,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.log_radial_profile_value_bounds,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.log_radial_profile_value_le_radius_add}
  \leanok
  CNS1984 Lemma5.1 p386 supports: actual positive-radius Frechet calculus
  in any real normed space, derivative norm control, singular global C0,
  divergent positive-ray slope and exact flat value/normal derivative.
  The profile is nonnegative for positive height and radius below1, with
  the actual logarithmic upper bound and radius-plus-epsilon bound.
  No independence, finite dimension, completeness or unknown regularity
  is assumed by the generic results. These are not boundary comparison.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Shared positive-quadratic and first-derivative production replaces both
  original inline calculus bodies. Mathlib calculus, continuous-mul-log
  and its logarithmic limit handle the real singular set. One scalar value
  proof replaces the old zeroth-order inline block and supplies the new
  consequence via the ordinary r-log-r inequality. Actual off-axis, scalar,
  singular and generic Fin0 clients distinguish the dimensional scopes.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Generated plane and ambient profile operator margins]
  \label{lem:log-radial-operator}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.plane_log_radial_barrier_trace_lower,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.tangent_normal_log_radial_barrier_trace_lower,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_log_radial_drift_margin_radius,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_log_radial_zeroth_margin_radius}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:log-radial-calculus}
  Raw quadratic ellipticity, without symmetry, gives the SAME profile's
  actual plane and ambient Hessian contraction. Numerical data generate
  radii before every coefficient, tangent, drift, zeroth term and point,
  giving margins ell/(8r) with drift and ell/(16r) with bounded nonpositive
  zeroth term. Constants24/4/8/16 are conservative derived supports.
  Ambient m+2 starts at dimension2. No comparison or existence is concluded.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Canonical symmetric-part elementwise control replaces the old two-point
  polarization proof. Actual frame isometry, compression and Hessian
  coordinates generate the same-operator coupling before the plane call.
  One numeric radius controls the logarithmic drift, and the shared value
  bounds give the zeroth-order margin. Real arbitrary-skew and nonzero-drift
  clients choose radii once before constructing actual candidate points.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Polynomial half-ball barrier and curved boundary]
  \label{lem:halfball-polynomial}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.halfball_polynomial_differential_data,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.exists_halfball_polynomial_negative_operator,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.halfball_polynomial_norm_floor,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.halfball_polynomial_curved_lower}
  \leanok
  The actual polynomial xn+norm(x) squared-(E+1)xn squared is smooth.
  Numerical ellipticity/drift data generate E/delta before all raw
  quadratic coefficients, drifts and nonpositive zeroth terms. In the
  half-ball it is nonnegative with operator at most-1; the ordinary height
  budget also yields the curved-boundary radius-squared lower bound.
  Ambient m+1 includes the line; this constructs no classical solution.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  One canonical quadratic supplies all differential data. A single norm-floor
  proof replaces the old inline nonnegativity and serves curved-boundary
  control through the actual coordinate norm bound. Actual positive-normal
  scalar/skew clients and curved Fin1/Fin2 clients apply the sources.
\end{proof}


\begin{lemma}[Actual orthogonal frame and finite coordinates]
  \label{lem:orthogonal-frame-coordinates}

        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.orthogonal_pair_inner_sq_le,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.clm_apply_eq_sum_single,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.norm_le_card_mul_of_coord_bound}
  \leanok
  An orthogonal unit pair in any real inner-product space gives the actual
  norm identity and Bessel bound, without finite dimension/completeness.
  Finite coordinates evaluate a continuous linear map into any real normed
  codomain; component bounds generate the Euclidean drift norm. Empty indices
  are included. These support CNS1984 Lemma5.1 p386, not existence.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  One actual pair preparation calls Mathlib's inner sum and Bessel.
  Its norm source replaces the old ambient-frame body. One basis evaluation
  replaces both the old Holder and tangential expansions, with exact old
  scopes retained. Real arbitrary-codomain/nonzero and empty clients apply it.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Generated radius and actual half-ball frontier]
  \label{lem:halfball-radius-frontier}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.tangent_normal_radius_le,
        FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.frontier_halfBall}
  \leanok
  \uses{lem:orthogonal-frame-coordinates, lem:halfball-within-normal}
  The actual unit tangent and last normal generate the shifted planar radius
  bound by ambient norm plus absolute shift, in ambient dimension m+2.
  The SAME half-ball closure/interior give its exact flat-or-curved frontier
  for R positive and ambient dimension m+1. No radius/coupling is assumed.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Actual orthogonality feeds Bessel, followed by the actual two-coordinate
  triangle inequality. Canonical closure and interior give the frontier.
  A genuine shifted Fin2 point and the two nonempty ambientFin1 boundary
  points apply the sources. No unknown-function regularity is concluded.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Pointwise elliptic Hessian-gradient-value linearity]
  \label{lem:elliptic-operator-linearity}
  \lean{FunctionalInequalities.Analysis.elliptic_operator_linear_combination}
  \leanok
  Local C2 of the actual functions gives the same scalar combination for
  trace(A Hess u)+Du(b)+a*u. Coefficients are arbitrary; dimension0 is included.
  No equation, symmetry, ellipticity, coefficient continuity or supplied
  derivative identity occurs. This is CNS1984 comparison preparation only.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Call canonical pointwise Hessian and Mathlib Frechet linearity. The old
  C0-barrier difference consumer uses the real zero-drift/zeroth specialization.
  A nonzero-drift quadratic/affine client generates actual value24; the empty
  index point client applies the same source. Full comparison remains open.
\end{proof}
